# Architecture: F1 Timings

Status: approved by the owner (2026-10-25). Cited as A§x. Decisions are recorded in `docs/adr/`; this file is the overview. Facts marked **[U]** are unverified and are checked by the first phase that depends on them.

## 1. Product

- **What:** a personal website showing lap times, sector times, mini-sectors, speed traps and tyre stints for F1 sessions (FP1–3, sprint qualifying, sprint, qualifying, race). Phase 2 adds **live timing and replay**: you watch the video in the F1 TV app and follow the timing on this site, with an adjustable delay.
- **Who:** the owner only. The whole site is behind Cloudflare Access for one email address. Nothing is public.
- **Seasons:** 2023 to now in v1 (OpenF1). 2018–2022 in phase 3 (official F1 archive with the owner's F1 TV Pro). No source has sector times before 2018.
- **Non-goals:** video (F1 TV streams are DRM-protected and the ToS forbids re-serving them), telemetry traces, a car-position track map, championship standings, other users or accounts, public access, a native app, Dutch UI.

## 2. Principles

1. **Small and boring.** One user, one VM. Reuse Waterheight's stack and patterns, and drop the ops machinery that exists for a public site under load (ADR-0002).
2. **Isolated from Waterheight.** A separate compose project, database, tunnel, networks, firewall table, secrets and GIDs. Nothing in the F1 stack can break Waterheight (ADR-0003).
3. **Store raw, derive later.** Every fetched OpenF1 response and every live message is kept raw, so a parser fix can re-derive without fetching again (`rederive`, A§8, #103).
4. **Honest gaps.** Missing values render as `—` with a reason in the tooltip. Nothing is invented or interpolated.
5. **One parser, one playback path.** The live feed and the F1 archive share the same SignalR topic format and one TS parser (ADR-0006). Live and replay are both "state at session time T" (ADR-0007).
6. **Never call F1 or OpenF1 from CI.** Tests use recorded fixtures. GitHub's runners are blocked by F1 anyway (FastF1 #988).

## 3. Tech stack

Same versions as Waterheight at P0 time. Re-check each pin against the 7-day release-age rule when installing.

| Area | Choice | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Language/runtime | TypeScript 6.0.3 strict, Node 26.10.0 | TS 7 forbidden. Erasable syntax only (no enums or namespaces). `.ts` imports with extension. |
| Package manager | pnpm 12.5.1 workspace | `minimumReleaseAge: 10080`, `strictDepBuilds`, `blockExoticSubdeps`, `--frozen-lockfile` |
| Lint/format | Biome 2.5.15 | `"preset": "recommended"` (not `"none"`; see the Waterheight gotcha) |
| Tests | Vitest 5.0.4 (`unit` + `integration` projects), msw 3.0.3, fast-check 4.10.2 | Integration tests run on real PostgreSQL 18, not testcontainers |
| Server | Hono 4.13.16 + @hono/node-server 2.1.6, undici 8.12.3, pino 10.4.0, croner 11.0.0, zod 4.6.7 | One image with roles (A§4) |
| Database | PostgreSQL 18.6 (own container), Kysely 0.29.8 + pg 8.23.1, kysely-codegen 0.20.0, dbmate 2.36.0 | Immutable migrations |
| Web | React 19.3.0, Vite 8.3.4 + @vitejs/plugin-react 6.1.2, TanStack Query 5.104.2, ECharts 6.1.0 (lazy chunk) | English only, no i18n library. Times through `Intl.DateTimeFormat` in Europe/Amsterdam, no Temporal polyfill (owner, 2026-10-28) |
| Live feed (P2) | SignalR Core client: a hand-rolled ~200-line client on Node's global WebSocket (undici), no dependency | Owner, 2026-10-30 (A§11.1) |
| Edge | Caddy 2.11.7, cloudflared 2026.10.3, Cloudflare Access (free plan) | Own tunnel |
| Images | Build: `node:26.11.1-trixie-slim`. Runtime: `gcr.io/distroless/nodejs26-debian13:nonroot`. Web: `caddy:2.11.7-alpine`. Also `postgres:18.6-trixie`, `cloudflare/cloudflared:2026.10.3`, dbmate 2.36.0 (release binary, sha256-checked) and, in the CI nft check only, `debian:trixie-slim`. All pinned by digest | |

### Reused from Waterheight / dropped

| Waterheight piece | Here | Why |
|---|---|---|
| TS-only, pnpm policy, Biome, Vitest unit+integration, msw, fast-check | **reuse** | Same toolchain, same agents, same gotchas |
| Hono API, Kysely + pg, dbmate, croner with own busy guard, pino, heartbeat healthcheck | **reuse** | Proven patterns |
| `packages/contracts` (zod, shared server↔web) | **reuse** | The web parses every API answer |
| `packages/core` | drop | Nothing to share beyond contracts |
| Adapter fixture standard (`.raw` / `.meta.json` / `.golden.json`) | **reuse, lighter** | ≥ 1 normal + 1 edge + 1 empty per endpoint. No coverage gate per file, one property test per parser |
| Hardened compose anchor, file secrets, fixed bridge names + own nftables table, tick timer | **reuse** | Same host, same safety |
| Strict CSP, no third-party browser requests, lazy ECharts | **reuse** | Cheap, and it removes a whole class of bugs |
| Signed pull-based releases (cosign, promote gate, manifest) | drop | One user, private site. GHCR digests + a pull timer are enough (ADR-0002). Revisit if anyone else commits |
| Static-first publisher (ADR-0005), brownout, k6 load tests, chaos, benches | drop | No traffic spikes; one reader |
| Owner-audience machinery (ADR-0017), WireGuard owner site | drop | The whole site is owner-only behind Access |
| Paraglide i18n | drop | English only |
| MapLibre/PMTiles/basemap | drop | No map |
| restic backups, restore drill (ADR-0011) | drop | All DB data can be re-fetched. Accepted risk: raw live recordings |
| Watchdog role, public status page | drop | A status panel in the UI plus 2 healthchecks.io checks |
| BOM table + `check-bom.ts`, `check-boundaries`, OpenAPI generators, CodeQL, 3-browser Playwright matrix, Lighthouse | drop | Exact pins + Dependabot cooldown cover the supply-chain part; the rest guards a public product |
| dbmate built from source in the image | lighter | Official static release binary, sha256-pinned in the Dockerfile |
| `harden-runner` in every CI job | drop | In audit mode it blocks nothing, and it is one less third-party action with access to every job (owner, 2026-10-25) |
| Separate `dbmate` CI job with a round-trip script | lighter | The `integration` test creates a database and runs the real `migrate` role up → down → up (owner, 2026-10-25) |

## 4. Components

One server image (`ghcr.io/mwijkhuisen/f1-server`) whose first argument picks the role, and one web image (`f1-web`: Caddy plus the built SPA).

| Role | Kind | Does |
|---|---|---|
| `api` | daemon | Hono on `:8080`: REST `/api/v1/*` (P1c) and SSE playback streams (P2d). Verifies the Cloudflare Access JWT on `/api`; reads as `f1_api`; reads `/srv/f1/live` read-only (P2d) |
| `ingest` | daemon | croner scheduler: OpenF1 schedule sync, per-session fetch pipeline, first backfill (A§8) and, since P5c (owner, 2026-11-07), the rain job that fetches Open-Meteo's model rain grid per session (A§8) |
| `live` (P2c) | daemon | Logs in to F1 TV, connects to the SignalR feed around each scheduled session, appends raw messages to disk and writes snapshots (A§11) |
| `migrate` | job | dbmate up as the `f1_migrator` role |
| `healthcheck` | probe | Exits 0 if `/tmp/f1-heartbeat` is under 120 s old |
| `refetch <session_key> \| --failed \| --meeting <meeting_key>` / `backfill` | job | Manual tools for OpenF1 corrections. `refetch` fetches the given session, every `failed` OpenF1 session or a meeting's ended sessions from OpenF1 now, on one rate limiter (#103, A§8); `backfill` refuses while the `ingest` daemon holds the ingest lock (A§8) |
| `rederive <session_key>… \| --all` (#103) | job | Re-runs the normaliser and the timing rules (A§9) over the stored `raw_response` rows of OpenF1 sessions and replaces their child rows, so a rule or normaliser fix reaches stored sessions (A§8). No network call: logs in as `f1_ingest` |
| `rain-refetch <session_key>` (#103) | job | Sets a `failed` rain grid back to `live` and due now, for the daemon's rain step to fetch (A§8). No network call: logs in as `f1_ingest` |
| `accept-empty <session_key> <endpoint>` (#103) | job | Deletes one endpoint's stored `raw_response` row after OpenF1 confirms that the endpoint is empty now, which clears the empty guard (A§8). One OpenF1 request: logs in as `f1_ingest` |
| `archive <year> [<session_key>…]` (P3a) | job (`jobs` profile) | Downloads one year of 2018–2022 from the F1 archive and imports its sessions through the P2 parser (A§6.2, A§7). Logs in as `f1_ingest`, reads the F1 TV token that `live` caches (read-only) and writes `/srv/f1/archive`. No timer: the owner starts it (runbook `update.md`) |
| `fill <session_key>… \| --all [--archive-dir <p>] [--live-dir <p>]` (P3b) | job (`jobs` profile) | Builds `f1-archive` and `f1-live` lap candidates for `source = 'openf1'` sessions from the local files under `/srv/f1/archive` and `/srv/f1/live` and merges every session it is given again (A§7). A session without files is a pure re-merge. No network call: logs in as `f1_ingest`, reads both directories read-only. No timer: the owner starts it |
| `jolpica [<year>…] [--dir <path>]` (P3c) | job (`jobs` profile) | Imports the Jolpica-F1 CSV dump's races and qualifyings of 1950–2017 as `jolpica` rows, and from 2018 on adds lap candidates to the existing sessions (A§6.3, A§7). No network call: logs in as `f1_ingest`, reads `/srv/f1/jolpica` read-only. No timer: the owner starts it (runbook `update.md`) |

```
Cloudflare Access ─► cloudflared ─► caddy (SPA + reverse proxy /api) ─► api ──┐
                                                                            ├─► postgres
OpenF1 (HTTPS) ◄─ ingest ───────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
Open-Meteo (HTTPS) ◄─ ingest (rain job, P5c) ───────────────────────────────┤
F1 live feed / archive (HTTPS, WSS, F1 TV token) ◄─ live / archive ─────────┘
                                         └─► /srv/f1/live/<recording>/ (raw + snapshots; the api reads the raw lines only since P2e)
```

## 5. Repository layout

```
apps/server/src/{main.ts, api/, ingest/, openf1/, rain/, live/, timing/, db/}
apps/server/test/
apps/web/{index.html, src/, vite.config.ts}
apps/web/src/map/              # circuit outlines <id>.json (derived from bacinger, MIT), osm/ the ODbL layers (P5c)
apps/web/public/               # third-party-licences.txt, served as /third-party-licences.txt (ADR-0008)
data/third-party/f1-circuits/  # bacinger/f1-circuits, vendored verbatim (MIT, A§6.3)
packages/contracts/src/        # zod schemas for every API answer
db/migrations/                 # dbmate, YYYYMMDDHHMMSS_name.sql, never edited once applied
deploy/{compose.yaml, server/Dockerfile, web/Dockerfile, web/Caddyfile, host/, bin/, systemd/}
docs/{plan/, adr/, runbooks/}
scripts/                       # record-fixture.ts, record-rain-fixture.ts, map-extract.ts (all refuse under CI), rain-facts.sql, jolpica-counts.py and jolpica-facts.sql (the P3c [box]), smoke tools
```

## 6. Data sources and data map

Checked against the live APIs on 2026-10-25 (Austin quali `11392`, Marina Bay race `11388`, Marina Bay sprint `11383`, Bahrain 2023 race `7953`).

### 6.1 OpenF1 (`https://api.openf1.org/v1`), v1 source

- Free, no auth, 2023+. Paid only within "live", which runs from 30 min before a session starts until 30 min after it ends. Free limit: 3 req/s and 30 req/min. `meetings`/`sessions` refresh daily at 00:00 UTC.
- Sessions per year: 2023: 118, 2024: 123, 2025: 123, 2026: 131 (including testing days and future sessions). Session names: `Practice 1–3`, `Sprint Qualifying`, `Sprint`, `Qualifying`, `Race`, `Day 1–3`.
- `meetings.gmt_offset` is a string `[-]HH:MM:SS` with no `+` for a positive offset ("-06:00:00", "02:00:00"; the committed fixtures); ingest stores it unchanged and the web parses it (P4c, A§13 Times).
- **Cancelled sessions stay listed** with `is_cancelled: true` (`sessions-year` fixture: 10 of the 131 sessions of 2026, those of the cancelled Bahrain `1282` and Saudi Arabia `1283` meetings, 10–19 April). Their data endpoints answer "No results found.". P1b stores the flag on `meeting` and `session` and never fetches a cancelled session, so it stays `scheduled` without `next_fetch_at` and P1c can tell "cancelled" from "no data".
- Known issues (open on `br-g/openf1`): null pit `stop_duration` (#484), sessions needing re-ingest (#483), wrong `tyre_age_at_start` for used sets (#473), slowness right after a session becomes free (#474).
- **`pit` rows removed after the event** (owner, 2026-11-09; found by the P5a re-queue, A§8). OpenF1 now answers `pit` with 404 "No results found." for 72 of the 80 past non-race sessions of 2026: practice, qualifying, sprint qualifying and testing, from the first test day to the Austin weekend (23–24 Oct). Their stored responses held 48–276 rows each.
  - **Still there on 9 Nov:** the two latest weekends (Mexico City 11397–11400, São Paulo 11405–11408), every 2026 race and sprint, and the 2024–2025 non-race sessions sampled (9465, 9468, 9627, 9686, 9689, 9989).
  - **2023:** the samples (7765, 7768, 9222) answer 404 too, but no 2023 session tripped the empty guard.
  - **Impact:** no web view reads the pit rows (`GET /sessions/:key/pits` serves them, and nothing calls it), so the owner accepted the removal (A§8).
- **During a live session every free request fails, past sessions included** (P1a, 2026-10-25 20:03 UTC, Austin race): HTTP 401 `{"detail":"Live F1 session in progress. Global API access (including past sessions) is restricted to authenticated users until the session ends. …"}`, with a link to buy a paid API key (the plan uses none). It lifted between 22:26:45 and 22:31:46 UTC, at the end of the live window (scheduled end 22:00 + 30 min), not at the chequered flag. The client reports it as `http 401` without retrying. P1b: nothing calls OpenF1 while a session in `session` is inside its window (`date_start − 30 min` … `date_end + 30 min`; a cancelled session or a row longer than 12 h opens none). The tick waits it out, the daemon's sync retries 15 min later, the backfill stops (exit 1) and a `refetch` fails with exit 1 and writes nothing (A§8). The very first sync has no schedule to check yet: a 401 there fails with `http_401` and is retried the same way.
- **A query without rows answers 404 `{"detail":"No results found."}`**, not `[]` (P1a). The client turns exactly that answer into an empty list; any other 404 is an error. Bahrain 2023 race `7953` has no `pit` rows at all.
- **Nulls** (P1a scan, 2026-10-25: the full responses of 11388, 11392, 11383 and 7953, plus `meetings`/`sessions` 2023–2026; the zod schemas in `apps/server/src/openf1/schemas.ts` follow it):
  - `laps` (2,898 rows): `date_start` 2, `lap_duration` 42, S1 102, S2 11, S3 20, `i1_speed` 618, `i2_speed` 11, `st_speed` 502, each `segments_sector_n` array 6–8, and single `null` elements inside the arrays.
  - `pit` (169): `pit_duration` and `lane_duration` 22, `stop_duration` all 169.
  - `race_control` (428): `driver_number` 336, `flag` and `scope` 191, `sector` 298, `lap_number` 39, `qualifying_phase` 408.
  - `session_result` (86): `position` 14, `duration` 30, `gap_to_leader` 16 (a number, a string such as `+1 LAP`, or Q1–Q3 in qualifying). `points` is missing from qualifying results.
  - Never null in the scan: `meetings`, `sessions`, `stints`, and the `drivers` fields A§7 stores (`country_code`, not stored, is null in 66 of 86).
  - **Deleted and reinstated laps in `race_control`** (P1c, 2026-10-27: the 454 final sessions 2023–2026 of the dev backfill). These numbers and the Q-phase check below come from a one-off, read-only run of the real `markLaps` over the dev backfill database. Since #105 `node scripts/rules-facts.ts` reproduces the totals per form and outcome and the Q-phase tallies: the same run on the owner's machine (`DATABASE_URL` of a dev backfill, the `openf1` sessions only, a READ ONLY transaction that is rolled back, counts and session keys only), per form and outcome for the deleted and reinstated messages, and per qualifying phase for the fastest valid lap against `result.durations_ms`. Its outcome columns merge two splits below (`LAP n` among several with `LAP n` when no lap has the time, 19 and 8) and the four no-lap reasons (80, 29, 5, 3) sit in one column (`test/rules-facts.test.ts` pins its counting on synthetic rows). The committed fixtures and `apps/server/test/timing.test.ts` pin the rules: 3,677 DELETED messages, 3,672 in a known form (A§9), and 3,555 of those name a lap of the session: 2,983 by a unique time within 1 ms, 42 by the one exact time among several laps within 1 ms (never a different lap than `LAP n` names), 19 by `LAP n` among several, 8 by `LAP n` when no lap has the time, 503 in the LAP form. 117 name no lap: 80 carry neither a time nor `LAP n` (`… (NEXT LAP PIT)`), 29 name a car without laps in the session (support-series messages in 9223), 5 a `LAP n` OpenF1 does not have, 3 a time no lap has. 5 are in no known form (`CAR 243 TIME 0:00.000 DELETED - DOUBLE YELLOW`, `CAR LEC TIME 1:36.518 DELETED - … (Q1)`). All 26 REINSTATED messages parse and each restores the lap a DELETED message had deleted.
  - **The scan was too small** (P1b [box] backfill, 2026-10-26): about 5% of real sessions failed the P1a schemas with `schema`. Seen: a string `session_result.position` (11384), a null `stints.compound` (11246, 11342), a null `session_result.number_of_laps` (11275). Since then every field outside a row key, `meeting.year` and the session dates may be null or missing (it parses to null), a position string is kept only when it is a number ("DQ" → null; `dnf`/`dns`/`dsq` say why), and each field is bounded by its column (integers that fit smallint/int, seconds that fit int ms, text without NUL), so a value the schema accepts never fails an INSERT. (2026-11-09: the timestamp check also requires year ≥ 0001 and an offset within ±15:59, the `timestamptz` bounds that zod's format check alone let through.)
  - **Where lap values are missing** (P1d probe, 2026-10-28: the 128,497 laps of every session since 2025-01-01 in the dev backfill, a one-off read-only query, grouped by race (`session.type = 'Race'`, sprints included), lap 1, out-lap and in-lap). The rates are nulls per lap of that kind; the lap table's reasons (A§13, `missingReason` in `apps/web/src/views/LapTable.tsx`) keep only these four cases:
    - **Out-lap, S1:** null on 7,965 of 11,839 out-laps after lap 1 outside races (67 %), 65 of 1,531 in races (4 %), against 145 of 49,261 (0.3 %) and 285 of 47,424 (0.6 %) on laps that are neither out nor in. **Lap 1 out of the garage** (outside races, 3,111 laps, `is_pit_out` set) also lacks its lap time on 2,691 (86 %) and S1 on 2,704; a pit-lane start in a race lacks both on 19 of 19. Outside races, out-laps after lap 1 do not lack S2, S3 or the lap time more than other laps (lap time 75 of 11,839).
    - **In-lap:** outside races, 11,574 in-laps lack S3 on 1,663 (14 %, against 2.3 %), S2 on 571 (4.9 %, against 0.5 %), the lap time on 560 (4.8 %, against 2.1 %) and S1 on 256 (2.2 %, against 0.3 %). In races, S3 on 20 of 1,500 (1.3 %, against 0.4 %).
    - **Race lap 1 from the grid** (not an out-lap): 1,075 laps lack the lap time on 31, S1 on 41, S2 on 32, S3 on 29 (3–4 %), about ten times the other race laps (0.3–0.6 %).
    - Everything else gets "No time in the OpenF1 data".
  - **Track status forms** (P4b [box], 2026-11-07: `scripts/race-control-forms.sql`, run by the owner on the VM over the 113 races and sprints of 2023–2026, 11,274 `race_control` rows, read-only as `f1_api`; the output is counts and the digit-masked forms of the period-class messages, stewards' notes left out). The forms of the safety car, VSC, red flag, restart and chequered flag, with their rows:
    - **Safety car:** `SafetyCar` `SAFETY CAR DEPLOYED` 73 rows in 52 races and `SAFETY CAR IN THIS LAP` 62 in 47, 2023–2026. **VSC:** `VIRTUAL SAFETY CAR DEPLOYED` 34 and `… ENDING` 27 in 2023–2025, `VSC DEPLOYED` 25 and `VSC ENDING` 24 in 2026 (the 2026 wording is shorter).
    - **Red flag:** `Flag` `RED` with the message `RED FLAG` 13 times in 2023–2025. 2026 sends a red flag as category `Other`, `RED FLAG - RACE SUSPENDED`, 4 times, with no `Flag` `RED` row. The restart is `Other` `RACE WILL RESUME AT #:#` 25 times (15 plain, 6 `- STANDING START PROCEDURE`, 4 `- ROLLING START PROCEDURE`, 2023–2026). The chequered flag is `Flag` `CHEQUERED`, 112 rows in 112 races.
    - **How a period ends** (the next period form in the same race): `SAFETY CAR DEPLOYED` is followed by `IN THIS LAP` 62 times, by a red flag 9 and by the chequered flag 2; a VSC start by its ending 51, by a safety car 5, by the chequered flag 2 and by a red flag 1; a red flag by a restart 15, by a safety car 1 and by nothing 1; one red flag follows the chequered flag. So a period often has no end row of its own.
    - **Rows without a lap number:** 10 of the 11,274 (0, 1, 1 and 8 in 2023 to 2026), none of them in a period form.
    - **`lap_number` is the leader's lap** (the highest `lap_number` any driver had started by the row's `date`, over the 256 rows of the period forms): equal on 249. Every safety-car row matches; 102 of 108 VSC rows match and 6 are 1–12 laps ahead of the laps, which looks like lap data trailing race control in one race; 12 of 13 red-flag rows match and one is a lap behind; 6 rows come before any lap started.
    - **Years and unmatched forms** (owner, 2026-11-13, #105): `scripts/race-control-forms.sql` takes the years as psql variables (`-v from=… -v to=…`, defaults 2023 and 2026; `-v from=2018 -v to=2022` for the archive years). For 2018–2022, queries 2 and 6 print F1-archive text, which stays on the VM and is never pasted into the repository, a PR or an issue (A§12.1 invariant 3): only counts and digit-masked form descriptions are quoted, with driver codes and free text redacted. Its query 6 lists the period-like rows that no form accepts, digits masked, with counts, so a rewording shows there. A row is period-like when it matches `/SAFETY CAR|VSC|RED FLAG|RESUME|SUSPENDED|(^|[^A-Z])SC([^A-Z]|$)/` (`SC` as a word) and ignored when it matches the stewards' notes (`INCIDENT INVOLVING`, as in "INCIDENT INVOLVING CAR(S) …" also after "TURN # ", "PIT EXIT " or "CORRECTION: ", and `STEWARDS`, as in "FIA STEWARDS: …", and `PENALTY FOR CAR`, the archive's penalty line without that prefix; a period line that merely mentions an incident still counts) or a known procedural form (`LIGHTS (ON|OFF)`, `OVERTAKE (THE )?SAFETY CAR`, `THROUGH THE PIT LANE`, `FORMATION LAP`, `START/FINISH STRAIGHT`, `WILL ENTER PITS`, `START BEHIND THE SAFETY CAR`, `WILL STAY OUT`, `COVERS ON`, `START(ING)? PROCEDURE SUSPENDED`, `WILL NOT RESUME`). The first dev-backfill run (2026-11-13) found 22 such procedural forms in about 30 races, which D4 added to the ignore list; with `SUSPENDED` period-like, the aborted start `STARTING PROCEDURE SUSPENDED` (3 races) joined it, so the count there is 0. The patterns are `PERIOD_LIKE` and `IGNORED` in `packages/contracts/src/race-control.ts` (`isUnmatched`, which the ingest log and `/status` use, A§8, A§10), valid in JS and PostgreSQL alike, and `test/race-control-forms.test.ts` pins the query's copy to them.
    - **The F1 archive's forms** (owner, 2026-11-13, VM, query 6 over `-v from=2018 -v to=2022`): 10 rows in 9 races that no form accepted, in five wordings (digits as #):
      - `RACE WILL BE RESUMED AT #:#`, 1 row: a red-flag restart in an older wording, a real miss (the red band ran past the restart). It is now the resume form `^RACE WILL (BE )?RESUME`.
      - `# SECOND TIME PENALTY FOR CAR # (ABC) - <reason that mentions the safety car>`, 6 rows: stewards' penalties, which OpenF1 prefixes with `FIA STEWARDS:` and the archive does not. Ignored through `PENALTY FOR CAR`.
      - `LAPPED CARS # (ABC) - # - # TO OVERTAKE SAFETY CAR`, 1 row: the older lapped-cars message, without "THE" (now `OVERTAKE (THE )?SAFETY CAR`).
      - `START PROCEDURE SUSPENDED`, 1 row: the archive's aborted start (now `START(ING)? PROCEDURE SUSPENDED`).
      - `RACE WILL NOT RESUME`, 1 row: a red flag that ends the race. It matches no form and is ignored: the open red period closes at the last lap seen, so the band is right.
      - After this change query 6 for 2018–2022 is expected to print 0 rows; the owner rechecks it on the VM.
  - **Rules built from them** (`trackPeriods`, `apps/web/src/lib/track.ts`, with the exact regexes of the query): a state machine over the rows in order, one open period per kind. A safety-car start closes an open VSC, a red flag closes an open safety car and VSC, `IN THIS LAP` and the VSC ending close their kind, `RACE WILL RESUME` (or, in the archive, `RACE WILL BE RESUMED AT`) closes the red flag at the lap before its own (the restart lap is green), and so does a safety-car start (`RED → SC+` once in query 3, with no restart row between), the chequered flag closes everything open, and a period still open at the end closes at the last lap number seen. A repeated start keeps the first. A row in a period form without a lap number is listed under the table and never banded. A band marks the leader's lap, so a lapped car's band can be a lap off.
  - **`date_start`** (owner, 2026-11-07, P4f; a one-off read-only query on the dev database, 2026-11-08, over the 2026 races and sprints with laps: 24 sessions, 23,280 laps; the gap chart (A§13) depends on it):
    - **Where it is null:** only on lap 1 (6 of 525), never after it.
    - **Against the previous lap:** on 22,729 consecutive pairs, `date_start(n+1) − date_start(n) − lap_ms(n)` has |d| median 51 ms, p95 164 ms, max 59,835 ms. On the 798 pairs where lap n is a pit-in lap or lap n+1 a pit-out lap: median 61.5 ms, p95 202 ms.
    - **The gap at the flag** from line crossings (the gap chart's method) against `session_result.gap_to_leader`, over 273 classified drivers: |error| median 73 ms, p95 4,982 ms, max 20,051 ms. The tail sits at about 5, 10 and 20 s, which matches post-race time penalties: the official gap includes them and the on-track crossings do not.
    - **A hole:** 11234 lacks laps of the winner, so 5 of its drivers are not comparable.
- **Weather** (owner, 2026-11-07, P5a): `/weather?session_key=` is the seventh per-session endpoint (A§8), the track-side station that the feed's `WeatherData` also carries (A§11.1): air and track temperature (°C), humidity (%), pressure (mbar, the same as hPa), rainfall, wind direction (degrees) and wind speed (m/s), one row per sample, keyed by `date`. The **[U]** facts, from the fixtures recorded 2026-11-09 (`weather-normal` race 11388, `weather-wet` the 2024 São Paulo race 9636, `weather-quali` 11392, `weather-sprint` 11383; pinned by "[U] the weather fixtures" in `apps/server/test/openf1-schemas.test.ts`):
  - **Sample interval:** one a minute, 59.77–60.23 s between consecutive rows (median 60.0 s), in every one of the four sessions; no gap.
  - **Rows outside [date_start, date_end]:** yes, the rows cover the station's feed rather than the session. They start 52 min before `date_start` in both races (11388: 11:07:56 for 12:00; 9636: 14:38 for 15:30), 43 min before in the sprint and 12.5 min before in qualifying, and end 13 min before `date_end` (11388; `date_end` is the scheduled end), 3.5 min after (11392), 28 min after (9636) or 54 min after (11383, whose `date_end` is 09:30 for a 09:00 sprint). The owner's rule (2026-11-09): the tiles, the rain periods and the session head summarise every row OpenF1 lists for the session, the hour before a race included (A§13).
  - **Rainfall:** only 0 and 1 (9636: 117 × 1 and 84 × 0; 11383: 20 × 1 of 127; 11388 and 11392 all 0). The UI reads any value > 0 as rain.
  - **JSON types:** `humidity` is a JSON number, which OpenF1 writes with a decimal point: whole in all 201 rows of 9636 (`86.0`; the fixtures re-serialise it as `86`) and fractional in 141 of 160 rows of 11388 (`69.6`), so the column is `real`. `wind_direction` is an integer in every row (0–354 in the fixtures, 0–360 on the VM). Whether it names where the wind comes from or where it blows to is not documented (**[U]**), so the UI shows only its compass point. Pressure is about 927 mbar at Interlagos and 1009 at Marina Bay.
  - **A dropout comes as 0, not null** (found on the screenshots, 2026-11-09): `weather-normal` has a track temperature of 0 at 11:32:56, between 43.1 and 43.4 °C; no other air or track temperature, humidity or pressure of 0 in the four sessions (pinned in the same test). The owner's rule (2026-11-09): the stored rows keep what OpenF1 sent, and the web reads a 0 air or track temperature, humidity or pressure as no reading (`reading` in `lib/weather.ts`, A§13).
  - **Calm samples:** `wind_speed` 0 in 2 rows of 11388 (both with direction 0) and 11 of 9636 (9 with another direction, 191 to 330); direction 0 in 19 rows of 9636, 17 of them with 0.4–1.0 m/s. A calm sample counts no direction in the prevailing point (owner, 2026-11-09); a direction of 0 with wind stays N.
  - **Nulls:** none in the four sessions; every value is still nullable and bounded by its column (`real` refuses ±3.4e38 and beyond and a non-zero value that would round to 0; smallint for rainfall and the direction), the P1b lesson.
  - **Measured on the VM** (owner, 2026-11-09; `scripts/weather-facts.sql`, read-only as `f1_api`, after the re-queue had drained):
    - **Coverage:** 464 of the 465 fetched or final sessions of 2023–2026 have weather, 52,230 rows in all:
      - 2023: 113 sessions, 12,799 rows
      - 2024: 123 sessions, 12,847 rows
      - 2025: 123 sessions, 13,637 rows
      - 2026: 105 of 106 sessions, 12,947 rows
    - **The one without** is the 2026 race still `fetched`, most likely the 8 Nov race: it was last fetched before the deploy, and the re-queue took only `final` sessions. It gains weather at its `date_end + 24 h` refetch.
    - **Rainfall:** only 0 (50,004 rows, 463 sessions) and 1 (2,226 rows, 85 sessions).
    - **Readings:**
      - No null in any column.
      - Dropouts: 40 track temperatures of 0; no air temperature, humidity or pressure of 0.
      - Calm: 868 samples at 0 m/s, and 1,229 samples with direction 0 and wind.
      - `humidity` is fractional in 11,517 rows. `wind_direction` runs 0–360, wider than the fixtures' 0–354; `compass` reads 360 as N.
    - **Interval:** median 60.001 s and p95 60.028 s over 51,766 intervals. There are 11 gaps over 5 min in 9 sessions, the longest 78 min (4,680.7 s); the 5-minute rule in `rainPeriods` splits a period across them.
    - **First row against `date_start`** (median, then range):

      | Type | Median | Range |
      |---|---|---|
      | Practice | 13.8 min before | 74.9 min before to 106.0 min after |
      | Qualifying | 13.0 min before | 74.2 min before to 24.3 min after |
      | Race | 52.3 min before | 58.9 to 0.8 min before |

    - **Last row against `date_end`:**

      | Type | Median | Range |
      |---|---|---|
      | Practice | 6.4 min after | 266.4 min before to 186.0 min after |
      | Qualifying | 7.1 min after | 40.2 min before to 86.5 min after |
      | Race | 18.4 min before | 39.8 min before to 81.0 min after |

    - **Rows outside [date_start − 1 h, date_end + 1 h]:** 267 rows in 11 sessions, 60 early and 207 late. Every row is summarised (owner, 2026-11-09), so these count in the tiles and the chart too.
- **Race story facts** (#115; a one-off read-only query on the dev database, 2026-11-13, OpenF1 races 2023–2026): **PENALTY:** 299 race-control messages contain PENALTY, in 83 races, and every one names the car as `CAR n (TLA)` (forms: "n SECOND TIME PENALTY", "PENALTY SERVED", "DRIVE THROUGH", "STOP/GO", "INCIDENT … FAILING TO SERVE TIME PENALTY"), so a penalty is matched to a car by that number. **Stints after a race in-lap:** of 3,533 in-laps, 2,998 have a new stint from lap n + 1; 374, in 12 sessions (7787, 9094, 9110, 9488, 9523, 9531, 9693, 9963, 9979, 11286, 11291, 11299), have it at n itself (OpenF1 numbers those stints from the in-lap); 74 have none nearby (drive-throughs, pit-lane passes); 87 are one or two laps off (not counted as stops). **`is_pit_out` on lap 1:** set for almost the whole field in 11404 (22 cars), 11234 (19) and 9531 (20), so it marks no pit-lane start. **Cool-down rows:** 21 of 113 races have lap rows past the most laps any of their results lists, 50 rows in all, every one with a `date_start` and none with a `lap_ms` (the in-lap after the flag). **A finisher's last lap:** of 1,990 classified finishers, 0 have a last lap with no lap time and no following row (which would read as a retirement).
- **Starting grid** (#115; O1 owner, 2026-11-13): `/starting_grid?session_key=` (beta) gives `session_key`, `meeting_key`, `driver_number`, `position` and `lap_duration` (the qualifying lap); only `position` is stored, as `result.grid` of the race (A§7, A§8). Its source is formula1.com's starting-grid page, so grid penalties are in it (br-g/openf1#268), unlike the qualifying order.
  - **Linked to the qualifying session**, not the race: "currently linked to the qualifying sessions rather than the race sessions" (the maintainer, br-g/openf1#356). The race's own key answers 404 "No results found." (`starting_grid-racekey`, 11388), so ingest asks with the key of the race's `Qualifying`, or of the sprint's `Sprint Qualifying` (`Sprint Shootout` in 2023).
  - **[U] facts**, from the fixtures recorded 2026-11-13 (`normal` 11384, race 11388's qualifying; `sprint` 11379, sprint 11383's; `edge` 11400, the Mexico City race 11404's; `racekey` 11388; `empty` 11416, a qualifying before it ran, 404; pinned by "[U] the starting_grid fixtures" in `apps/server/test/openf1-schemas.test.ts`): 22 rows each, every row with its qualifying's `session_key`, positions 1…22 with no null and no repeat. 1, 2 and 1 rows have a null `lap_duration`: a car without a qualifying time still gets a position, so a back-of-grid or pit-lane start is just a position (Jolpica's 0 does not occur).
  - **Beta:** it may change or vanish as `pit` did (above), so it fails on its own (A§8).

| View | Endpoints → fields | Known gaps → UI |
|---|---|---|
| Session header | `meetings` (name, circuit, `gmt_offset`), `sessions` (type, name, start/end), `session_result` (position, laps, dnf/dns/dsq, `duration` (an array of Q1/Q2/Q3 in qualifying), `gap_to_leader`) | — |
| Lap table | `laps`: `lap_number`, `lap_duration`, `duration_sector_1..3`, `is_pit_out_lap`, `date_start` (a live-ingested value, not exact: the P4f check above gives its error). `drivers`: `name_acronym`, `team_name`, `team_colour`. In-lap = the lap before an out-lap (A§9); `pit.lap_number` is the in-lap only in races and sprints. `race_control` → deleted laps (A§9), SC, VSC and red-flag bands from `race_control` (A§13) | Null `lap_duration` or sectors on out-laps and some flying laps (11392: 84 of 280 S1 null, 25 laps without a time; the P1d rates are listed above) → `—` with a reason behind a tap: "Out-lap", "In-lap" or "Lap 1 from the grid" followed by ": no time in the OpenF1 data", else "No time in the OpenF1 data" (A§13) |
| Comparison | Same as the lap table; the gap chart also reads `date_start` (P4f) | Gaps break the line, never interpolated (P1e: a lap missing or without a time is a null point, A§13) |
| Mini-sectors | `laps.segments_sector_1..3` codes: 0 = n/a, 2048 = yellow, 2049 = green, 2051 = purple, 2064 = pit lane. 2050/2052/2068 meaning unknown → grey. P1e count over every lap since 2025 in the dev backfill (2026-10-28, one-off read-only query; non-race / race): 2048 1,294,827 / 955,758; 2049 376,548 / 185,557; 2064 69,476 / 8,243; 2051 35,359 / 10,621; a null element 33,272 / 40,039; 0 7,655 / 1,030; 2050 578 / 7; 2052 28 / 28; 2068 7 / 0; 2065 1 / 0 (seen once, so grey like every other unknown code). Array lengths vary within a session: in Austin quali 11392, S1 6, S2 8, S3 1–8 | OpenF1 says segments are "not available during races" and race data is mostly 2048 → race tab shows a banner: "Mini-sector colours unreliable in races". 0 and a null element → an empty "no data" box; a shorter or null array is padded with those to the session's longest (A§13) |
| Speed traps | `laps.i1_speed`, `i2_speed`, `st_speed` (km/h) | `i1_speed` null on ~25% and `st_speed` on ~20% of race laps → `—` (11392: none null) |
| Tyres/stints | `stints`: `stint_number`, `lap_start`, `lap_end`, `compound`, `tyre_age_at_start`. `pit`: `pit_duration`, `lane_duration`, `stop_duration` | Tyre age may be wrong for used sets (#473), `stop_duration` often null (#484) → caveat icon |
| Running order (#115) | `laps`: `date_start`, `lap_duration` (the crossings, A§13 Order). `starting_grid`: `position` → the Grid row and lap 1's gains (`results[].grid`, A§10) | A missing crossing → `—` with "No crossing for VER after lap n" and the row marked incomplete; no grid (no single qualifying, or a failed fetch with none stored, A§8) → "Grid not in the OpenF1 data", never the qualifying order (A§13) |
| Weather (P5a) | `weather`: `date`, `air_temperature`, `track_temperature`, `humidity`, `pressure`, `rainfall`, `wind_direction`, `wind_speed` | Rows from up to about an hour before the start to past `date_end` → every row is summarised (above); a value null in every sample → `—` with a reason; a session without rows → "No weather in the OpenF1 data." (A§13) |
| Qualifying phase per lap | No field. P1c derives it (A§9): the phase of the last `race_control` row with a `qualifying_phase` dated at or before the lap's `date_start`. Checked against `session_result.duration[p]` over the 112 qualifying sessions of the dev backfill (the same one-off run): per driver and phase, the fastest valid lap (not deleted, out or in) equals the official time in 4,972 cases and differs in 7; 41 official times have no lap at all (OpenF1 has no laps for 9900). The 7 are documented exceptions, not rule errors: two last laps that end in the pits after the flag (not in-laps, A§9; 9112, 9298; owner 2026-10-27: no rule change), two deletions race control never sent as DELETED (9983: only `LAP TIME OF CAR 87 (BEA) - UNDER REVIEW`; 11271 car 23 in SQ1: only a stewards' track-limits investigation after the session), three laps missing from OpenF1 (11379). In five 2026 sessions (11230, 11271, 11276, 11282, 11287) Q1's race-control rows carry no `qualifying_phase`; the Q1 inference (A§9) recovers their 103 Q1 times | Unknown phase → lap shown without a Q badge |

### 6.2 Official F1 archive (`livetiming.formula1.com/static`), P3 source

- Since ~15 Oct 2026 it returns 401 without a token. It needs F1 TV Access/Pro/Premium (the owner has Pro). Seasons 2018+.
- **How far back** (research, 2026-11-10; P3 decisions H1–H16, sources in ADR-0009):
  - FastF1's maintainer: "the lap timing isn't available either before 2018 on the F1 API" (FastF1 discussion #550). The docs say timing, car and position data are "available from 2018 onwards".
  - Without a token every year answers 401, 2016, 2017 and even 2099 (checked from a datacenter IP, which also gets CloudFront's 403 with a token). So whether `static/2017/Index.json` exists is **[U]**, for P3d's probe with the token.
  - FastF1's public mirror answers 404 for 2017–2019 and 200 for 2021, which shows only what the mirror holds.
  - The per-session `Index.json`, which lists a session's topics, exists from the 2018 US GP FP1 on (FastF1 #248, which lists 31 topic names).
  - Testing sessions are in the archive, but "hasn't always been replay data available" (FastF1 #688). Its path code has `Day_N` fixes for the 2025 and 2026 tests.
- **Known 2018 gaps** (FastF1 issues; **[U]** until P3d counts them):
  - Australia FP2 has about 2 s of position data in all (#677).
  - The Italian GP race has phantom stint messages (#930, open).
  - Delayed tyre data dropped stints, for example Azerbaijan (#863).
  - `CountryCode` and `HeadshotUrl` are empty (#668).
  - 2018–2020 stints were reported wrong, then judged plausible (#841).
- **What the archive has beyond OpenF1 for 2023 on** (from the #248 topic list and FastF1's `_api`; **[U]**):
  - `TimingStats` (best sectors, speed-trap ranks), the finish-line speed (`FL`; OpenF1 has none), `TyreStintSeries`, `CurrentTyres`, `DriverRaceInfo`, `LapSeries`, `PitLaneTimeCollection`, `ChampionshipPrediction`, `TopThree`, `SessionData` and the full `TeamRadio` list;
  - mini-sectors in races (OpenF1: "Segments are not available during races").
- Structure: `static/<year>/Index.json` → meetings → sessions (`Path`) → `<Path><Topic>.jsonStream` (and `.json`). The topics are the same as the live feed (A§11.1), with a session-relative timestamp on each line.
- The Index `Key` equals OpenF1's `session_key` (checked on 11392, 11397 and 11404).
- A topic the session lacks answers **403**, not 404 (`LapCount` outside a race). `scripts/fetch-archive.ts` skips 403 and 404 per file and stops only on 401, since the Index has already proved the token. Each `--topic <name>` (a letter, then letters and digits, an optional final `.z`) adds one topic to its list, so a [U] check can fetch a topic the views do not read yet (#96). Its own list holds the recorder's pit topics too (A§11.1). The archive job's list (`apps/server/src/archive/job.ts`) is unchanged, so `/srv/f1/archive` has no pit files until P3e.
- **The session `<Path>Index.json` lists its topics** (#96, 2026-11-13, 11404 race and 11408 qualifying): its only key paths are `Feeds.<Topic>.KeyFramePath` and `Feeds.<Topic>.StreamPath`, 32 topics for the race and 27 for the qualifying. On both sessions it matched every fetched topic: a listed one answered 200, an unlisted one 403. Five listed topics were not fetched. So it could replace the hard-coded download lists, for a later decision (P3e). The recorder can't use it, because it exists only in the archive.
- **Line format** (`.jsonStream`): a UTF-8 BOM, CRLF or LF line ends, then the time and the payload. The time is .NET TimeSpan text: 12 characters, `HH:MM:SS.mmm`, and past 24 h a day count in front, `d.HH:MM:SS.mmm`. A stream can open more than a day before its session (11404 opened 26 h 40 min before the start), and its clock runs continuously, so the first Heartbeat's `Utc` minus its time gives a t0 that holds within a few hundred ms. A `.z` payload is a quoted base64 string.
- **The final `.json` states are written later than the stream.** `ArchiveStatus` is "Generating" in the stream and "Complete" in the file, and `ExtrapolatedClock` differs. The stream carries `_deleted` keys (A§11.3) and the file holds none. Merging a stream gives the file's state apart from those two (11392, 11397).
- **The Heartbeat t0 runs late against OpenF1's timebase** by about 2 s (A§11.3, Timebase). The SessionData t0 below does not.
- **The archive t0** (P3a, `archiveT0` in `apps/server/src/live/read.ts`; owner decision O3, 2026-11-10): every SessionData `Series` and `StatusSeries` entry outside the stream's first line sits at exactly its own `Utc` on the archive clock (the first line, a list, holds older entries). So t0 is the most frequent `Utc` minus line time over those entries (list and dict forms), when at least 2 agree (a tie: the value seen first). Else (owner decision O4, 2026-11-10) the same mode over the `Utc` of the ExtrapolatedClock lines, when at least 2 agree; else the first Heartbeat. On 11392, 11397 and 11404 the SessionData t0 is 2021, 2018 and 2239 ms before the Heartbeat t0, which are exactly the P2b offsets against OpenF1 (A§11.3), so it is OpenF1's timebase to the ms and needs no `.z` download. [U] (WSL [box], 2026-11-10): the 2019 race 5498 has no SessionData (403), and its first Heartbeat sits about 470 s off the other Heartbeat lines, which shifted every lap start by 7.8 min. Every ExtrapolatedClock entry's `Utc` minus line time equals the SessionData t0 to the ms on 11392, 11397, 11404 and 6565 (31 of 31), and on 5498 all 14 agree on one offset. The archive job logs `t0` (`sessiondata`, `clock` or `heartbeat`) and `t0_heartbeat_delta_ms` per session; `compare-laps` and `feed-keys` read an archive directory with the same rule. At equal t the archive events keep topic-name order except SessionInfo, which goes first (the session gate in `laps.ts` reads it before the other topics).
- **SessionData rebuilt for 2018–2019** (P3a, `rebuildSessionData` in `apps/server/src/archive/rows.ts`; owner decision O6, 2026-11-10): when a session's streams hold no SessionData event at all, the archive rows rebuild it after the t0, from topics the old archives do have: a `StatusSeries` entry `{Utc, SessionStatus}` per SessionStatus line with a string `Status`, and a `Series` entry `{Utc, QualifyingPart}` per change of TimingData's top-level `SessionPart` (none for an unchanged value), each at its source's line time, which is also its `Utc`. Indices run per kind in the dict form. The entries are merged into the events in the order above, so they never feed the t0, and they give race lap 1 its `Started` start (A§11.3) and a qualifying its parts (A§7). An archive with SessionData (2020 on) is unchanged; `compare-laps` and `feed-keys` read only what the files hold (`archiveEvents`, no rebuild). [U] (WSL [box], 2026-11-10): SessionData answers 403 on 5086, 5122, 5494, 5498 and 5502 (2018 and 2019) and 200 on 5744 and 5748 (2020). A SessionStatus `Started` line's time on the archive t0 equals the SessionData `StatusSeries` `Started` `Utc` to the ms (11404: 3 of 3, 6675: 1 of 1), and TimingData's `SessionPart` changes at exactly the `QualifyingPart` instants (6565: 4 of 4, 0→1→2→3; 11392: 3 of 3, 1→2→3). The 2019 race 5498 and qualifying 5502 have a SessionStatus stream, and 5502 has `SessionPart` 1→2→3; without the rebuild all 20 lap-1 rows of 5498 had no `date_start`.
- **The `archive` job** (P3a, `apps/server/src/archive/`; `node apps/server/src/main.ts archive <year> [<session_key>…] [--token-file <path>] [--dir <path>]`):
  - **CLI:** years 2018–2022 only, else exit 64 (2023 on is OpenF1's, and the files that `fetch-archive.ts` and, later, P3e download for those years feed the `fill` job, never this one, A§7); keys are digits (`^\d{1,9}$`), else exit 64. The token file defaults to `/srv/f1/state/f1tv-token` (the `live` cache) and the directory to `/srv/f1/archive`; the flags are for a WSL run, with `--dir ~/F1_Timing/fixtures-private/archive` (gitignored, invariant 3), never a tracked path: the CLI takes `--dir` only when it resolves to `/srv/f1/archive` or has a path segment named exactly `fixtures-private`, else exit 64. The token goes through `readTokenFile` and `checkToken` (A§12.3); only its expiry is logged. An expired or bad token exits 1 with its code. Exit 0 when nothing failed, else 1.
  - **One run at a time:** the job first takes `pg_try_advisory_lock` on a fixed key (`LOCK_KEY`), session-level, on a connection it holds for the run and releases at the end; when another run holds it, it exits 1 with `busy` before any request (two runs would double the token's request rate).
  - **Laps through the merge** (P3b, owner decisions D2–D4, 2026-11-12): the job's laps are the `f1-archive` candidates of `writeLaps` (A§7), written in the same transaction after the other child rows. The job no longer deletes a session's `lap` rows itself: it replaces its own candidates and `lap` is rebuilt, so another source's fills stay (on an archive session nothing else writes laps yet, so `lap` equals the archive's rows with `filled` `{}`).
  - **Walk:** Index → meetings → sessions whose `Path` passes `isArchivePath` (`<year>/<segment>/<segment>/`, each segment letters of any script, digits, `_` and `-`, so never `.`, `/`, `%`, a space or a control character; owner decision O5, 2026-11-10) and starts with `<year>/` (other paths are counted as `bad_path` and logged; a session without a numeric Key or valid dates, or whose UTC `StartDate`/`EndDate` leave the year, end before they start or end after the job's now, is counted as `bad_index` and logged, so an archive row never looks like a coming session to the recorder or ingest's live window), optionally limited to the given keys (a key not in the Index counts as failed, `not_in_index`). Per session 10 topics: SessionInfo, Heartbeat, SessionData, ExtrapolatedClock (the t0 fallback, O4), TimingData, TimingAppData, DriverList, RaceControlMessages, WeatherData and SessionStatus (the SessionData rebuild, O6) (what the rows need; a later phase can add topics and the job resumes). [U] (WSL [box], 2026-11-10): an Index `Path` can hold non-ASCII letters (São Paulo 2021) and a `../uat/…` path (Key 12001 in the 2021 Index, dated 2022, which counts as `bad_index`).
  - **Rate:** one request at a time, at least 2 s between request starts (≤ 1,800 an hour). A season is about 110 sessions × 10 topics + 1 Index ≈ 1,100 requests (about 37 min); 2018–2022 is about 550 sessions × 10 + 5 ≈ 5,500 requests ≈ 3 h.
  - **Files:** `<dir>/index-<year>.json` and `<dir>/<session_key>/<Topic>.jsonStream`, the `fetch-archive.ts` layout, so `compare-laps` and `feed-keys` read them as they are. A 403 or 404 leaves an empty `<Topic>.missing` marker, so a rerun does not ask again; never for SessionInfo or TimingData, where it ends the session for this run as `incomplete` and a rerun asks again. Every file is written under a fresh temporary name (`<Topic>.jsonStream.<uuid>.tmp`, opened `wx`) and renamed, so a present file is complete; a body cut mid-download writes nothing. The files are kept plain, not gzipped (960 GB free, A§14.1).
  - **Resume:** a rerun makes only the Index request and skips every file or marker that is present, then rebuilds the rows from the local files (the write is idempotent, so a parser fix applies by rerunning).
  - **Errors:** 403 or 404 → the topic is missing, the job goes on; **401 stops the run** (exit 1), and so does a token within 60 s of its `exp`, checked before each request (`token_expired`); a timeout (60 s), network error, other status, a 200 with an empty body (no file and no marker is written) or a file over 64 MiB (the largest seen is the 29 h race TimingData, about 7 MB) fails that session for this run (logged as `timeout`, `network`, `http_<status>`, `empty`, `too_large`); so does a session whose topic files hold over 16 MiB on disk in all (`MAX_SESSION_BYTES`, summed before any is read, so nothing is parsed: the parse holds the whole session, about 18 MB of RSS per MB of input above a ~150 MB base, below), logged `too_large` and the run goes on and exits 1. No retries inside a run: the rerun is the retry. Index failures stop the run (`index_<code>`, `index_empty`, or `index_format`).
  - **Disk floor:** before each session the job stops with exit 1 (`disk_low`) when `statfs` says `<dir>` has under 5 GiB free: the recorder warns at 5 GiB and stops at 1 GiB (A§11.2), so the archive never pushes it to its floor.
  - **Written only when whole:** a session without SessionInfo or TimingData gets no meeting or session row and is logged `incomplete` (else the season page would show an empty session as ready); a SessionInfo `Key` that is not the Index key is logged `key_mismatch` and skipped.
  - **Live window:** before the Index and before each session the job checks the A§6.1 window (ingest's `live()`) and stops with exit 1 inside one (`live_session`). That keeps it clear of the recorder, which uses the same token, and of F1's busiest hours.
  - **Log:** per session `archive {session_key, year, type, files, skipped_files, t0, t0_heartbeat_delta_ms, laps, stints, drivers, race_control, weather, phases, rules, skipped_lines, merge, ms, error}`, at the end `archive_done {year, imported, failed, incomplete, bad_path, t0_heartbeat, error}` (`t0_heartbeat`: imported sessions whose t0 fell back to the Heartbeat, with neither SessionData nor ExtrapolatedClock, limit B; such a session's `archive` line is at error level, since its times can be minutes off). `laps: 0` or `phases: 0` shows an old-format gap. Never the token, a URL, a payload or a message text.
- **[box] 2018–2022 facts (P3a; WSL, 2026-11-10, a local PostgreSQL 18, Node 26.10.0; counts and offsets only, no values):** four sessions imported: the 2019 Italian GP race 5498 and qualifying 5502, the 2021 Mexico City GP qualifying 6565 and the 2021 São Paulo GP race 6675.
  - **Index Keys:** every listed session has a numeric Key: 2019 5394–5558 and 2021 6210–7165, and the samples of 2018 5074–5134 and 2020 5744–5796. The archive's session keys stay below 7,200 and its meeting keys run 1013–1104; the local database's OpenF1 rows start at session 7763 and meeting 1140, so there is no collision through 2021. 2022 answers 403 (the VM import below); the write refuses any collision anyway (`not_archive`).
  - **2021 sprints:** Type `Race`, Name `Sprint Qualifying` (keys 6425, 6272, 6674).
  - **t0 source:** 2018 and 2019 have no SessionData, so they use `clock`: 5498, and 5502 at −1,969 ms against the first Heartbeat. 2020 and 2021 use `sessiondata`: 6565 at −1,991 ms and 6675 at +818,227 ms. On 5498 the first Heartbeat was 469,926 ms off and on 6675 818,227 ms off, so a Heartbeat t0 can be minutes wrong, not just about 2 s. No imported session fell back to the Heartbeat (`t0_heartbeat: 0`).
  - **Rebuilt SessionData (2019):** on 5498 lap 1 has a start on 20 of 20 drivers; on 5502 `phases` is 3 and 274 of 275 laps are phased.
  - **Size:** 9–10 topic files plus markers per session: race 5498 3.02 MB, race 6675 6.61 MB, qualifying 6565 1.22 MB, qualifying 5502 0.41 MB. A season (about 21 meetings of 5 sessions) is about 0.1–0.2 GB, so 2018–2022 is about 0.5–1 GB. Both are estimates from these 4 measured sessions (2 races, 2 qualifyings); no practice, sprint or test session was measured.
  - **Speed:** about 17 s per session at the 2 s gap (9–10 requests); a rerun of an imported session takes under 1 s plus the Index request.
  - **Peak RSS** (`/usr/bin/time -v`, the `archive` role for one session): 157–271 MB. 5502 157 MB, 6565 202 MB, 5498 226 MB with downloads and 219–222 MB on reruns, 6675 271 MB (the 1,363-lap race). All are well under the service's 512m limit.
  - **FastF1 3.9.2 spot check** (signed in to F1 TV, in a venv outside the repository; laps paired by driver and the nearest start within 1 s after removing the median start offset; LapTime and S1–S3 must agree within 1 ms, and Deleted against ours):
    - 5498 race: 983 of 991 laps paired. Without lap 1, 961 of 963 match within 1 ms; the 2 misses are laps where the archive has no S1 and FastF1 derives S1 as LapTime − S2 − S3. Lap 1 (20 drivers) differs in LapTime and S1 by definition: ours is OpenF1's rule (Started to lap 2's start, S1 the rest), which matched OpenF1 on 11404's lap 1, and FastF1 has its own. Deleted agrees on 983 of 983. The 8 unpaired are flying laps within 5 s.
    - 5502 qualifying: 223 of 275 paired, 194 of 223 within 1 ms. All 29 misses are FastF1 giving no time where the archive feed has one: 27 laps over 8 min across red flags or part breaks, and 2 laps around the red flags. Deleted agrees on 223 of 223. The 52 unpaired are pit-in and pit-out laps (FastF1 starts an out-lap at pit exit).
    - 6565 qualifying: 249 of 311 paired, 232 of 249 within 1 ms. All 17 misses are break-spanning laps of about 28–33 min that FastF1 leaves without a time. Deleted agrees on 249 of 249. The 62 unpaired are out-laps.
    - Start offset (ours − FastF1), median: 316, 360 and 241 ms; on the 2026 OpenF1 rows of 11392 it is 65 ms (and 209 of 210 laps are within 1 ms there). So FastF1's lap starts carry their own few-hundred-ms error; the archive t0 equals OpenF1's timebase (above).
- **VM import (2026-11-12, the first release with P3a; counts only):**
  - **2018–2021:** 2018 95 sessions and 52,756 laps, 2019 104 and 59,016, 2020 83 and 46,529, 2021 108 and 59,555. Apart from 7165 below, no session failed, none was `incomplete` or `bad_path`, and none fell back to the Heartbeat t0 (`t0_heartbeat: 0`). `/srv/f1/archive` holds 660 MB. A season took 28–36 min.
  - **2018 Index gaps:** the 2018 Index has no Australian GP, Bahrain lists 5 sessions of which 1 has a `Path`, and China 5 with 4. The job never sees a session without a `Path`, so these are gaps in F1's archive, not failures.
  - **7165** (2021 Abu Dhabi GP, Practice 1): the Index Key is 7165, but its SessionInfo says 6594, with the same meeting key (1107). 6594 is nowhere in the 2021 Index; the weekend's other keys are 6595, 6596, 6597 and 6601. The job skips it as `key_mismatch`, so every 2021 run ends with `failed: 1` and exit 1. It stays out (owner decision, 2026-11-12).
  - **2022:** the Index and the session files answer 403 (111 bytes) with a valid token, while 2021 and 2023 answer 200 (local probe on 2026-11-12 of `static/2022/Index.json` and the `SessionInfo.jsonStream` of two 2022 races). A 2022 run ends with `index_http_403` and imports nothing. The job keeps 2022 (owner decision, 2026-11-12), so a rerun fills it if F1 serves the year again. OpenF1 starts in 2023, so no other source has 2022's laps.
- **Token for local downloads:** `--token-file <path>`, a regular file with mode 0600 and one line that holds either the raw `subscriptionToken` JWT or the URL-encoded `login-session` cookie. Only the expiry is printed. The script refuses under `CI` and writes to the gitignored `fixtures-private/archive/<key>/`.
- Requests carry `Authorization: Bearer <subscriptionToken>` (A§12.3). It works from home IPs; datacenter ranges such as GitHub Actions get a CloudFront 403.

### 6.3 Others

- **Jolpica-F1** (`api.jolpi.ca/ergast`, the Ergast successor; P3c, owner 2026-11-10). Checked 2026-11-10 against its docs and API, and 2026-11-12 against the delayed CSV dump of 2026-10-27 (S0 and S2 of P3c; counts and formats only, never a value):
  - **Coverage:** results and driver standings from 1950, constructor standings from 1958, and sprints from 2021.
  - **Terms:** the data is CC BY-NC-SA 4.0 (non-commercial; ADR-0009). The API allows 4 req/s and 500 req/h. P3c imports the CSV dump (H12), never from CI and never from the API.
  - **The dump is not Ergast's format.**
    - **Download:** `GET https://api.jolpi.ca/data/dumps/download/` answers JSON whose `delayed_dumps.csv` (and `latest_dumps.csv`) holds the `uploaded_at`, `file_size` and `file_hash` (sha256). `GET …/download/delayed/?dump_type=csv` answers a 302 to S3 (`curl -L`). No key is needed. The free (delayed) copy is 14 days behind: the one of 2026-10-27 is 14,669,906 bytes.
    - **Files:** a flat zip, `jolpica-f1-csv.zip`, of 18 `formula_one_<table>.csv` files (one Django table each). The importer reads `season`, `round`, `session`, `circuit`, `driver`, `team`, `teamdriver`, `roundentry`, `sessionentry`, `lap` and `pitstop`. The largest file is `lap` (37 MB, 740,109 rows); the longest field is 83 characters.
    - **Layout:** UTF-8 without a BOM, a header row, CRLF line endings, and Postgres `COPY … CSV` quoting. No field holds a quote or a newline, but the reader handles RFC 4180. The columns are `id` first, then alphabetical: read by header name.
    - **Values:** a null is an empty unquoted field (never `\N`) and a boolean is `t` or `f`. A duration is `H+:MM:SS[.f{0,3}]` (0 to 3 fractional digits; race times pass an hour) and there is no milliseconds column. `session.timestamp` is `YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM:SS+00:00` (UTC), `pitstop.local_timestamp` is `HH:MM:SS` and `round.date` is `YYYY-MM-DD`. `round` has no year: it comes through `season_id` and `season.year`. `session.timezone` (IANA) is set on every session. The `penalty` and `baseteam` tables are empty.
    - **Session types** from 1950 to 2017: `R`, `FP1`–`FP3`, `Q1`–`Q3`, `QA`, `QB`, `QO`; `SR` and `SQ1`–`SQ3` come from 2021 and 2023. A race has one `R` session per round (976 rounds up to 2017). **Status codes** in a race: 0 and 1 finished, 10 and 11 retired, 20 DSQ, 30 DNS or withdrawn; 40 (DNQ) occurs outside races only (8 rows) and 41 never.
  - **`has_time_data`:** false on every race before 2000, mixed in 2000–2004 and true from 2005. When it is false the timestamp is exactly 00:00 UTC (3,944 of 3,944 sessions), so the dump knows the day only (E4, A§7).
  - **Qualifying:** `sessionentry.time` is never set in qualifying: a part's time is the best of the entry's `lap` rows in that session (rows without a lap number count, they are fastest-lap markers). There is none before 1994 (the QB sessions have no entries). The eras, and the entry's position as it is stored:

    | Years | Sessions of a round | Notes |
    |---|---|---|
    | 1994–95 | two `QB` | |
    | 1996–2002 | one `QB` | sparse: 148 entries in 1996, 22 in 2001 |
    | 2003–04 | `QB` and a `QO` | `QO` has no entries |
    | 2005 | one `QB` (13 rounds) or two `QA` (6 rounds) | |
    | 2006–2017 | `Q1`, `Q2`, `Q3` | a position on every entry (a part's rank) |

    One rule for every era: **the position in the last part the entry has one in** (`Q3`, else `Q2`, else `Q1`; the later session of a two-session round). The check (S2) compares it with the race's `grid` of the same entry (grid above 0; a pit-lane start and a null are left out). Exact is the share of entries whose position equals the grid; in order is the longest run of rising grids once a round is sorted by the rule's position, so the entries no penalty moved. Exact undercounts, because one grid penalty moves every car behind it up a place (in 2006–17, 398 of 623 misses in Q1, 283 of 437 in Q2 and 214 of 343 in Q3 are a grid one place better than the position):

    | Years | Entries | Exact | In order |
    |---|---|---|---|
    | 1994–95 (`QB` + `QB`) | 806 | 98.6 % | 100.0 % |
    | 1996–2002 (`QB`) | 737 | 97.7 % | 99.2 % |
    | 2003–04 (`QB`) | 680 | 89.3 % | 97.6 % |
    | 2005 (`QB`) | 260 | 75.4 % | 96.2 % |
    | 2005 (`QA` + `QA`) | 116 | 77.6 % | 94.8 % |
    | 2006–17 (`Q1`–`Q3`) | 4,880 | 71.5 % | 94.0 % |

    The best lowest position gives the same figures in every era, and the position in the round's last session, the ranks of the best lap-row time and the ranks of the sum of the part times are never better (in 2006–17 the best lap-row time is 50.3 % exact and 81.3 % in order), so the rule stands everywhere.
  - **Laps:** races only. Every race from 1996 has laps (14,431 lap rows in 1996) and 1995 has none (the earliest lap rows, 1994, are qualifying). A lap row has `number`, `time` and `session_entry_id`, never a date, so `date_start` stays null. In a race the rows never lack a lap number; the 22 to 1,000 rows a year with a null number are all qualifying. There is no duplicate (entry, lap number) and every `time` is set. From 2018 on the dump has the laps too (A§7, the 2018+ tier).
  - **Race time:** `sessionentry.time` is each driver's total race time, not a gap: in all 973 races up to 2017 with a winner time, every other time is at least the winner's (3 races have none). So `gaps_ms` is each time minus the winner's (A§7).
  - **Pit stops:** from 2011 (2010: 0; 6,251 up to 2017), with `lap_id` (never null, never duplicated, always an existing lap of the same entry), `duration` and `local_timestamp` (the local time of day). No tyre compound. Combined with the race's local date and the session's zone, all 6,251 up to 2017 fall 0–4 h after the race start. 35 of them have no next lap, so there are 6,216 out-laps.
  - **Car numbers** (`roundentry.car_number`, up to 999): numbers shared by two entries of one race occur in 45 sessions of the 1950s (129 extra entries), 14 of the 1960s (19), 1 of the 1970s and 1 of the 1980s; 6 entries (1960s) have none; there are no collisions from 1990 on. They come from shared drives (E3, A§7).
  - **Other columns:** `country_code` (circuit, team and driver) is always 3 letters. `team.primary_color` is null on 202 of 214 teams and `#rrggbb` on the rest. `driver.abbreviation` is null on 774 of 881 drivers (the 107 that have one mostly start from 2000) and 3 letters otherwise. Every race entry resolves through `roundentry`, `teamdriver`, `driver` and `team`. No session before 2018 is cancelled.
  - **Not used:** no sectors, mini-sectors or speed traps. Standings, points and penalties (7 of the 18 files) are not imported (#123, P3g).
- **FIA timing PDFs** (`fia.com/f1-archives`; P3f, owner 2026-11-10). Checked 2026-11-10 by reading 2012, 2014 and 2017 files:
  - **Seasons:** tabs from 2012 to 2025. Where 2026 lives, and anything before 2012, are **[U]**.
  - **Per event:** for practice and qualifying, the lap times. For the race, the lap chart, history chart, lap analysis, best sector times, speed trap, maximum speeds and pit stop summary.
  - **Sectors:** the 2012–2017 files carry lap times with the pit laps marked, plus each driver's best sectors and the top-10 trap table, so no per-lap sectors.
  - **Race Sector Analysis:** per-lap sectors and speeds, seen for 2023 (Saudi Arabia). Its first year is **[U]**.
  - **File names:** for example `2017_15_mas_f1_r0_timing_racespeedtrap_v01.pdf`.
  - **Terms:** © FOWC, "No part … may be reproduced … without prior permission"; the site terms allow private, non-commercial use. Treated like F1 TV data (H10, A§12.1).
- **Not used** (2026-11-10):
  - formula1.com's results pages: its legal notice allows "personal, non-commercial use only", and its guidelines bar text and data mining. They hold nothing the FIA PDFs and Jolpica lack (classifications from 1950, pit stop summaries in 2003 and 2015).
  - The Kaggle and TracingInsights copies of Ergast: they are marked CC0, but they derive from NC-SA data.
  - statsf1, forix and motorsportstats: no open licence.
  - No public recording of the pre-2018 live-timing applet was found.
- **F1 TV data screens** (H9, P3d [owner]): **[U]** whether the F1 TV web app fetches timing JSON for a replay beyond the archive. Only such JSON counts; never video, OCR or anything that gets around DRM.
- **FastF1** (MIT, Python): not a dependency. It is the reference for the archive/feed format, lap reconstruction and the F1 TV login flow. Any ported logic keeps its MIT notice.
- **Open-Meteo** (owner, 2026-11-07, P5c; `apps/server/src/rain/`, licences in ADR-0008): the rain map's source. It is a weather **model**, not radar. Checked on 2026-11-09, the U facts with their proof (a dozen plain requests, `user-agent: f1-timings`, no key):
  - **Hosts and terms:** `https://api.open-meteo.com/v1/forecast` and `https://historical-forecast-api.open-meteo.com/v1/forecast` (from 2022), the same parameters. Free and keyless for non-commercial use (a private site without ads or subscriptions counts), CC BY 4.0, 600 calls/min, 5,000/h, 10,000/day, 300,000/month. Errors are HTTP 400 `{"error":true,"reason":…}`; a 429 is undocumented. A one-location answer is a single object, a multi-location answer a list in request order, so the client always asks for 49 points and takes anything else as `schema`.
  - **The query** (`rainQuery`, C1): `latitude` and `longitude` lists of 49 points (4 decimals), `minutely_15=precipitation` (mm in the preceding 15 minutes, 2 decimals), `start_minutely_15` and `end_minutely_15` in UTC, `timeformat=unixtime`, `timezone=GMT` and `cell_selection=nearest` (U5). The same shape on both hosts: no `past_minutely_15`, so a forecast fetch never holds a step after now.
  - **U2, the cost of a call:** the pricing FAQ counts a call as more than one only past 10 variables or 2 weeks for a single location ("Typically, one API call corresponds to one HTTP API request."), and Open-Meteo's own post "Weather data for multiple locations" (3 Nov 2023) says "The number of locations is now a factor in this calculation" and "you can fetch data for up to 1000 locations in a single go!". Its server code floors each location at 1 call (`calculateQueryWeight`, open-meteo `main` 97132c5). So one fetch of 49 points, 1 variable, a few hours costs **49 location-calls**, and 1,000 locations per call is the maximum. The budget (A§8, D3) counts in location-calls.
  - **U3, when the last steps are final:** a query on both hosts at 2026-11-09 10:12 UTC for the last 9 h answered identically, with no trailing null, and the Historical host answered a window in the future in full. It serves the same stitched series as the Forecast host ("Each run's first few hours are stitched into a continuous hourly timeseries"), so a step is final only once a model run that started after it is published. Run times read from each model's `meta.json`: ICON-D2 every 3 h, published 2.5 h after its run, so a step settles within about 5.5 h; RRFS about 4.9 h, KNMI about 3.6 h, HRRR about 2.5 h, AROME HD about 1.6 h; for the global models that carry most of the calendar GFS about 11.5 h and ECMWF IFS 12.9–14.4 h. **End + 3 h is not safe; the final fetch is at `date_end` + 24 h** (OpenF1's last fetch time too), and the backfill takes only sessions past it (A§8).
  - **U4, native 15-minute data per circuit** (`native15` in `CIRCUITS`): `best_match` takes the first region that matches, and only Central Europe (ICON-D2), France and Western Europe (AROME HD) and North America (HRRR/RRFS) have 15-minute data; elsewhere Open-Meteo "uses interpolated hourly data". `native15` is **true** for Monte Carlo, Hungaroring, Imola, Monza, Spielberg (ICON-D2), and Montreal, Miami, Austin, Las Vegas (HRRR grid). It is **false** for Spa-Francorchamps and Zandvoort (the "Netherlands & Belgium" box, lat 49.35–53.79 and lon 2.19–7.66, comes first and uses KNMI HARMONIE plus hourly ICON, no 15-minute data: the 2023 Zandvoort grid repeats one value in every 15-minute step and in all 49 cells), for Catalunya and Madring (south of ICON-D2 at 43.18 N and of the AROME box at 42.10 N), for Mexico City (about 570 km south of the HRRR grid), for Silverstone (UK Met Office, interpolated) and for every other circuit (global models). The map says "Coarse model here: the field may look uniform." for those (A§13). ICON-D2 and HRRR are marked discontinued on 2026-11-30 in the docs (best_match still routed to them when read): **[U]** check again in December.
  - **U5, order and snapping:** the answer is in request order (entry k ≥ 1 carries `location_id: k`; checked on all 49 of `central-wet`), and the returned coordinates are the model cell's, not the request's (Monaco, ICON-D2: requested 43.7367, 7.4258, returned 43.734154, 7.4216666). The grid's 1.5 km step is finer than every model, so neighbouring points often share a cell: 29 distinct cells among the 49 at Monaco (ICON-D2), 5 at Singapore (global models). The default `cell_selection=land` moved a point 3 km south of Monaco, at sea, onto the circuit's own land cell, so a coastal circuit would draw its land rain over the sea: the query sets `nearest` (C7).
  - **U6, C1:** the Forecast host answers the `start_minutely_15` and `end_minutely_15` window for the last 9 h in full (37 steps, identical to the Historical host).
  - **Fixtures** (`apps/server/src/rain/fixtures/`, each `meta.json` with "Data: Open-Meteo.com, CC BY 4.0"): `central-wet` is the 2023 Monaco race (session 9094, ICON-D2; OpenF1's rainfall is 1 from 14:12 to 14:37 UTC, and the grid shows light rain in 6 cells at 13:45 and 2 at 14:00, at most 0.2 mm per step), `outside-dry` the 2026 Singapore race (11388, global models, dry), `error` a recorded 400. The 2023 Zandvoort race (9149) was recorded and rejected: its hourly totals are split evenly over the 15-minute steps and are identical in all 49 cells. No UK session is recorded (C6): Open-Meteo takes UK data from the UK Met Office, CC BY-SA 4.0.
- **bacinger/f1-circuits** (owner, 2026-11-07, P5c; MIT): the circuit outlines. Vendored verbatim in `data/third-party/f1-circuits/` at commit `8094e957b8d6aa8b9f4510c93968556cccd30e02` (2026-10-16): `f1-circuits.geojson`, 116,166 bytes, 40 `LineString` features whose coordinates are **`[lon, lat]`** (the Monza feature `it-1922` has its bounding-box centre at 45.6216 N, 9.2888 E, which matches the landmark). The 26 OpenF1 `circuit_short_name`s of 2023–2026 map by a hand table in `scripts/map-extract.ts` to a feature id, and the script writes `packages/contracts/src/circuits.ts` (`CIRCUITS`: id, centre = the bounding-box centre rounded to 4 decimals, `native15`) and one `apps/web/src/map/<id>.json` per circuit: the outline projected to km around the centre (`project` in `packages/contracts/src/geo.ts`, equirectangular at the centre latitude, R = 6371.0088 km), simplified (Douglas–Peucker, 15 m), clipped to ±6 km, rounded to 0.01 km, as SVG path data with north up, at most 40 KiB (the files are 290–510 bytes). A circuit name not in `CIRCUITS` has no map (C5).
- **Overpass and OpenStreetMap** (owner, 2026-11-07, P5c; ODbL 1.0, **route B** of ADR-0008, approved by the owner, 2026-11-09): `node scripts/map-extract.ts --osm` on the owner's machine only (it refuses under `CI`; CI never calls Overpass) sends one Overpass query per circuit at least 10 s apart for the coast, water and main roads (motorway, trunk, primary) within ±6 km, and writes `apps/web/src/map/osm/<id>.json`, a Derivative Database under the ODbL with its own notice (`osm/README.md`); the raw answers are never committed. The code and the outline files stay in their own licences (README, "Third-party data").

## 7. Data model (PostgreSQL 18)

All times are `timestamptz` in UTC, durations are integer milliseconds, and speeds are smallint km/h. Every row carries `source` (`openf1` | `f1-archive` | `f1-live` | `jolpica`; P3f adds `fia`). A lap's values can come from several sources (P3b, owner 2026-11-10): the row's `source` is the highest-ranked source that has the lap, and `lap.filled` names the source of each value that came from another one (`The merge` below).

| Table | Key | Columns (outline) |
|---|---|---|
| `meeting` | `meeting_key` | year, name, official_name, location, country_code, circuit_short_name, gmt_offset, date_start, date_end, is_cancelled |
| `session` | `session_key` | meeting_key, type, name, date_start, date_end (both NOT NULL), is_cancelled, `ingest_state` (scheduled · due · fetched · final · failed), `next_fetch_at`, `fetch_count` (successes), `fail_count` (consecutive failures), `last_error` (a fixed code, CHECK: `http_NNN` · `rate_limited` · `timeout` · `network` · `schema` · `empty` · `db` · `unknown`; never OpenF1 or PostgreSQL text) |
| `driver` | (session_key, driver_number) | acronym, full_name, team_name, team_colour |
| `lap` | (session_key, driver_number, lap_number) | date_start, lap_ms, s1_ms, s2_ms, s3_ms, i1_kph, i2_kph, st_kph, is_pit_out, is_pit_in, seg1/seg2/seg3 `smallint[]`, deleted, deleted_reason, quali_phase, `filled` (P3b: `jsonb` object, `{column: source}` for each value from another source than the row's `source`; `{}` when none) |
| `lap_source` (P3b) | (session_key, driver_number, lap_number, source) | `session_key` (`ON DELETE CASCADE`), `source` (the same CHECK as `lap`) and the merged value columns of `lap`: date_start, lap_ms, s1_ms, s2_ms, s3_ms, i1_kph, i2_kph, st_kph, is_pit_out, seg1/seg2/seg3 `smallint[]`, quali_phase. Every source's candidate laps of a session. No `is_pit_in`, `deleted` or `deleted_reason`: they are derived after the merge |
| `stint` | (session_key, driver_number, stint_number) | lap_start, lap_end, compound, tyre_age_at_start |
| `pit` | (session_key, driver_number, lap_number) | date, pit_ms, lane_ms, stop_ms |
| `race_control` | id | session_key, date, category, flag, scope, sector, driver_number, lap_number, qualifying_phase, message |
| `result` | (session_key, driver_number) | position, laps, `grid` (P3c, Jolpica, where 0 is a pit-lane start; since #115 also OpenF1's `starting_grid` for races and sprints, A§8), dnf, dns, dsq, durations_ms `int[]`, gaps_ms `int[]` (a race's single value as a 1-element array, Q1–Q3 in qualifying; a string gap such as `+1 LAP` is stored as `{NULL}`: 8 of 22 rows in `session_result-normal`) |
| `weather` (P5a) | (session_key, date) | air_temperature, track_temperature, humidity, pressure (`real`: °C, %, mbar), rainfall (smallint, 0 or 1), wind_direction (smallint, degrees), wind_speed (`real`, m/s); OpenF1's own names (owner, 2026-11-07) |
| `raw_response` | (session_key, endpoint) | endpoint one of the eight per-session endpoints (a CHECK; `weather` since P5a, `starting_grid` since #115: a race's grid, stored under the race's key although OpenF1 serves it under the qualifying's), fetched_at, http_status (OpenF1's 404 "No results found." is stored as 404 with body `[]`), body `jsonb` (latest only) |
| `ingest_status` (#105) | one row (`id boolean`, `CHECK (id)`) | last_sync_at (null until the daemon's first good sync), live_wait (the daemon is waiting out a live window), last_tick_at. The `ingest` daemon's own status for `/status` (A§8, A§10), as `recorder_status` is the recorder's; display only |
| `recording` (P2c) | id (identity) | session_key (`ON DELETE RESTRICT`: recordings stay replayable forever, ADR-0007), source (default `f1-live`), path (unique, relative to `/srv/f1/live`: `<session_key>-<startedAtMs>`, and a CHECK holds that shape), started_at, ended_at (null while open), bytes (raw feed bytes written, updated every 30 s), complete (A§11.2) |
| `recorder_status` (P2c) | one row (`id boolean`, `CHECK (id)`) | state (`idle` · `connecting` · `recording`), session_key (`ON DELETE SET NULL`), token_expires_at, `last_error` (a fixed code, CHECK: `no_credentials` · `login_*` · `token_file` · `token_format` · `token_expired` · `feed_*` · `disk` · `disk_low` · `unknown`; never F1 or PostgreSQL text), `warning` (`token_expiring` · `login_failed` · `disk_low`), login_failures, last_login_at, updated_at |
| `rain_grid` (P5c) | `session_key` (`ON DELETE CASCADE`) | source (CHECK `open-meteo`), map_id (the bacinger id), centre_lat, centre_lon (the grid's centre, from `CIRCUITS`), size (smallint, CHECK 7), step_m (int, CHECK > 0; 1500), state (`live` · `final` · `failed`), `next_fetch_at`, `fetch_count` (successful fetches), `fail_count` (consecutive failures), `attempt_count` (every Open-Meteo attempt, success or failure, counted in the write that records its outcome: the budget seed, A§8), `last_error` (a fixed code, CHECK: `http_NNN` · `rate_limited` · `timeout` · `network` · `schema` · `db` · `unknown`; never Open-Meteo or PostgreSQL text), updated_at (owner, 2026-11-07; D1 and C2 owner, 2026-11-09) |
| `rain_frame` (P5c) | (session_key, date) | session_key REFERENCES `rain_grid` (`ON DELETE CASCADE`, C2), mm `real[]` (CHECK cardinality 49): the precipitation in the 15 minutes before `date`, in mm, per grid point, row-major from the north-west (row 0 north, column 0 west); an element may be null |

Size (measured 2026-10-26, the [box] backfill of 2023 up to the 2026 Mexican GP): 495 sessions (455 past and processed, 25 future, 15 cancelled) take **102 MB** including raw JSON: `raw_response` 21 MB (21 %, 2,730 rows), `lap` 59 MB (258,639 rows), every other table under 6 MB. The Jolpica import (P3c, A§7 [box], the same database plus 4 archive sessions) adds 427,092 `lap` rows (426,643 of 1950–2017 and 449 laps added in the 2018+ tier) and 524,241 `lap_source` rows (the 1950–2017 laps and the 2018+ candidates), 29,933 `driver` and as many `result` rows, 6,251 `pit` rows, 1,321 sessions and 976 meetings. After it `lap` was 159 MB (691,342 rows) and `lap_source` 160 MB (791,969 rows), the database 379 MB; these include the dead rows of the repeated [box] runs (no `VACUUM FULL`), so they are an upper bound.

**Archive rows** (P3a, A§6.2; `apps/server/src/archive/rows.ts`): the `archive` job writes the same tables with `source = 'f1-archive'`, one transaction per session, replace-per-session as in A§8.
  - **meeting and session** come from Index.json, not from OpenF1. The Index dates are the circuit's local time, so `date_start` and `date_end` are `StartDate` and `EndDate` minus `GmtOffset`; a meeting's dates are the min and max of its sessions'. `gmt_offset` is written in OpenF1's `[-]HH:MM:SS` form, without a `+` (the web's `fmtTrack` refuses one). Archive sessions are `final` with `next_fetch_at` null and `fetch_count` 1, and they have no `raw_response` rows.
