If the meeting or session row already exists with another source, the job changes nothing (`not_archive`).
  - **No `pit` rows:** no view reads `/pits` (a follow-up).
  - **result:** one row per driver in the end state's TimingData lines, with only `position` (null when it is not an integer) and `laps` (`NumberOfLaps`); `dnf`, `dns`, `dsq` and the durations stay null (the views use only the position).
  - **quali_phase and race_control.qualifying_phase** come from the SessionData `Series[].QualifyingPart` (RaceControlMessages carry none; in 2018–2019, from its rebuild out of TimingData's `SessionPart`, A§6.2): a message gets the last part at or before its `Utc`, and `markLaps` also gets one message-less boundary per part change, so a lap's phase follows SessionData exactly. A message's `Utc` has no `Z` and is read as UTC.
  - **weather:** one row per WeatherData event, keyed on its instant (A§8). **driver:** from DriverList (`Tla`, `FullName`, `TeamName`, `TeamColour`). Laps and stints come from the P2b parser (A§11.3); the laps go through the merge (below), the stints as before.
  - **No migration was needed:** the `source` CHECK on every table already allows `f1-archive`, `f1_ingest` already has `INSERT`/`UPDATE` on `meeting` and `session` and `DELETE` on the child tables, and no CHECK limits years or keys.

**Jolpica rows** (P3c, A§6.3; `apps/server/src/jolpica/`): the `jolpica` job writes the 1950–2017 races and qualifyings of the Jolpica-F1 CSV dump as `source = 'jolpica'`, one transaction per session, replace-per-session as in A§8. The dump's facts are in A§6.3.
  - **Keys (confirmed, P3 "Keys for sessions without one"):** `meeting_key = 1_000_000 + year*100 + round` (`1YYYYRR`) and `session_key = meeting_key*100 + TT` (`1YYYYRRTT`, 9 digits: int4 and `^\d{1,9}$` safe, far above every OpenF1 and archive key). Only for years 1950–2017 and rounds 1–99, else `bad_key`. `TT` is a fixed code per session type, which P3f reuses:

    | TT | Session |
    |---|---|
    | 01 | Practice 1 |
    | 02 | Practice 2 |
    | 03 | Practice 3 |
    | 04 | Qualifying |
    | 05 | Race |

    The job writes only 04 and 05: practice, sprint and pre-qualifying sessions are not imported.
  - **meeting:** `name` is the round's name, `official_name` is null, `location` is the circuit's locality, `country_code` is its 3-letter code as given, `circuit_short_name` is its name, and `is_cancelled` is the round's. `gmt_offset` is in `[-]HH:MM:SS` (no `+`, as for the archive), from the race's IANA zone at its start (the first qualifying part's when the race has no time data), through `Intl.DateTimeFormat`; null without time data or a zone. `date_start` and `date_end` are the min and max of its sessions'.
  - **session:** `type` and `name` are `Race` or `Qualifying`. Every qualifying part of the round (`Q1`–`Q3`, or the single-session types, A§6.3) becomes one `Qualifying` session, written only when an entry has a time or a position. `ingest_state` is `final` with `next_fetch_at` null, `fetch_count` 1 and `fail_count` 0 (the archive pattern); there is no `raw_response`. With time data `date_start` is the timestamp and `date_end` is `date_start` + 2 h for a race, or 1 h after the last part's start for a qualifying. **E4, unknown start time** (owner, 2026-11-12): when `has_time_data` is false, `date_start` is the session's day at 00:00 UTC and `date_end = date_start`, Jolpica's own convention and no new column. P3g shows the date only for such a session.
  - **driver:** `acronym` is the abbreviation (null when none), `full_name` is the forename and surname, `team_name` comes through `teamdriver`, and `team_colour` is `primary_color` as 6 hex digits without `#`, else null. **E3, car numbers** (owner, 2026-11-12): the number is `roundentry.car_number`; an entry whose number is null, 1000 or more, or already taken in that session gets `driver_number = 1000 + k` instead (k = 1, 2, … in the dump's entry order). The first entry keeps its number and every result is kept. In the smoke below 156 entries got an E3 number (150 shared numbers and 6 null ones). P3g shows no number for 1000 and up.
  - **result, in a race:** `position` (null when `is_classified` is false), `laps` = `laps_completed`, and **`grid`** (new, below). `dnf` is true for status 10 and 11, `dsq` for 20 and `dns` for 30 (null when the status is). Status 40 and 41 (DNQ, DNPQ) are no race result: no row, counted as `dnq`. `durations_ms` is `[time]` and `gaps_ms` is `[time − the winner's time]` (the entry in position 1), so the winner's is `[0]`; without a time, or without a winner time, `gaps_ms` is `{NULL}` and `durations_ms` is `{NULL}` (the string-gap rule above).
  - **result, in a qualifying:** `position` is the position in the last part the entry has one in (A§6.3). `durations_ms` is each part's best lap-row time in part order, and `gaps_ms` is each part's time minus the fastest time of that part, null where the entry has none (a single-session qualifying gives one-element arrays). `laps`, `grid` and the flags are null.
  - **grid:** `result.grid` (`smallint`; null for the archive and the live feed, OpenF1 fills it since #115) is the race's starting grid position from `sessionentry.grid`; Jolpica's 0 means a pit-lane start and is stored as is. For an OpenF1 race a grid failure keeps the stored grid: its raw row stays untouched and the old `grid` values carry over by driver (O1, A§8). The API serves it since #115 (`results[].grid`, A§10).
  - **lap:** races 1996–2017, written through `writeLaps(trx, key, 'jolpica', laps)` (the merge, above): `lap_number` and `lap_ms`, every other value null (`date_start` included, the dump has none). A row without a number is skipped (`no_number`, a fastest-lap marker) and so is a duplicate number (`lap_dup`). `is_pit_out` is true on the lap after each pit stop's lap, false on the session's other laps when the session has pit data, and null without it (every race before 2011). The position per lap is not stored (a P3g follow-up for the lap chart).
  - **pit:** races 2011–2017. `lap_number` comes through `lap_id` to the lap's number (without one the row is skipped, `bad_pit`). `date` is the race's local date plus the stop's local time of day, in the session's zone, converted to UTC (null without time data or a zone); the dump's `local_timestamp` is only a time of day, and all 6,251 stops up to 2017 land 0–4 h after the race start. `pit_ms` and `lane_ms` are both the duration (OpenF1's two durations measure pit entry to exit); `stop_ms` stays null. A second stop on one (driver, lap) is skipped (`pit_dup`).
  - **The write:** per session one transaction. `meeting` and `session` are read `FOR UPDATE`, and a row that exists with another source is left as it is and counted as failed (`not_jolpica`). Otherwise it upserts both, deletes and inserts `driver`, `result` and `pit`, and calls `writeLaps` (jolpica's candidates only; the merge rebuilds `lap`). It is idempotent: a rerun replaces every session. A second run is kept out by the session-level advisory lock `0x46314A4F` (`busy`). Years run 1950 to the current year; none means every year in the dump.
  - **The dump is untrusted input:** each file must be a regular file (`lstat`, no symlink: `not_file`) of at most 64 MiB, a field at most 64 KiB and a record at most 64 columns. The largest file of the 2026-10-27 dump is 37 MB (`lap`), read at a peak of 172 MB RSS (code review, round 1); past the cap the job stops with `bad_dump` / `too_large`, and the cap (`MAX_FILE_BYTES`, `jolpica/csv.ts`) and the service's `mem_limit` (A§14.1) are raised together. A missing file, a missing header column, a bad quote or an unterminated one stops the job as `bad_dump` with the reader's code (`missing`, `not_file`, `unreadable`, `too_large`, `field_cap`, `column_cap`, `bad_quote`, `unterminated`, `bad_record`, `bad_header`) and the file's name, never its content. An unreadable duration is null and counted (`bad_time`), and so is a pit stop's `local_timestamp` other than `HH:MM:SS` (`bad_pit_time`); a round whose season has no year is skipped and counted (`no_year`). A header named `__proto__` is `bad_header`. There is no network code in `jolpica/`.
  - **Logs** are counts and fixed codes only, never a name, time, path or value: `jolpica_start` {years}, one `jolpica` line per session {session_key, year, round, type, drivers, results, laps, pits, renumbered, skipped{dnq, no_number, lap_dup, bad_pit, pit_dup}, merge} and `jolpica_done` {counts, error}. Exit 0 when the dump was read and no session failed, 1 otherwise (`bad_dump`, `busy`, `bad_key`, `not_jolpica`, `db`, and `stale_lap` in the 2018+ tier: refetch or re-archive that session, then rerun), 64 for a usage error, 78 without the database secret.
  - **Migration `20261113000001_jolpica.sql`:** adds `'jolpica'` to the `source` CHECK of the 7 tables the job writes (`meeting`, `session`, `driver`, `lap`, `lap_source`, `pit`, `result`; each dropped and re-added under its catalogue name) and adds `result.grid`. `stint`, `race_control`, `raw_response`, `weather` and `recording` keep their three values, since the job never writes them. **No new grants:** `f1_ingest` already has `INSERT`/`UPDATE` on `meeting` and `session`, `DELETE` on `driver`, `lap`, `pit` and `result`, and `SELECT`/`INSERT`/`DELETE` on `lap_source`. The down migration deletes the `jolpica` rows first (`meeting` cascades; then `lap_source`, `lap`, `driver`, `pit` and `result` rows of that source on other sessions), then drops `grid` and restores the CHECKs; a `lap.filled` value naming jolpica stays until the session's next merge.
  - **`ORDER`:** `['openf1', 'f1-archive', 'f1-live', 'jolpica']` (`merge/merge.ts`); P3f inserts `fia` before `jolpica`.
  - **Rollback:** an image rollback runs no down migration (A§14.4), and P3c's schema change is expand-only. Its data is not: the previous image's merge `rank()` throws on a source outside its `ORDER`, on any session that holds jolpica candidates. So a rollback past P3c after a `jolpica` run first deletes those candidates (`DELETE FROM lap_source WHERE source = 'jolpica'`, runbook `update.md`). The other jolpica rows stay readable for the old image: its ingest touches `openf1` sessions only, and its `fill` leaves a jolpica session unchanged as `stale_lap`. After rolling forward again, run `jolpica` again.
  - **The 2018+ tier** (H6; the rule is built in S4 of P3c): for each Jolpica race from 2018 that has laps, the job finds the one session with `type 'Race'`, name `'Race'`, not cancelled, `source IN ('openf1', 'f1-archive')` and `|date_start − timestamp| ≤ 1 h`; none or several are counted (`no_match`, `ambiguous`) and skipped. It writes laps only, for the driver numbers present in that session's `driver` rows (others are counted, `unknown_driver`): candidates with `lap_number` and `lap_ms`, through `writeLaps(trx, key, 'jolpica', laps)`, so the D3 rules apply (a higher value always stays, a conflict over 1 ms fills nothing, a missing lap is added unless it shows the E6 shift). It never writes `meeting`, `session`, `driver`, `result` or `pit` of those sessions, and a later OpenF1 refetch or `fill` keeps the stored jolpica candidates. Race lap 1 is not special-cased: a difference in its definition shows up as a conflict count, not as a fill. Built in `jolpica/attach.ts`:
    - **Per round:** its first R session by id. A round without one or without laps counts as `no_laps`, and a race without a start as `no_date`.
    - **Candidates:** a lap without a number, or outside 1–32,767, is `no_number`, and a repeated (car, lap) is `lap_dup`.
    - **Per matched session:** one transaction. `fill`'s stale-lap guard runs first, so a session whose `lap` row lacks its own candidate (an image before P3b wrote it) is left unchanged and fails as `stale_lap`.
    - **Logs:** one `jolpica_attach` line per round, and the counts (`attached`, `no_laps`, `no_match`, `ambiguous`, `unknown_driver`, `stale_lap`) in `jolpica_done`.
    - **Not failures:** `no_match` and `ambiguous` are logged at info level. OpenF1 and the archive cover only part of 2018 on (2022 answers 403, 7165 is left out, A§6.2), and a skip writes nothing. Only `stale_lap` and `db` fail the run.
    - **A later run that finds no match** leaves the stored jolpica candidates as they are.
    - **What it reads:** from 2018 on, only race entries and laps.
    - **[box] P3c** (WSL, 2026-11-12; the dev database with the OpenF1 sessions of 2023 to the 2026 Mexican GP and 4 archive sessions; counts only):
      - 87 races attached, 104 `no_match` (2018–2022 rounds with no session in that database), 6 `no_laps` (rounds not yet run), 0 `ambiguous`, 0 `unknown_driver` and 0 `stale_lap`.
      - Against a snapshot taken before the run, 0 values that were set changed, in all 17 columns.
      - 90 `lap_ms` were filled and 449 laps were added: 446 in the 2025 Miami race (10033), where OpenF1 lost about 24 laps per driver, and 3 in 11234.
      - 10 laps were skipped as `dup` (E6): 7 in 9157 and 3 in 11353. Before E6 they showed as duplicates, and 11353's TIME deletion moved onto the added copy.
      - There were 2,626 conflicts. 1,658 are lap 1 (another definition), and most of the rest are 11234, where OpenF1's lap n equals Jolpica's lap n + 1 for every driver (follow-up #140).
      - 11388 keeps its 4 `lap_ms` nulls. Each is a driver's last lap, never finished (a retirement), and Jolpica does not list it.
    - **VM run (A10)** (2026-11-13, the production database with OpenF1 from 2023 and the archive of 2018–2021, the same dump of 2026-10-27; counts only; the evidence is on issue #18):
      - **Counts:** 164 races attached, 27 `no_match`, 6 `no_laps`, 0 `ambiguous`, 0 `unknown_driver` and 0 `stale_lap`.
      - **The 27 `no_match`:**
        - 22 are 2022 (no archive) and 2 are 2018 races missing from the archive Index.
        - 3 are races whose Jolpica start is more than 1 h from OpenF1's: 2024 round 21 (1.5 h, the race was brought forward), 2024 round 22 (Jolpica's date is a day early) and 2026 round 4 (3 h). The dev run skipped the same 3.
      - **No set value changed:** every non-null `lap` value of the OpenF1 and archive races was unpivoted before and after the run (2,798,859 values). None changed or disappeared.
      - **Fills and added laps:** 81 `lap_ms` were filled and 552 laps added, in 9 races:
        - 446 in 10033, as on dev; 2 of them are deleted by race control (track limits);
        - 94 in 5744 (2020): laps 10–14 of 19 drivers, a gap in the archive;
        - 1–3 each in 5074, 5122, 5138, 5162, 5170, 5202 and 5434.
        - None of them equals an OpenF1 or archive neighbour lap within 1 ms. 11234 gets none (3 on dev).
      - **E6 at work:** E6 skipped 16–24 laps as `dup` in each of five 2018 archive races (5074, 5122, 5138, 5162 and 5170) and 1 in 5744. In those five, 17–19 drivers each lack an archive lap 2, and their archive lap 1 holds lap 2's time, so Jolpica's lap 2 shows the shift E6 guards against. This is a fault of the P3a archive import, not of P3c: follow-up #144.
  - **Smoke** (S3, on the real dump of 2026-10-27, a throw-away database; counts only): all years took 33 s at a peak of 439 MB RSS and imported 1,321 sessions (976 races and 345 qualifyings) with 0 failed. It wrote 29,933 drivers and as many results, 426,643 laps and 6,251 pit stops, every count matched an independent `python3 -I` count, and a second run changed no table's md5.
  - **[box] P3c** (WSL, 2026-11-12, the dev database; counts only):
    - All years took 38 s at a peak of 494–506 MB RSS, with 0 failed.
    - An independent stdlib counter over the dump gives the same counts in every year 1950–2017: 976 races, 345 qualifyings, 22,415 race entries, 7,518 qualifying entries, 426,643 race laps and 6,251 pit stops.
    - No 1996–2017 race lacks laps, in the database or in the dump.
    - 10 laps of 3 drivers in the 2017 Abu Dhabi race (120172005) match its FIA lap analysis within 1 ms.
    - A rerun leaves the md5 of `lap` (855 sessions), `result`, `pit`, `driver` and `lap_source` unchanged.
    - The season, race and qualifying views of 1950, 2005 and 2017 render at 390 px with no page error and no horizontal overflow.

**The merge** (P3b, owner decisions D2–D4, 2026-11-12; E6 of P3c refines D3, owner, 2026-11-12; `apps/server/src/merge/`): the laps of a session can come from several sources, and a refetch used to delete every lap of the session, wiping a value filled from another source (gap 1 of #17). So every source keeps its own candidate rows in `lap_source`, and `lap` is rebuilt from them.
  - **One write path:** every lap write goes through `writeLaps(trx, key, source, laps)` (`merge/write.ts`), inside the writer's transaction. It locks the session row (`FOR UPDATE`; a second lock in the same transaction is a no-op), replaces only its own source's rows in `lap_source` (delete, then insert in chunks of 1,000), reads every source's candidates, the session's `race_control` rows and its `type`, merges them (`merge/merge.ts`, pure) and replaces the session's `lap` rows with the result. It returns the merge counts. The OpenF1 ingest (after `race_control`, A§8) and the `archive` job (A§6.2) call it; so does the `fill` job. P3c and P3f write their 2018+ laps through it too (the Jolpica rows below).
  - **Order (D4):** `openf1` > `f1-archive` > `f1-live` > `jolpica`, one array (`ORDER`) for every era: each era has a subset of the sources, so H6's three orders are its subsets. P3c appended `jolpica` with its CHECK migration, and P3f inserts `fia` before it.
  - **Pairing:** laps are paired by (driver_number, lap_number), the `compare-laps.ts` key. The base of a merged row is the highest-ranked candidate that has the lap, and the row's `source` is its source.
  - **Fill (D2):** each lower candidate, in order, fills the base's null values, and `filled[column] = source` for each (the columns are those of `lap_source`). A segment array is filled whole when it is null; a null element inside an array stays null.
  - **Conflict (D3):** a higher value always stays. If any of `lap_ms`, `s1_ms`, `s2_ms` or `s3_ms` that both have differs by more than 1 ms, that source fills nothing on this lap and the lap counts as a conflict for it.
  - **Added laps (D3, E6):** a lap that the higher sources lack entirely is added from the next source that has it (counted per source, `added`). **E6** (owner, 2026-11-12, for every lower source, so P3f's `fia` too): a lower source's lap n is not added when its `lap_ms` is within 1 ms of the same driver's lap n − 1, where that lap's `lap_ms` (its base or its fill) comes from a source ranked higher than this one; the skip is counted per source (`dup`) and leaves no row, fill or diff. Since the review of P3c (round 1) a second condition must hold too: this source's own lap n − 1 is absent, has no `lap_ms`, or differs from the higher lap n − 1's `lap_ms` by more than 1 ms. That is the shift's signature: the higher source's lap n − 1 carries lap n's time, so the lower source, which has the right lap n − 1, disagrees with it there. When the lower source agrees on lap n − 1, its lap n is a genuine repeat and is added. Without the second condition a genuine lap within 1 ms of the previous one at the first lap of a hole was dropped: the review measured 1,014 of 383,115 consecutive lap pairs in the dump's races from 2010 on within 1 ms (0.26 %), about 5 % per session for a 20-driver hole such as 10033; that case is now kept, and the 10 laps of 9157 and 11353 are still skipped. A null on either side never blocks, and a lap n − 1 that came from the same lower source does not either, so a block of added laps keeps two equal consecutive times. The sources are tried in order, so the next one can still add the lap. Why: the P3c [box] attached Jolpica to 87 OpenF1 races, and in 2 (9157, 11353) OpenF1 had put lap n + 1's time on its lap n and lost lap n + 1 (a live-ingest hole), so D3 added Jolpica's lap n + 1 and the same lap showed twice, 10 laps in all; in 11353 the TIME deletion moved to the added copy. In 10033 (Miami 2025) Jolpica still adds the 446 laps of a real 24-lap OpenF1 hole.
  - **Differences (H6):** every value both candidates have that is not equal is counted per source and field: a time within 1 ms, `date_start` within 1 s (`compare-laps.ts` rules), everything else exactly. The counts (`rows`, `added`, `dup`, `filled`, `diffs`, `conflicts` and the A§9 `rules`) are the `merge` field of the writer's log line (`session` for the ingest, `archive`, `fill`): counts only, never a value.
  - **After the merge:** `markPitIn` runs on the merged rows (an added lap n+1 with `is_pit_out` makes lap n an in-lap), then `markLaps` on the session's `race_control` and `type` (A§9), so a filled lap time can match a TIME ruling. `race_control` is read with `date` as an ISO string (pg returns a `Date`, whose `Date.parse` drops the ms) and `ORDER BY id` (ties go by input order), which gives a single-source session exactly the deletions it had. Then each row keeps its merged per-value `quali_phase` (the archive's phases use SessionData boundaries that are not in `race_control`), and only where that is null does it take the phase `markLaps` computed, so a recording-only lap (`createLaps` gives no phase) or a lap whose `date_start` another source filled gets one. So a single-source session equals its rows, except a lap whose own phase is null, which takes race control's (remote for the archive: a qualifying whose first stored phase row is Q2).
  - **Recordings (D4, #95):** only a session's `complete` recording is a candidate (`f1-live`), the earliest by `started_at` when there are several, found through the `recording` table. So a recorder that started mid-session cannot add a phantom lap 1. Incomplete recordings can join after #95.
  - **`fill`:** the job builds the `f1-archive` candidates from `<archive-dir>/<session_key>/` (the P3a parser) and the `f1-live` candidates from the complete recording, for `source = 'openf1'` sessions only, and keeps the stored candidates of a source whose files are absent (an archive without SessionInfo, TimingData or a t0, `incomplete`, counts as absent). It reads and parses the files before the transaction opens, so no lock is held while parsing. Of the archive it reads only the archive job's topics, each a regular file (a symlink is `not_file`), and refuses them over `MAX_SESSION_BYTES` together, as it does a recording's kept lines: `too_large`. An archive on the Heartbeat t0 is skipped (`t0_heartbeat`: its times can be minutes off, A§6.2). A complete recording must have its directory (`recording_missing`), a sealed segment and SessionInfo (`incomplete`); of it only the lines of the archive job's topics are kept and counted. A failing source (`too_large`, `key_mismatch`, `t0_heartbeat`, `not_file`, `recording_missing`, `incomplete` for a recording) keeps its stored candidates and is logged under `errors` in the session's `fill` line; the other source is still stored, the session still merged, and it counts as failed. It then merges every session it is given again, each in its own locked transaction (`staleLap`, `storeCandidates`, then `mergeSession` once, in `merge/write.ts`). Before it stores anything, a session in which any `lap` row has no `lap_source` row of the same (session_key, driver_number, lap_number, source) (an image before P3b wrote `lap` only) is left unchanged and fails as `stale_lap`, files or not. After any merge every `lap` row's base candidate exists, so the guard has no false positive; it cannot see a candidate that is present but stale (a session the old image refetched), so after an image rollback refetch (or re-archive) a session before `fill`. The guard is `fill`'s only: a writer's merge always replaces `lap`, so a writer with no laps and no other candidates deletes the session's laps. `--all` runs newest `date_end` first and skips the `source = 'jolpica'` sessions (P3c): their laps have one source and no files, so a re-merge could change nothing (an explicit key is still merged).
  - **Not merged:** `stint`, `pit` and `result` stay each writer's own (P3b is per lap). The API is unchanged: #132 exposes `filled`.
  - **[box] P3b PR 1** (WSL, 2026-11-12; the local PostgreSQL 18 dev database with 495 OpenF1 and 4 archive sessions; counts only):
    - **Migration:** 2.0 s. It copied all 262,292 laps into `lap_source` and left every `lap` row unchanged.
    - **Identity:** `fill --all` without files (a pure re-merge) took 14 s and peaked at 278 MB RSS, with 499 sessions and 0 failed.
      - The 7 sessions whose stored A§9 rules were current are unchanged, both OpenF1 sessions and the archive imports.
      - 260 OpenF1 sessions changed. They had been ingested before P1c and still held its defaults (A§7). The merge applied the A§9 rules to them: 3,358 laps newly deleted and 33,078 phases from null to set. No deletion was undone, no existing phase changed, and no other column moved.
      - A refetch of one of them (9090, Qualifying, 420 laps) gives exactly the merged rows. On the VM every session was fetched again after P1c (the P5a re-queue, A§8), so a `fill --all` there should change nothing.
    - **Archive fills** (`fill 11388 11404 11392 11397 --archive-dir …/fixtures-private/archive`): 3.2 s, peak 401 MB RSS. The `—` cells counted are `lap_ms`, `s1_ms`–`s3_ms`, `i1_kph`, `i2_kph` and `st_kph`.
      - **11404** (Mexico race), 427 → 401: 26 values filled on 13 laps where OpenF1 lost about 40 s live (A§11.3): S2 11, I2 11, S1 2 and I1 2. There were 0 conflicts and 0 added laps; the only differences are 47 segment arrays. The fills survive a refetch.
      - **11392 and 11397:** 0 fills, 0 conflicts and 0 differences, as compare-laps found (A§11.3).
      - **11388** (Singapore race): 575 → 575. Every empty cell is empty in the archive too (I1 304 and ST 256 of them), because our parser follows OpenF1's rules on the same feed.

        The counts suggest the feed does not resend an unchanged trap speed. Of 751 pairs of consecutive laps that both have an I1 speed, only 1 is equal, while 25 % of laps have none. I2 has 292 equal pairs and 2 nulls, though, so this is **[U]**, a follow-up issue. The Singapore [box] moved to P3b PR 2 (owner decision, 2026-11-12).
    - **The `f1-live` tier** (the round-2 review run, 2026-11-12, read-only, counts only): the Mexico race 11404 recording gives 1,369 laps against the 1,369 rows, with 0 added, 0 conflicts, 0 fills and 4 `date_start` differences, at a peak of 313 MB RSS.

Built in P1b: `db/migrations/20261026000001_timing_tables.sql`. Every child table references `session` and `session` references `meeting` (`ON DELETE CASCADE`), except `recording` (P2c), which is `ON DELETE RESTRICT`, so a session with a recording cannot be deleted; nothing references `driver`, because OpenF1 can list a lap for a driver missing from `drivers`. The ingest fills `deleted`, `deleted_reason` and `quali_phase` since P1c (A§8, A§9); a session ingested before P1c keeps the defaults until it is fetched again.

Roles (owner, 2026-10-25; built in P1b): `deploy/postgres/roles.sql`, a PostgreSQL init script that runs once when the volume is created, makes the logins `f1_migrator` (owns every object), `f1_ingest` (writes) and `f1_api` (reads). Each login's password is a file secret `db_<login>`.

- It is plain SQL: a `DO` block reads each password on the server with `pg_read_file(prefix || login)`, where the prefix is the setting `f1.secret_prefix` or else `/run/secrets/db_`. In production the password never appears in SQL text or a statement log, and an error names only the login and the SQLSTATE. It is idempotent (create if missing, then `ALTER ROLE … NOSUPERUSER NOCREATEDB NOCREATEROLE NOREPLICATION NOBYPASSRLS`), makes `f1_migrator` the database owner, revokes every database privilege from `PUBLIC` (and `CONNECT` on `postgres` and `template1`) and grants `CONNECT` to `f1_ingest` and `f1_api`. No `CONNECTION LIMIT`: each process's pool is capped in code (2 connections).
- **One database per cluster** is a premise: roles and the `CONNECT` revokes are cluster-wide, and a database created later keeps `PUBLIC` `CONNECT`, so "no login reaches another database" holds only while `f1` is the only one (P1f can add a `pg_hba` guard).
- The `db` container must be able to read `/run/secrets/db_f1_*`: it needs the secrets' GID (`group_add`, P1f). After rotating a `db_*` secret, run `roles.sql` again by hand; it runs by itself only on a new volume.
- Grants live in `20261026000002_grants.sql`: `f1_ingest` gets `SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE` on `meeting` and `session` and also `DELETE` on the seven child tables it replaces (a `DELETE` on `meeting` or `session` would cascade through every timing table); `f1_api` gets `SELECT` on all but `raw_response`. Later phases grant their own tables (no default privileges): `20261030000001_recording.sql` (P2c) creates `recording` and `recorder_status` and grants `f1_ingest` `SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE` on both and `f1_api` `SELECT`. The `live` role logs in as `f1_ingest` (owner, 2026-10-30), the second consumer of `db_f1_ingest` after `ingest`; there is no fourth login and `roles.sql` is unchanged. `20261109000001_weather.sql` (P5a, owner, 2026-11-07) creates `weather` (`ON DELETE CASCADE` from `session`, like every child table), adds `weather` to `raw_response`'s endpoint CHECK, grants `f1_ingest` `SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE` and `f1_api` `SELECT` on it (nothing new on `meeting` or `session`), and re-queues the final sessions (A§8). Its down migration deletes the weather raw rows first, so the old CHECK can come back; an image rollback runs no down migration, and the previous image works on the new schema (the CHECK is a superset). `20261113000002_ingest_status.sql` (#105) creates `ingest_status` and grants `f1_ingest` `SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE` and `f1_api` `SELECT`; the old image ignores the table.
- The rain tables (P5c, `20261109000002_rain.sql`; owner, 2026-11-07): `f1_ingest` gets `SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE` on `rain_grid` (no `DELETE`: a grid goes only by the cascade from its session, which runs as the table owner) and `SELECT, INSERT, DELETE` on `rain_frame` (the job replaces a grid's frames and never updates one), `f1_api` gets `SELECT` on both; the down migration revokes and drops. A frame cascades from `session` through its grid and can never exist without one (C2); a CHECK cannot read another table, so `rain_grid` holds `size = 7` and `rain_frame` `cardinality(mm) = 49`: another grid size is a new migration. `scripts/rain-facts.sql` is the [box] check (A§8).
- The lap source table (P3b, `20261112000001_lap_source.sql`; owner decision D2, 2026-11-12): creates `lap_source`, adds `lap.filled` (`jsonb NOT NULL DEFAULT '{}'`, CHECK `jsonb_typeof(filled) = 'object'`) and copies every existing `lap` row into `lap_source` as the candidate of its own source, so the first merge of an old session keeps its rows. `f1_ingest` gets `SELECT, INSERT, DELETE` on `lap_source` (no `UPDATE`: a writer replaces its rows and never updates one), `f1_api` gets nothing (the API reads `lap`). The down migration revokes, drops `lap.filled` and drops `lap_source`. An image rollback runs no down migration, and the previous image works on the new schema: `filled` has a default and the new table goes unread.
- The Jolpica migration (P3c, `20261113000001_jolpica.sql`; owner decision, 2026-11-12): no new grants. It adds `'jolpica'` to the `source` CHECK of the 7 tables the `jolpica` job writes and `result.grid`, and `f1_ingest` already holds every privilege the job needs (the Jolpica rows in A§7). The down migration deletes the `jolpica` rows, drops `grid` and restores the CHECKs. An image rollback past P3c runs no down migration either; after a `jolpica` run it first deletes the jolpica lap candidates, because the previous image's merge throws on the new source (A§7, "Rollback").
- The starting grid migration (#115, `20261114000001_starting_grid.sql`; O1 owner, 2026-11-13): widens `raw_response`'s endpoint CHECK by `starting_grid` (dropped and re-added under its catalogue name) and adds **no grant**: `f1_ingest` already writes `result` and `raw_response`, and `result.grid` exists since P3c. It re-queues the final OpenF1 races and sprints (A§8). The down migration deletes the grid raw rows and sets OpenF1's `result.grid` to null (a Jolpica grid stays), then restores the CHECK.
- Development and tests: `apps/server/scripts/dev-db.ts` writes a random password per login on the server with `COPY … TO` (so in development the password does appear in that statement's text), sets `f1.secret_prefix`, runs `roles.sql` and empties the files again. `apps/server/test/db.ts` (`createTestDb()`) does this for each integration test file.

## 8. Ingestion (OpenF1)

- **Schedule sync:** daily at 00:30 UTC, plus at start: `meetings` and `sessions` for the current year and every year from 2023 that has no `meeting` row yet (all of them on the first run). Upserts `meeting`/`session`, metadata only. A new session is `scheduled` with `next_fetch_at = date_end + 35 min` (none when cancelled); a changed `date_end` moves `next_fetch_at` only while the session is still `scheduled`. A session whose meeting is missing from `meetings` is skipped and counted. The raw schedule responses are not stored.
  - The daemon skips the sync during a live window (A§6.1). A skipped or failed sync is tried again from the minute job at most every 15 min until it succeeds.
  - One response is one parse and one transaction: a single bad row (a missing date, a value the schema rejects) fails that year's sync, which keeps the previous schedule and shows up as `schema` or `db` in the log.
- **Per-session pipeline** (P1b, `apps/server/src/ingest/`; the rules are pure functions in `state.ts`):
  - Every minute the tick marks `scheduled`/`fetched` sessions with `next_fetch_at ≤ now` as `due`, then processes the due ones one at a time, newest `date_end` first. Cancelled sessions are never due. It waits while any session is live (A§6.1) and logs the wait when it starts and when it ends.
  - **Success at t** (after the 35 min wait, i.e. after the paid window): `fetch_count + 1`, `fail_count = 0`, `last_error = null`, then the first of `date_end + 3 h` and `date_end + 24 h` that is later than t → `fetched` with that `next_fetch_at`, or else `final`. A session older than 24 h is fetched once, so the backfill costs 7 requests per session (6 before P5a; 8 for a race or sprint, whose grid is the eighth since #115). A success before `date_end + 35 min` (only a `refetch` gets there) leaves the session `scheduled` for its first fetch.
  - **Failure n** (consecutive): `due` again at t + min(5 min · 2^(n−1), 6 h), that is 5, 10, 20, 40, 80, 160, 320 and 360 min; the 9th → `failed` (shown in the status panel, P1c). `last_error` holds the code: HTTP 429 → `rate_limited`, any other status → `http_<status>`, `timeout`, `network`, a body that is not JSON or does not match the zod schema → `schema`, a PostgreSQL error → `db`, anything else (a bug in the normaliser) → `unknown`.
  - **One transaction:** all seven endpoints (`drivers`, `laps`, `stints`, `pit`, `race_control`, `session_result` and, since P5a, `weather`) are fetched first, and any fetch or schema error writes nothing but the failure: a weather fetch or schema failure fails the session's fetch like any other endpoint (owner, 2026-11-07, P5a). Then one transaction locks the session row (`FOR UPDATE`), decides the transition from that locked row, replaces the seven `raw_response` rows and all the session's child rows (delete, then insert in chunks of 1,000 rows), and sets the state. Replace-per-session is idempotent and drops rows OpenF1 removed; the lock serialises a `refetch` against the daemon. A race also fetches its starting grid, after the seven, which fails on its own (O1, below). The laps are the exception (P3b, A§7): they are written through `writeLaps` in the same transaction, after `race_control`. A refetch replaces only OpenF1's candidates in `lap_source` and rebuilds `lap`, so the fills from the archive and a recording come back with it.
  - **The starting grid** (#115; O1 owner, 2026-11-13; `processSession` in `ingest.ts`, `withGrid` in `normalise.ts`):
    - **Which grid:** only for `type = 'Race'`. A race named `Race` takes its meeting's not cancelled session named `Qualifying`; a race named `Sprint` takes `Sprint Qualifying` or `Sprint Shootout` (2023's name). Exactly one such session, else no request (`no_quali`). The grid is linked to the qualifying session (A§6.1).
    - **Fetch:** after the seven, `starting_grid?session_key=<the qualifying's key>`. It is not one of the seven: the main empty guard ignores it.
    - **O1, an exception to all-or-nothing:** a grid problem never fails the session. The other seven commit as usual (state, counters, every table replaced), and the stored grid survives. Inside the transaction, after the lock and before the replace delete, ingest reads the session's old `result.grid` by driver number and whether a `starting_grid` raw row is stored, with its `jsonb_array_length(body)` only (never the body). A grid is **stored** when a result has a non-null `grid` or the raw row is not empty.
    - **Keep** (the stored grid survives) on a grid fetch failure (its code), a schema failure (`schema`), a fresh answer with no usable row while a grid is stored (the grid's own empty guard, `empty`), `no_quali` while a grid is stored (`no_quali`), and a fresh raw row PostgreSQL refuses (`schema`, below). A keep deletes only the other seven raw rows (`endpoint <> 'starting_grid'`), so the stored grid raw row stays untouched (`fetched_at`, status and body), and each new result row takes its old `grid` by driver number; a driver new to the results gets null. The stored body is never read, so one the schema no longer accepts, or a changed qualifying key, keeps the grids too.
    - **Usable rows:** the grid rows whose `session_key` is the qualifying's and whose `position` is not null, after the duplicate rule (below).
    - **Fresh** (otherwise): the new raw row (`fetched_at` from the app clock, its HTTP status and body) replaces the stored one inside a savepoint (`SAVEPOINT grid`; on an error `ROLLBACK TO SAVEPOINT grid`, then the keep path with `schema`). Why the savepoint: a NUL (`\u0000`) or a lone surrogate in a field the schema strips passes the parse, but `jsonb` refuses it, and without the savepoint that failed the whole session (`db`). Then `result.grid` comes from its rows (below). A fresh answer with no usable row and nothing stored is still fresh: its raw row is stored and every `grid` is null.
    - **Size cap:** the schema takes at most 100 rows (`z.array(StartingGrid).max(100)`), so an over-long answer is a grid `schema` failure on its own.
    - **Requests:** `client.get` retries a 5xx, a 429, a timeout or a network error up to 5 times, so a hard-down grid endpoint costs up to 5 requests per race (the shared limiter still holds 25 a minute).
    - Why: the endpoint is beta and scraped from formula1.com, "currently linked to the qualifying sessions" (br-g/openf1#356), and the `pit` removal (below) showed what a vanished endpoint does under all-or-nothing: 72 sessions stuck on `empty`.
    - **The rows:** `result.grid` is set by driver number from the grid rows whose `session_key` is the qualifying's; another session's row, a duplicate (the last wins) or a driver without a result row is skipped, and a result without a grid row keeps null.
    - **No grid raw row** for a session that is not a race, or `no_quali` with nothing stored: the replace delete removes an old one, and its results' `grid` is null. A session that is not a race gets no `grid` field in its log line.
  - **Raw is stored in that same transaction, not first** (a P1b decision, against the PHASES wording "store raw → normalise"): the empty guard compares a new reply with the stored raw, so storing a transient `[]` first would replace the good raw and the next normalise would wipe good rows. The cost: a reply that fails the schema is not kept (only its `schema` code is). Since #103 `rederive` reads the stored `raw_response` rows back, so "store raw, derive later" (A§2, principle 3) is built.
  - **Empty guard:** an endpoint that answers `[]` where its stored `raw_response` had rows is a failure with code `empty`, and the stored data stays.
  - **Timing rules (P1c):** the normaliser applies them (deleted laps, `quali_phase`, A§9) to the lap rows before the insert, so the same transaction writes them with the rows and replace-per-session never wipes them. Since P3b the merge runs `markPitIn` and `markLaps` again on the merged rows (A§7). It takes the session's `type` from the `session` row.
  - The app clock is injected: no state decision uses SQL `now()`, so unit and integration tests run on a fake clock.
  - **In/out laps:** `is_pit_out` is OpenF1's `is_pit_out_lap`; `is_pit_in` is derived (A§9). Seconds become integer ms (rounded), and nulls stay null.
  - One log line per session: key, new state, rows per table (`weather` among them since P5a), skipped rows (another session's rows, or duplicate keys: the last wins; a weather row is keyed on its instant, so one instant in two spellings is one key), the timing rules' message counts (`rules`: `deleted`, `reinstated`, `unmatched`, `unparsed`), the merge counts (`merge`, A§7), for a Race session `rc_unmatched` (the period-like race-control rows that match no form, A§6.1; #105), `grid` (#115: `{rows, skipped}` on success, `{error, kept}` on a keep with its fixed code and the number of non-null grids carried over, plus zod's `issues` (paths and codes, at most 10) on a schema failure of the answer, `'no_quali'` with nothing stored, absent outside races), duration and `last_error`; a failure adds the endpoint and, for `schema`, zod's paths and codes (at most 10), and for `empty` (#103) `endpoints`, the names of every endpoint that answered `[]` where rows were stored (fixed names), which tells `accept-empty` which one to take. Never a value, a URL, a password or a response body.
- **Rate budget:** at most 25 requests in any 60 s and 2 in any 1 s (sliding windows, shared by every call of the client), below the 30/3 limit. On a 429, honour `Retry-After` or wait 60 s. Each attempt refuses redirects, ends after 120 s in all (headers and body: `timeout`) and stops reading past 64 MiB (`schema`, not retried); a race's `laps` is about 1 MB. 7 requests per session (P5a; a session took 3.2–3.6 s in the local re-queue run below), 8 for a race or sprint (#115). Free requests fail with 401 during any live session (A§6.1), so the sync, the tick, the backfill and a refetch wait for the live window to end.
- **First backfill** (`backfill` role): syncs every year from 2023, then processes every session that is neither `final` nor cancelled and ended more than 35 min ago, newest first, each once per run, with the same transitions as the tick. Exit 0 when nothing failed, else 1. A live window stops it (exit 1); run it again later. ~470 × 6 ≈ 2,800 requests ≈ **1 h 55 min** (measured 2026-10-26: 1 h 54 min 44 s over two runs, see the [box] backfill below); since P5a ~470 × 7 ≈ 3,300 requests ≈ **2 h 15 min** at 25 a minute (an estimate). It's resumable, because state lives in the DB. It refuses while the `ingest` daemon runs (the ingest lock, below).
  - **The ingest lock** (owner, 2026-11-14, D1, #103; `ingestLock` in `ingest.ts`): the daemon takes the session-level advisory lock `0x4631494e` ('F1IN') on a `pg.Client` of its own, outside the pool, and holds it while it runs. `backfill` takes the same lock in its own connection and, when it cannot, logs `{error: 'daemon_running'}` and exits 1 with no request made: the daemon, or another backfill, holds it. It replaces the old rule in prose, "don't run it next to a running `ingest` daemon: each process has its own rate limiter".
    - **Re-taken every minute:** a daemon that does not hold the lock tries again each minute, so a database restart or a dropped connection loses it for at most a minute. While it does not hold it, the minute job skips the schedule sync and the OpenF1 tick; the rain step keeps running (it makes no OpenF1 call, and its heartbeat keeps the container healthy). `ingest_status.last_tick_at` goes stale meanwhile, and `/status` shows that (A§10). The log has `ingest lock {held}` on a change only, at error level when not held.
    - **`refetch` is allowed next to the daemon:** it takes no lock. The session row lock serialises it against the daemon's own fetch (above), but it has its own rate limiter, so run a long batch outside busy hours. `rederive`, `rain-refetch` and `accept-empty` take no lock either.
- **[box] backfill** (WSL, home IP, a throw-away PostgreSQL 18). The superuser login is used because the grants are proven in CI (handoff §12):

  ```
  export DATABASE_URL='postgres://postgres@127.0.0.1:5439/f1?sslmode=disable'
  dbmate create
  node apps/server/scripts/dev-db.ts
  node apps/server/src/main.ts migrate
  date -u; time node apps/server/src/main.ts backfill > backfill.log 2>&1; echo "exit $?"; date -u
  psql -h 127.0.0.1 -p 5439 -U postgres -d f1 \
    -c "SELECT pg_size_pretty(pg_database_size('f1')), pg_size_pretty(pg_total_relation_size('raw_response'))" \
    -c "SELECT relname, pg_size_pretty(pg_total_relation_size(relid)) FROM pg_stat_user_tables ORDER BY pg_total_relation_size(relid) DESC" \
    -c "SELECT ingest_state, count(*) FROM session GROUP BY 1"
  ```

  Measured 2026-10-26: run 1 (8ade505, before the real-data schema fix) 1 h 45 min 41 s, 455 sessions, 36 `schema` failures, exit 1; run 2 on the merged code (6770997) 9 min 3 s, the 37 remaining sessions, 0 failures, exit 0. Together 1 h 54 min 44 s. End state: 454 `final`, 1 `fetched` (the latest race, waiting for end + 24 h), 40 `scheduled` (25 future, 15 cancelled). Database 102 MB (A§7).
- **The P5a re-queue** (owner, 2026-11-07; `20261109000001_weather.sql`): the migration sets every `final`, not cancelled session back to `fetched` with `next_fetch_at = date_end + 35 min`, long past, so the running daemon's next tick marks them all `due` and fetches each once more with its seven endpoints, newest first: about 455 sessions × 7 requests ≈ 2 h 15 min, paused during live windows like any tick. No SQL `now()`: the tick's clock decides, and each becomes `final` again by the success rule above. Scheduled, due, fetched, failed and cancelled sessions are untouched, so a `failed` session gets weather only through a `refetch`. The empty guard still applies: an endpoint that now answers `[]` where rows were stored fails with `empty` and keeps the rows. Any failure of a re-queued session (a 429, a timeout, a weather `schema` error, `empty`) takes the normal backoff, and the 9th in a row makes it `failed` although it holds complete data: the rows stay, but the API then serves them `no-store`, `/status` lists the session, and a `refetch` recovers it (P5a code review). Until its turn, a re-queued session's Weather tab says "No weather in the OpenF1 data." and its head "data provisional" (amended, #99, owner 2026-11-13: after a fetch that succeeded an empty tab names the source, and the head says the data may still change, A§13). The down migration does not undo it. The [box] checks once it has drained: `/status` shows no extra `fetched`, and its `failed` list is the one from before the deploy. Locally (2026-11-09, a throw-away PostgreSQL 18 with 2024 and 2026 synced and 9636, 11388 and 11392 refetched): after a rollback and `migrate` the three were `fetched` at end + 35 min, and one tick fetched them back to `final` with 201, 160 and 77 weather rows, about 3.3 s each.
  - **On the VM** (owner, 2026-11-09):
    - The drain ran at about 3.5 sessions a minute: 7 requests each within the 25-a-minute budget. The last 60 sessions took 16 min.
    - **72 sessions failed with `empty`:** 2026 practice, qualifying, sprint qualifying and testing up to the Austin weekend. OpenF1 had removed their `pit` rows (A§6.1). A `refetch` cannot clear this, because it applies the same guard.
    - **The fix:** the owner accepted the removal and cleared the guard by deleting the stored `pit` responses of the sessions failing with `empty` that are not races. It printed `DELETE 72`, and each session's next tick made it `final` with weather and no pit rows:

      ```
      DELETE FROM raw_response r USING session s
      WHERE r.session_key = s.session_key AND r.endpoint = 'pit' AND jsonb_array_length(r.body) > 0
        AND s.last_error = 'empty' AND s.fail_count > 0
        AND s.type IS DISTINCT FROM 'Race' AND NOT s.is_cancelled
      ```

    - **End state:** after the drain, 464 sessions are `final` and none `due`, and the failed list is empty, as it was before the deploy.
  - **When OpenF1 removes an endpoint's rows for good**, this is the remedy: delete that endpoint's stored raw response for the sessions failing with `empty`; it serves only the guard. Run the same filter as a SELECT first. The next fetch then takes OpenF1's answer. This accepts the loss of those rows, so it is the owner's call each time. Since #103 `accept-empty <session_key> <endpoint>` does this per session and checks OpenF1 first (Recovery tools, below); the DELETE above is the superseded hand form.
- **The #115 re-queue** (O1 owner, 2026-11-13; `20261114000001_starting_grid.sql`): the P5a pattern for the final, not cancelled OpenF1 `Race`-type sessions only (races and sprints): about 113 sessions × 8 requests ≈ 900 requests ≈ **36 min** at 25 a minute, paused during live windows; a hard-down grid endpoint costs up to 5 requests per race (the retries, above), so the drain may take longer, while the shared limiter still holds the 25-a-minute budget. No SQL `now()`; each becomes `final` again by the success rule. A grid failure does not fail a re-queued session (O1), so the drain cannot strand sessions on the grid as P5a's did on `pit`; any other failure takes the normal backoff. The down migration does not undo it.
  - **Rollback:** an image rollback runs no down migration, and the previous image works on the new schema: the CHECK is a superset, and its next fetch of a race drops the grid raw row and nulls the grid (its replace deletes every raw row and writes no grid).
  - **If OpenF1 drops the grid for good,** each fetch logs `grid: {error: 'empty', kept: n}` and keeps the stored grid; deleting the session's `starting_grid` raw row and setting its `result.grid` to null (the remedy above, plus the grids, since both count as stored) makes the next fetch take OpenF1's answer.
- **OpenF1 sessions only** (P3a, owner decision O2, 2026-11-10): the OpenF1 jobs touch sessions with `source = 'openf1'` only. `processSession`, which every caller goes through, reads `source` with its first query and answers `not_openf1` for any other, changing nothing: OpenF1's `[]` would otherwise wipe archive rows, because the empty guard keys on `raw_response` and archive sessions have none. The tick's due marking and selection and the backfill's query add `source = 'openf1'`, so a later migration that re-queues finals (as P5a's did) never makes an archive session due, stuck or failed. The `fill` job (P3b) follows the same rule: it reads local files for `source = 'openf1'` sessions only (A§7). The rain job's backfill and live grids (`gridless`) do too: archive sessions get no rain grid and spend no Open-Meteo budget (the Historical host starts in 2022); rain for old seasons is a follow-up. The schedule sync covers 2023 onwards only, and its upserts update a meeting or session row only while its `source` is `openf1` (`ON CONFLICT … DO UPDATE … WHERE source = 'openf1'`), so an OpenF1 key that collides with an archive Key never takes over the archive row.
- **Recovery tools** (owner, 2026-11-14, #103; `apps/server/src/ingest/recovery.ts`): four jobs for the cases above. They log in as `f1_ingest` and need no migration (the existing grants cover them). A per-session failure never throws: it is logged with a fixed code, the run goes on and the exit is 1. Exit codes: 0 ok, 1 on a failure or a refusal, 64 a usage error, 78 without the database secret. Decisions: D1 the ingest lock (above), D2 `rederive` keeps `result.grid`, D3 the code review on Sonnet xhigh and a security review on Opus high, D4 `accept-empty` checks OpenF1 first (all owner, 2026-11-14). Runbook: `docs/runbooks/ingest.md`.
  - **`refetch <session_key> | --failed | --meeting <meeting_key>`:** the session pipeline's `processSession` with the live-window refusal, on one client, so one rate limiter serves the whole run. `--failed` takes the `failed`, not cancelled OpenF1 sessions; `--meeting` takes the meeting's not cancelled OpenF1 sessions that ended more than 35 min ago (the backfill filter); both newest `date_end` first. A live window stops the batch at the first session (`live_session`). It logs `refetch_done {sessions, done, failed}`. `--meeting` with no session logs `no_sessions` and exits 1; `--failed` with none exits 0. Per-session codes are the `last_error` codes above, `unknown_session`, `not_openf1` and `live_session`.
  - **`rederive <session_key>… | --all`:** per session one transaction: it locks the session row, reads its stored raw rows inside the lock, parses them (`schema` writes nothing), runs the normaliser and the timing rules (A§9) and replaces the child rows as the fetch does (including `writeLaps`, A§7). State, counters and `raw_response` stay as they are, no request is made and the empty guard does not apply. `result.grid` is carried over by driver number and the grid raw row is never read (D2, the keep path above). `--all` takes every OpenF1 session, newest first, and touches the heartbeat after each, because a run container has the node healthcheck. A session with no raw rows is skipped (`no_raw`), one with fewer than the seven endpoints too (`incomplete`); a skip is no failure. Failures: `unknown_session`, `not_openf1`, `schema` (with the endpoint and zod's paths and codes), or a thrown error's code (a SQLSTATE, else `unknown`). It logs one `rederive` line per session (rows, skipped, `rules`, `merge`, `ms`) and `rederive_done {sessions, rederived, skipped, failed, ms}`. Never a value.
    - **[box] #103** (VM, 2026-11-14, after #164 was deployed; the production database; counts only; the evidence is on issue #103): `rederive --all` took 1 min 16 s (`ms` 73,625) with 495 sessions, 465 rederived, 30 skipped and 0 failed.
      - **The 30 skipped** are all `no_raw`: the 15 scheduled and the 15 cancelled sessions. None was `incomplete`, so every fetched session holds all seven raw rows (the P5a and #115 re-queues).
      - **Spot check:** the md5 of `driver`, `lap`, `lap_source`, `stint`, `pit`, `race_control` (without `id`), `result` and `weather` over the latest final OpenF1 Race, Qualifying and Sprint was identical before and after. Today's code derives the stored rows again exactly.
  - **`accept-empty <session_key> <endpoint>`:** for a session failing with `empty` (D4). It checks the session (`unknown_session`, `not_openf1`), refuses in a live window (`live_session`), refuses when the stored row has no rows (`no_rows`, before any request), makes one OpenF1 request for that endpoint and refuses unless OpenF1 answers `[]` now (`not_empty`, or the fetch's code). Then one transaction locks the session and deletes that one `(session_key, endpoint)` raw row, nothing else, only if it is still the row that was checked (the same `fetched_at`); a row replaced meanwhile, or gone, is `changed` or `no_rows` and nothing is deleted. A thrown error is logged as its code (a SQLSTATE, else `unknown`). The next fetch takes OpenF1's answer: the daemon's tick, or `refetch` for a `failed` or `final` session. It logs `accept_empty {session_key, endpoint, rows}` with the number of deleted rows. The endpoint is one of the seven, not `starting_grid`.
  - **`rain-refetch <session_key>`:** one `UPDATE rain_grid SET state = 'live', fail_count = 0, last_error = null, next_fetch_at = now, updated_at = now WHERE state = 'failed'`. Codes: `no_grid`, `not_failed` (nothing changes). It logs `rain_refetch {session_key}`. The daemon's next rain step fetches the grid as a due one (Historical when past `date_end` + 24 h) within a minute or so. The budget's restart seed may over-count that grid, which is conservative.
- **Manual:** `refetch <session_key>` (digits only, else exit 64; unknown key → exit 1; an archive session's key → exit 1, `not_openf1`) for OpenF1 re-ingests (as in #483). It processes the session now: a success makes the normal transition, a failure changes nothing (a `final` session stays `final`) and exits 1. During a live window it writes nothing and exits 1 (it does not wait: a manual command should not hang for hours).
- **Scheduling pattern:** in-process croner (UTC). One job runs every minute inside a busy guard: it syncs the schedule when due (at start, after `30 0 * * *` asks for it, or 15 min after a failed try), then ticks. A minute that fires while the job runs is remembered, and the job runs once more when it ends (missed ticks run once, the Waterheight pattern). SIGTERM/SIGINT stops the cron, lets the current session finish, closes the pool and exits 0.
- **Heartbeat:** the work touches it at start, as each OpenF1 attempt starts (after the rate limiter), after each call, after each session and at the end of each tick (a waiting one too). One attempt lasts at most 120 s (the client's deadline), within the 120 s limit (A§4), so slow but progressing work never goes stale. Staleness means an attempt that ran into its deadline, a 429 `Retry-After` over 2 min, or a database wait past its timeouts; then `f1-tick` restarts the container.
- **Database waits are bounded:** connect 10 s, a statement 60 s on the server and 70 s on the client, TCP keepalive on, so a half-open connection cannot hang the job and its busy guard.
- **Failure pings and the status row** (owner, 2026-11-13, #105; `ingestHealth` in `ingest.ts`):
  - **`f1-ingest/fail`** gets a POST whose body is a fixed code, never a key, a URL or a value (A§14.5): `session_failed <code>` (the `last_error` codes above) when a session newly reaches `failed`, `rain_failed <code>` (the rain codes below) when a grid does, and `due_backlog` when a session with `ingest_state = 'due'`, `source = 'openf1'`, not cancelled, has waited over 6 h (`next_fetch_at ≤ now − 6 h`), checked outside a live window only. The backlog pings on the rising edge only and a live window leaves its latch as it is. Every good sync re-arms it (D3), so a standing backlog alerts at most once a day, and the next good daily sync pings `f1-ingest` as before. A re-queue migration like P5a's sets `next_fetch_at` far in the past, so it trips `due_backlog` once.
  - **Only the daemon pings:** `ping` is in the `ingest` role's deps only, so `backfill` and `refetch` never do. A failed ping is logged as a code and the work goes on; no key means no request.
  - **The `ingest_status` row** (A§7) is upserted after every processed session and at the end of every tick, so a long catch-up tick keeps it fresh, and `last_sync_at` is written by a good sync only (an upsert from a tick keeps the stored value). A failed write is logged (`report failed`, a code) and never fatal; if the sync or the tick throws, there is no write and `/status` shows the row stale after 5 min (A§10).
- **Rain job** (P5c, owner, 2026-11-07; D3 owner, 2026-11-09; `apps/server/src/rain/{state,grid,client,job}.ts`; the pure rules are in `state.ts`): the `ingest` daemon only, so `backfill` and `refetch` run no rain step. One rain grid per session: 7 × 7 model points 1.5 km apart around the circuit's centre (D1: the cells cover ±5.25 km, the map's rings reach 5 km), fetched from Open-Meteo (A§6.3) and stored as 15-minute frames (A§7).
  - **Scheduling:** `rainStep` runs inside the minute job's busy guard, after the schedule sync and the OpenF1 tick. The tick breaks out of a live window instead of waiting in it, so the rain step runs every minute, OpenF1's live windows included: **Open-Meteo has no live window, and the rain job never waits for OpenF1's.** Each run does the work that is due in this order (C4): sessions in their live window that have no grid get one (`live`, due now; the database only, every run); then **at most one Open-Meteo request**: the due grid with the newest `date_end` (a live refresh and a final fetch are both a due `live` grid), else at most one backfill session. So the busy guard never waits on more than one request (A§4, the OpenF1 tick is held up by at most one), and at most one new session a minute (at most 2,940 location-calls an hour); two live sessions never overlap in practice, so one request a minute keeps every live grid on its 15 min. While a 429's pause lasts (`pausedFor()` > 0, see Client), the run makes no request, takes no budget and counts no failure, with one `rain_paused {ms}` line per pause.
  - **Live window:** the A§6.1 rule (`inLiveWindow`): not cancelled, a row of at most 12 h, `date_start − 30 min` ≤ now < `date_end + 30 min`. A circuit without a mapping (`CIRCUITS`) gets no grid ever (C5): `rain_unmapped {count}` is logged once per process, and the Weather tab says "No circuit outline for this venue." (A§13).
  - **Windows:** a refresh every 15 min while the next time is within `date_end + 30 min`, from the Forecast host over **[floor15(`date_start − 60 min`), min(floor15(now), ceil15(`date_end + 30 min`))]** (the Forecast host is never asked past now, C1); then the next fetch is `date_end + 24 h` (U3, A§6.3), from the Historical Forecast host over [floor15(`date_start − 60 min`), ceil15(`date_end + 30 min`)], and the grid becomes `final`. Each fetch replaces all of the grid's frames.
  - **Backfill:** the newest session that is not cancelled, has a mapped circuit and a row of at most 12 h, ended more than 24 h ago and has no grid gets one and its Historical fetch in the same run, if the budget allows. About 490 sessions × 49 = 24,010 location-calls at no more than 6,400 a day is about **3.75 days** (the P5c bar is 4). `scripts/rain-facts.sql` shows what is left.
  - **Success** (one transaction, as the session pipeline): the fetch comes first, then the transaction locks the grid row and counts the attempt (`UPDATE … SET attempt_count = attempt_count + 1 … RETURNING`, whatever the grid's state), skips it if the grid is no longer `live`, deletes its frames, inserts the new ones and sets the state: `final` (Historical) or `live` with the next time, `fetch_count + 1`, `fail_count = 0`, `last_error = null`, `updated_at` = the app clock (no SQL `now()`).
  - **Failure n** (consecutive): the same back-off as the sessions, a short locked write that also counts the attempt: due again at t + min(5 min · 2^(n−1), 6 h), the 9th → `failed`, with no automatic retry (`rain-refetch <session_key>` resets it, below). `last_error` is a fixed code: HTTP 429 → `rate_limited`, any other status → `http_<status>`, `timeout`, `network` (a redirect or a connection error), `schema` (not JSON, another shape, other than 49 locations, steps that are not the asked window's, or a body over 4 MiB), `db`, `unknown`.
  - **Budget** (D3, location-calls: a 49-point fetch is 49, U2): at most 4,000 in any hour (the free tier allows 5,000) and 8,000 in any 24 h (it allows 10,000), and the backfill takes only while the 24 h total stays within **6,400**, which keeps 1,600 for live refreshes and final fetches (a sprint Saturday needs about 800). `take` refuses and never waits. A refusal is no failure: the step ends, with one `rain_budget {kind, used}` line when a streak of refusals starts. A due grid takes from live's share, except a backfill grid that has never succeeded (Historical, `fetch_count` 0), whose retry takes from the backfill's; a live grid's final fetch (`fetch_count` > 0) stays live. After a restart both windows are seeded once from the database as Σ `attempt_count` × `size`² over the grids with `updated_at` in the window, placed at now: an over-estimate (a grid's lifetime attempts, counted as just made), so a restart never exceeds a cap. The one gap is an attempt whose own write failed, logged as `unrecorded`.
  - **Client** (`client.ts`): the two fixed URLs only, picked by `api`; Node's global `fetch` with an undici Agent (so msw covers it); a limiter of 6 requests in any 60 s (6 × 49 = 294 location-calls a minute against the free tier's 600, U2) and 1 in any 1 s; **one attempt per call** (C3), the job's back-off is the retry, so a slow Open-Meteo never holds the busy guard; a 429 pauses the limiter for `Retry-After` or 60 s, **at most 5 min** (a far date must not stop the rain job for days), and `pausedFor()` tells the job; redirects refused; a 60 s deadline for headers and body (`timeout`); at most 4 MiB read; `User-Agent: f1-timings`. An error carries a code, never a URL, body or message. **The answer is tied to its request:** 49 locations on the same steps, at least one step, 900 s apart and all within the asked [from, to] (so at most (to − from)/900 s + 1); else `schema`. A precipitation is 0 to 1000 mm or null (else `schema`); one that PostgreSQL's `real` cannot hold (non-zero below 1e-37, `isReal` in `@f1/contracts`) is stored as null, as P5b's `weatherRow` does.
  - **Log line** per grid: `rain` `{session_key, api, state, frames, points, ms, last_error}` (and `cause`, the PostgreSQL SQLSTATE or the error name, on a `db` or `unknown` failure; `unrecorded`, the SQLSTATE, when the failure's own write failed), at error level on a failure. Besides it: `rain_unmapped` `{count}`, `rain_budget` `{kind, used}`, `rain_paused` `{ms}` and `rain failed` `{cause}` (the step itself failed). Never a value, a coordinate, a URL or a body. A grid that newly reaches `failed` pings `f1-ingest/fail` with `rain_failed <code>` (#105, above).
  - **Heartbeat:** touched when the step starts, as its Open-Meteo attempt starts, after the grid and at the end. One attempt lasts at most 60 s, within the 120 s limit (A§4). The step checks `stopping` before its request.
  - **[box] check** (`scripts/rain-facts.sql`, read-only as `f1_api`; owner, 2026-11-07): circuit names of meetings with an OpenF1 session without a mapping (expected 0 rows; `test/rain-facts.test.ts` pins its list to `CIRCUITS`; archive-only meetings of 2018–2022 are left out, as the job grids OpenF1 sessions only; owner, 2026-11-13), grids per state and `last_error`, mapped OpenF1 sessions past `date_end + 24 h` without a grid (0 after about 4 days), the location-calls spent and grids updated per UTC day, frames per grid, and any frame outside [`date_start − 1 h`, `date_end + 45 min`]. Log counts: `docker compose logs ingest | grep -c '"msg":"rain"'` per day, plus the `rain_budget` lines.

## 9. Timing rules (pure TS in `apps/server/src/timing/`, unit- and property-tested)

- **Deleted laps** (`rules.ts`; the lap rule amended by the owner 2026-10-26 after the full-data check, A§6.1): parse `race_control.message` after collapsing whitespace. The car is taken from the text, because `driver_number` is null in the data. `CAR n (ABC)`, then `TIME m:ss.mmm` or `LAP`, then `DELETED - <reason>`, then optionally `LAP n hh:mm:ss` and ` (PIT)`:
  - `CAR 87 (BEA) TIME 1:40.294 DELETED - TRACK LIMITS AT TURN 2 LAP 3 20:07:17`
  - `CAR 11 (PER) LAP DELETED - DOUBLE YELLOW AT TURN 12 LAP 2 21:32:22`, `CAR 5 (BOR) LAP DELETED - TRACK LIMITS AT TURN 11 LAP 9 16:29:31 (PIT)`
  - without `LAP n`: `CAR 87 (BEA) TIME 1:15.197 DELETED - TRACK LIMITS AT TURN 8 (NEXT LAP)`, `CAR 12 (ANT) LAP DELETED - TRACK LIMITS AT TURN 20 (NEXT LAP PIT)`
  - The reason is the text after ` - `, without the `LAP n …` part.
  - **Which lap:** a TIME matches the car's laps within 1 ms: one → that lap; several → the one lap with exactly that time (owner 2026-10-27), else the expected lap if it is one of them, else none; none → `LAP n`. **`LAP n` is the driver's lap n in a `Race`-type session (race, sprint) and lap n − 1 everywhere else** (practice, qualifying, sprint qualifying): of the 2,784 TIME + `LAP n` messages whose time one lap has, 2,773 sit there; the 11 others are OpenF1 numbering gaps that the time already resolves. A message that names no lap is counted, never applied.
  - **REINSTATED** (26 in 2023–2026): `CAR 63 (RUS) TIME 1:27.168 WILL BE REINSTATED` and `CAR 43 (COL) LAP 13 WILL BE REINSTATED`. The same rule, among the car's laps deleted at that moment, clears the lap.
  - **Order:** the messages in date order (dates compared parsed: they mix `+00:00` and `.095000+00:00`; ties keep their order). A lap deleted twice keeps the first reason; a REINSTATED before its DELETED clears nothing.
  - A DELETED message may start with `CORRECTION - ` and may carry its driver tag twice (`CAR 77 (BOT) (BOT) TIME …`, a feed typo): both read as the plain form (VM check 2026-11-12: sessions 5745, 5769 and 6708 of the F1 archive).
  - A message containing DELETED or REINSTATED in no known form is counted as `unparsed`. The per-session log line carries the counts, never a message (A§8). The same check found 10 more on the VM, left unparsed on purpose: session 6250's five hand-typed 2021 variants (`LAP 5 - 1.30.805 - CAR 10 (GAS) DELETED TRACK LIMITS`), session 9222's car 243 (OpenF1 data), and session 11287's `CAR LEC TIME … (NEXT LAP) (Q1)` form (a code instead of the car number; one qualifying so far, every other 2026 session has none). Counting them is the designed outcome; each affects a lap or two of one session.
  - A deleted lap keeps its values and is excluded from all bests.
- **Qualifying phase** (`rules.ts`): the phase of the last `race_control` row with a non-null `qualifying_phase` dated at or before the lap's `date_start` (a lap starting exactly on a row takes the new phase; same dates keep their order). Null without such a row or without a `date_start`. Sessions without phase rows (practice, races) get null; a lap started after the chequered flag keeps that phase. **Q1 inference** (owner 2026-10-27): in a session of type `Qualifying` (sprint qualifying included) whose first phase row is phase 2, a lap that starts before that row is Q1, because OpenF1 sent that session's Q1 rows without a phase (five 2026 sessions). Checked in A§6.1. Since P3b the merge keeps a lap's merged per-value phase and takes this one only where that is null (A§7), so a single-source session equals its rows except a lap whose own phase is null, which takes race control's (remote for the archive: a qualifying whose first stored phase row is Q2).
- **Colours in the historical view (final semantics)** (`colours.ts`, per request in the `api`; no column stores them): purple = the fastest valid value of that sector or lap in the session; green = the driver's own fastest valid value; yellow = the rest; null = no value. Out-laps and in-laps are never purple or green. Live (P2) uses progressive semantics: purple/green at that moment, as on the F1 tower (`progressiveColours` in `apps/server/src/timing/colours.ts`, rules in A§11.3).
  - Valid = not deleted, not an out-lap (`is_pit_out` null counts as false) and not an in-lap. A non-valid value is yellow.
  - Each driver's own best is one lap: the minimum valid value; on a tie the earlier `date_start` (nulls last), then the lower lap number. Purple is the best own best by value, `date_start`, lap number, then driver number: the first to set it holds it. Exactly one purple per field per session, or none without a valid value (a fast-check property).
- **In/out laps** (owner, 2026-10-25): out = `is_pit_out_lap`; in = lap n when the same driver's lap n+1 is an out-lap. A last lap that ends in the pits (a retirement, a qualifying in-lap after the flag) has no out-lap after it and is not marked.
  - `pit.lap_number` is not used for this. The **[U]** "`pit.lap_number` = in-lap" held only in races and sprints. P1a checked every pit row against the out-laps of 7 full sessions:
    - The out-lap is `pit.lap_number + 1` in race 11388 (25 of 25 rows) and sprint 11383 (54 of 54).
    - A pit row is not always a stop. In sprint 11383, race control sent "ALL CARS THROUGH THE PIT LANE" under the safety car (laps 2–4). Each pass is a pit row (`lane_duration` ≈ 18–19 s), and the next lap is an out-lap, so the rule above still holds.
    - The out-lap is `pit.lap_number` itself in qualifying 11392 (90/90) and 11384 (103/103), sprint qualifying 11379 (74/74) and practice 11378 (76/76) and 11391 (92/92). Lap-1 rows with null durations there are garage exits.
  - The test `[U] pit.lap_number against the out-laps` in `apps/server/test/openf1-schemas.test.ts` pins both meanings on the fixtures.
- **Sector deltas (comparison):** per lap, `driverB − driverA` per sector, and since #102 for the lap time too (`lap`). Shown only when both values exist and neither lap is deleted. `sectorDeltas(a, b)` lives in `packages/contracts` (owner, P1c), where the web (P1e) imports it. **Best-lap row** (owner, 2026-10-28): the table opens with each driver's own best lap, the lap the server colours purple or green (exactly the own best above), paired whatever their lap numbers by giving both lap number 0 in `sectorDeltas`; it is labelled with both numbers ("Best lap", then "L12 vs L15" on a second line) and is always shown: a driver without a best lap gets "L—" and `—` cells.

## 10. API surface (`/api/v1`, JSON, zod contracts in `packages/contracts`)

| Route | Returns |
|---|---|
| `GET /seasons` | years with counts of meetings and sessions (not cancelled), newest first |
| `GET /seasons/:year` | meetings with their sessions, `ingest_state` and `is_cancelled`, by `date_start` |
| `GET /sessions/:key` | session, meeting, drivers, results (with `grid` since #115: the starting grid position, OpenF1's for races and sprints or Jolpica's, null without one; a contract change). (amended, #99, owner 2026-11-13) The session also carries `source`, `fetch_count`, `fail_count`, `last_error` and `next_fetch_at` (A§7, A§8), so a page can say why data is missing; `last_error` is one of the A§8 CHECK codes, never text (the contract's regex is the DDL's) |
| `GET /sessions/:key/laps` | all laps with colours, deleted flags, `quali_phase` and stint compound, computed server-side (a race is ~1,240 rows, ~20 KB gzipped; two queries: the session lookup, then the laps with their compound) |
| `GET /sessions/:key/stints` · `/pits` · `/race-control` | as named |
| `GET /sessions/:key/weather` (P5a, owner, 2026-11-07) | every `weather` sample of the session by `date`, with the A§7 column names (`Weather` contract); `[]` when there are none. The cache rule and the errors below apply |
| `GET /sessions/:key/rain` (P5c, owner, 2026-11-07) | `{grid, frames}`: the session's `rain_grid` as `{map_id, centre_lat, centre_lon, size, step_m, state}` (`null` without one) and its `rain_frame` rows by `date` as `{date, mm}`, with `mm` the 49 values of A§7 (an element may be null); `frames` is `[]` without a grid (`Rain` contract). `private, max-age=86400` only when the session **and** the grid are `final`, else `no-store`; the errors below apply |
| `GET /status` | the api's `revision` (below), session counts per `ingest_state` (not cancelled), the cancelled count, and every session whose last fetch failed (`fail_count` > 0: retrying or `failed`), and `recorder`: the one `recorder_status` row (state, session key and names, F1 TV token expiry, last error, warning, `updated_at`) with `stale` (no report for 5 min, computed here), or null before the recorder has reported; `ingest` (#105): the one `ingest_status` row (`last_sync_at`, `live_wait`, `last_tick_at`) with `stale` (no tick report for 5 min), or null before the daemon has written; `rain`: the rain grids' counts per state (`live`, `final`, `failed`) and the grids with `fail_count` > 0 (the same item as `failed`); `rc_unmatched`: the non-cancelled Race sessions with period-like race-control rows that match no form (A§6.1), newest first with the row count, computed per request from `race_control` with `isUnmatched` in `packages/contracts` |
| `GET /live` (P2d) | `{session, recording}`: the open recording and its session if there is one, else the next session and its latest recording. "Next" uses the recorder's window (`date_end` + 30 min, the exported `AFTER_MS`) without the recorder's completed-recording exclusion, so a finished session stays selectable for replay; each is null when absent. `no-store` |
| `GET /recordings` (P2e) | every recording with its session and meeting, newest `started_at` first (then `id`): `{id, session_key, year, meeting_name, session_name, session_type, date_start, started_at, ended_at, complete}`. `no-store` |
| `GET /playback/:recording/stream?at=<ms>\|live&delay=<s>&speed=<n>&still=1` (P2d; `still` #101) | SSE: one `snapshot` event, then `delta` and `laps` events, then `end` (A§11.3) |

- JSON uses the A§7 column names (snake_case, no mapping layer), times as ISO-8601 UTC strings and durations as integer ms. Every answer has a strict zod contract in `packages/contracts` (P1c); an integration test parses each route against it.
- `Cache-Control: private, max-age=86400` on a per-session route of a `final` session (the rain route needs its grid `final` too) and `no-store` on everything else under `/api`: a session that is not final, `/seasons*`, `/status`, and every 4xx/5xx. TanStack Query caches on the client. So a final session stays cached privately in the browser for 24 h: a later rules change or a `refetch` shows only after that, or after a hard reload. (owner, 2026-11-13, #98): the web adds `?v=<revision>` to the six per-session GETs as a cache key only (the answer is the same), so a new build never reads an entry written under another one, and `max-age` applies only when `v` is absent or equal to the api's revision: for another one the answer is `no-store`, because `compose up -d` recreates api and web together and the new SPA can briefly reach the old api, whose body would otherwise be cached under the new key for 24 h. Considered and not chosen: `hono/etag` with `no-cache`, which costs a round trip through Access and the database per route and view of a final session.
- **Revision** (owner, 2026-11-13, #98): `F1_REVISION` is the release's git commit (40 lowercase hex, CI's `github.sha`), or `dev` for any other build (`REVISION` in `packages/contracts`). The server image takes it as an `ENV` from the build arg (it is not a secret: it is also the image's revision label) and the web build embeds it through Vite's `define` (`import.meta.env.F1_REVISION`). A malformed value makes the `api` exit 78, logging the variable's name only, and fails the web build. The api sets `F1-Revision: <revision>` on every answer under `/api/v1` behind the JWT (a 401 carries none, nor does a 404 outside `/api/v1`; the stream's HEAD answer carries it), and `/status` reports it. When the header differs from the build's own, `getJson` still returns the data and raises the app-wide stale flag (A§13, owner, 2026-11-13, D4); it never throws for it, and an absent header is fine.
- **The service worker** (#121, owner, 2026-11-13; `apps/web/src/sw/`, A§13) is a client-side layer over that rule; the HTTP cache rule is unchanged. It stores an answer of `^/api/v1/sessions/\d{1,9}(/(laps|stints|pits|race-control|weather|rain))?$` (the pathname only, so #98's `?v=` passes; the key is the full URL) only if it is a 200, not redirected, `application/json`, with a non-zero `max-age` and neither `no-store` nor `no-cache` (in any case): a final session's answers and nothing else, because every live or 4xx/5xx answer is `no-store`. Every path it handles, `/assets/*` and navigations included, is network-first: it serves the stored copy only when the fetch rejects (no network, or Access's redirect under the page's `redirect: 'error'`, which is then never stored).
