Window script can write Cache Storage, so a stored copy is never served online; hashed assets still come from the immutable HTTP cache without a round trip. The web's query client sets `networkMode: 'always'` (TanStack's default `'online'` pauses a query after an `offline` event, so it would never reach the worker). It never intercepts a non-GET, another origin, `/cdn-cgi/` (Access's own endpoints), `/seasons*`, `/status`, `/live`, `/recordings`, `/playback` or `/healthz`.
- Errors are fixed: a bad `key` (`^\d{1,9}$`) or `year` (`^\d{4}$`) → 400 `{"error":"bad request"}`, an unknown one → 404 `{"error":"not found"}`, a `v` that is empty, malformed or repeated → the same 400, anything else → 500 `{"error":"internal"}`, logged as a code. No answer or log echoes input, a header, the token or a database message.
- **The playback stream** (P2d, `apps/server/src/api/app.ts`; the engine is A§11.3):
  - **Parameters:** `:recording` is `^\d{1,9}$`. `at` is required: digits (epoch ms) or the literal `live`, and a number must lie within [`started_at`, `ended_at` ?? now]. `delay` is an integer 0–120 and only with `at=live` (default 0). `speed` is one of 1, 2, 4, 8, 16 (default 1) and must be 1 with `at=live`. `still` (#101) is absent or exactly `1`, and only with a numeric `at`: it marks a still frame (a paused seek, or a load that resumes at `?t`, A§13) and changes nothing but the open log line below. A bad parameter is 400 `{"error":"bad request"}`, an unknown recording or a missing recording directory 404 `{"error":"not found"}`, both fixed as above. Access (the `/api/*` middleware) covers every route here.
  - **Path:** the directory is `/srv/f1/live` joined with the `recording` row's `path`, checked again against `^\d+-\d+$`. Nothing from the URL goes into a path.
  - **Cap:** at most 8 streams per process; over it, 503 `{"error":"unavailable"}`, `no-store`. Since P4e a paused seek opens a short-lived still-frame stream (closed by its snapshot, A§13), still at most one stream per tab.
  - **Headers:** `Content-Type: text/event-stream`, `Cache-Control: no-store, no-transform`.
  - **HEAD:** hono runs the GET handler for HEAD and `@hono/node-server` discards a streaming body without cancelling it, so a HEAD would leak a slot, a file handle and a generator until restart (Caddy allows HEAD). The route answers HEAD itself, after validation and the recording lookup and before the cap: 200, the headers above, an empty body, no generator.
  - **Write timeout:** one SSE write (or ping) that has not completed within 60 s ends the stream: the controller aborts, the generator's `finally` runs and the slot is freed. Not covered by an automated test: a stalled client needs socket buffers filled by a real recording of tens of MB or a timer injection into hono's stream internals, either slow or flaky. `createApp` takes `writeTimeoutMs` for a later test.
  - **Events:** `snapshot` `{t, state, laps, stints, weather}`, `delta` `{t, topic, data}`, `laps` `{t, laps, stints}`, `end` `{t}` and the heartbeat `ping` `{t}` (`PlaybackPing`), with `t` in epoch ms on the receive axis (A§11.3). `data` is what `createFeed().apply` returned, and the client applies it the same way: an object merges into `state[topic]`, anything else replaces it. `laps` and `stints` are the rows of the `Lap` and `Stints` contracts (P2e): all of them so far in a `snapshot`, and in a `laps` event only the rows whose JSON changed since the last `snapshot` or `laps`, which the client puts over the row with the same (`driver_number`, `lap_number`) or (`driver_number`, `stint_number`). `weather` (P5b, owner, 2026-11-07) holds the rows of the `Weather` contract (the shape of `/sessions/:key/weather`, above): every `WeatherData` sample since the stream's last session switch, one per applied `WeatherData` delta at its receive time `t`, but at most the last 1,440 (`MAX_WEATHER_ROWS`, a day at one a minute, P5b review), whatever the recording holds. The client adds `weatherRow` of the merged `WeatherData` view at the delta's `t` with `withWeatherRow`, which drops the oldest past that cap as the server does (`weatherRow` in `packages/contracts`: each feed string becomes its number when it is non-blank, finite and fits the A§7 column, a `real` or, for rainfall and wind direction, a smallint integer, else null; `date` is the ISO time of `t`). A `laps` event comes at most once per second of wall time after a TimingData, TimingAppData, RaceControlMessages or SessionData change, and one is flushed before `end`; its `t` is the receive time of the last line applied. The rows carry the progressive colours, the stint's compound, and `deleted`, `deleted_reason` and `quali_phase` by A§9 as of T (A§11.3, #95); `seg1`–`seg3` are null (the tower reads the segments from the state). There is no `id:` field; the client reopens with a new `at` after an error. `end` and `ping` carry data because EventSource drops an event without any. The heartbeat is an event, not an SSE comment (P2e fix): EventSource never delivers a comment to the page, so only an event can feed the client's liveness check (A§13).
  - **Open log** (#101): one `info` line per stream, `playback open`, when its first `snapshot` is serialized (before the write, so a still frame that closes at once counts): `mode` (`live`, `replay` or `still`), `scan_ms` (the seek's wall time from the generator's start to the built snapshot: the file reads, the parsing and the lap rows), `lines` (the lines the seek applied) and `snapshot_bytes` (the snapshot event's `data` in UTF-8 bytes, without the SSE framing). Those four fields, pino's `level`, `time` and `msg` and the logger's base `role` (`api`), nothing else: no URL, recording id, path, `at` or feed text. `scan_ms` is on `Date.now`, so a clock step during a seek skews that one line, and it excludes serializing the snapshot. A HEAD, a 400 and a later snapshot (a session switch, a backlog) log none. `playback()` itself still logs nothing: it reports through `onOpen`. The [CI] test is "one open log line per stream" in `playback.int.test.ts`.
  - **Database:** one lookup at the start, then `ended_at` at most every 30 s while the reader sits at the end of the recording. No pool connection is held across a stream (the pool maximum is 2).
- **The Access JWT** (P1c, `apps/server/src/api/access.ts`; ADR-0004 amended) is checked on every `/api` request, every method and unknown paths included, by our own middleware on hono's `verifyWithJwks`, not the `hono/jwk` middleware (that one wants `Bearer <token>`, and Cloudflare sends the bare JWT):
  - The token is the `Cf-Access-Jwt-Assertion` header; `Authorization` and cookies are ignored. A header with whitespace, or a token whose header is not RS256 with a `kid`, is refused before any certs fetch.
  - Then the signature against the team's certs, the `kid`, `iss` = `https://<team>.cloudflareaccess.com`, `aud` (a string or an array holding the AUD tag), `exp` (required; hono skips an absent one) and `nbf` (≤ now + 60 s). `iat` is not checked: hono has no leeway for it, and `exp`/`nbf` cover freshness. The keys are passed in, so a token's `jku`/`x5u` is never followed.
  - Every refusal is the same 401 `{"error":"unauthorized"}`, with no reason in the answer or the log.
  - The certs (`https://<team>.cloudflareaccess.com/cdn-cgi/access/certs`, 5 s timeout, no redirects, parsed non-strictly) are kept per process for 5 min; concurrent requests share one fetch. Only RSA keys with a `kid` are used, any other entry is skipped, and a list without one is a failure. When a refresh after the 5 min fails, the last keys serve for up to 1 h after they were fetched (each such request logs `access_certs` with `stale: true`), then 503 `{"error":"unavailable"}`. A cold start without keys answers 503 at once, logged as `access_certs`, and the next request fetches again (no negative cache). An unknown `kid` is a 401 without a refetch, so a key rotation can refuse logins for up to 5 min. A `ponytail:` note in the code names both fixes: a refetch on an unknown `kid` at most once a minute, and a 30 s negative cache.
  - Config: `CF_ACCESS_TEAM` (`^[a-z0-9][a-z0-9-]{0,62}$`, which keeps the certs URL on `cloudflareaccess.com`) and `CF_ACCESS_AUD` (`^[0-9a-f]{64}$`) from `/etc/f1/f1.env`, a Compose `env_file` (P1f). They are not secrets. Missing or malformed → the `api` role logs the variable's name and exits 78 before listening.
- `/healthz` (`select 1` → 200 `{"ok":true}`, else 503 `{"ok":false}`, `no-store`) is the only route without the JWT check. The local update smoke test uses it, and through the tunnel it still sits behind Access.

## 11. Live and replay (phase 2)

### 11.1 Feed

- SignalR Core at `wss://livetiming.formula1.com/signalrcore` (negotiate at `/signalrcore/negotiate`, preceded by an OPTIONS request for the `AWSALBCORS` cookie), hub `Streaming`, `Subscribe([topics])`, messages arrive on the `feed` method. The token goes in through the access-token factory. (Source: FastF1 3.9.0 `livetiming/client.py`.)
- Topics: `Heartbeat, DriverList, ExtrapolatedClock, RaceControlMessages, SessionInfo, SessionStatus, SessionData, TimingAppData, TimingStats, TimingData, TrackStatus, WeatherData, TopThree, LapCount, TeamRadio, RcmSeries, AudioStreams, ContentStreams, PitLaneTimeCollection, PitStopSeries, PitStop, CarData.z, Position.z`. Record all of them; parse only those the views need (CarData/Position are recorded for later, not parsed, and so are the pit topics).
- **Topics and the token** (P2a, Mexico City FP1 `11397`, 2026-10-30, counts only): without a token 19,117 messages arrived on 17 topics; with a token 19 topics. `CarData.z` and `Position.z` need the token; `RcmSeries` never appeared in the state. So a recording without a token lacks the car and position data (recorded for later, not parsed).
- **Pit topics** (#96; archive check on 2026-11-13 against the Mexico City race 11404 and the São Paulo qualifying 11408, with `fetch-archive.ts --topic` and `feed-keys.ts --topic`, key paths and counts only):
  - `PitLaneTimeCollection` is in both (race 110 messages, qualifying 161). Its paths are `PitTimes.#.{Duration, Lap, RacingNumber}`, and a later `PitTimes._deleted` removes the entry.
  - `PitStopSeries` is race only (33 messages; qualifying 403). Its paths are `PitTimes.#.#.{Timestamp, PitStop.{Lap, PitLaneTime, PitStopTime, RacingNumber}}`. `PitStopTime` is the stationary time that OpenF1 lacks (A§6.1).
  - `PitStop` is race only, listed by the session `Index.json`. It sends one stop per message with the same fields at the top level (33 messages).
  - The recorder subscribes to all three since #96, and `fetch-archive.ts` downloads them. Playback skips them like the `.z` topics (`UNPARSED`, A§11.3) until a pit-stop view parses them. A real Subscribe with the 23 names on 2026-11-13, outside any session, succeeded, and its result held `PitLaneTimeCollection` but neither `PitStopSeries` nor `PitStop`. So whether the live feed sends those two is **[U]**, for the Las Vegas recordings.
- **Archive topics not subscribed** (#96, the same check; a later decision):
  - `TyreStintSeries`, `LapSeries` and `CurrentTyres` are in both sessions. `DriverRaceInfo`, `OvertakeSeries` and `ChampionshipPrediction` are race only.
  - The session `Index.json` also lists `ArchiveStatus`, `DriverTracker`, `TimingDataF1`, `TlaRcm` and `WeatherDataSeries`, which were not fetched.
- **Client** (owner, 2026-10-30): hand-rolled, on Node's global WebSocket (undici), no dependency: `apps/server/src/live/feed-client.ts`. It sends the OPTIONS request for `AWSALBCORS`, negotiates with the cookie and the token, opens the socket with both as headers, speaks the JSON hub protocol (handshake, `Subscribe`, a ping every 15 s, a 30 s idle timeout that any frame resets, and a 60 s silence timeout that only a feed message resets, armed by a connection's first one: both close as `feed_timeout`, #94) and reports a close as a fixed code, never the server's text. Evidence, the P2a spike's FP1 run with `native:token` and `@microsoft/signalr` with a token side by side: each received 28,499 feed messages, with 0 duplicates and 0 unparsable lines; the native client needed 4 connects, the library 6. All three connections (the anonymous one too) flapped for about 3 min around 17:30 UTC, the −60 min mark, then held for about 2.4 h without a drop. Volume: 4.7 MB/h raw, 2.6 MB/h gzipped, a peak of 614 messages per minute, a median latency of 1.55 s.
- **The `_subscribe` order** (proven in the spike): the spike wrote the `Subscribe` result from the promise, and a promise reaction runs after every frame of the same network read, so the full state could land after newer deltas of the same read. `feed-client.ts` calls `onSubscribed` in frame order and the recorder writes the `_subscribe` line there.
- **`SessionStatus`:** checked on an archived qualifying session and on FP1: a connect's first state can hold the previous session's `Ends`, `Ends` appears once at the end, and between qualifying segments the status is `Finished` then `Inactive`. The race 11404 (planned 20:00–22:00 UTC) confirms this and adds: a red flag is `Aborted` then `Started` again; `Finished` came 23 min and `Ends` 55 min after `date_end`, so a stop at `date_end` + 30 min would have missed the end; and about 50 min before the start a `Started` lasted 2 s, then `Inactive`. The recorder polls every 30 s, so it rarely sees that blip; if it does, the recording only counts as started early, and the session's real `Ends` still ends it.
- **The quiet feed and `Ends`** (owner, 2026-11-12, #94; times and counts only, on the owner's machine, from the P2a recordings of 11397 FP1 and 11404 race, the P2c recordings of the São Paulo qualifying 11408, and the F1 archive of São Paulo FP1–FP3 11405–11407):
  - **Heartbeat** comes every 15.0 s (median) while a session is live: p99 15.1–16.6 s, max 15.6 s (11397), 17.3 s (11408) and once 39.6 s (11404). The longest stretch without any feed message while Heartbeat ran was 37.5 s (11404). The 60 s silence timeout is 1.5 times the longest Heartbeat gap.
  - **Outside the live part the feed is silent:** after the subscribe at −60 min no message at all, Heartbeat included, until 12 min (11397), 13 min (11408) or 51 min (11404) before the start. After `Ends` one more SessionInfo/SessionData comes 45–67 s later, then nothing. So the silence timeout is armed by a connection's first feed message (owner, 2026-11-12). Armed at the subscribe, it would reconnect about once a minute through those stretches, about 75 times per session. The limit: a connection that never gets a message is not watched. In the live part, Heartbeat reaches a new connection within 15 s.
  - **`Ends` after `date_end`:** 11397 FP1 +26.9 min, 11405 FP1 +26.3, 11406 FP2 +29.0, 11407 FP3 +26.1, 11408 Q +107.3 (red-flagged and delayed), 11404 race +55.0. On each recording the live `Ends` came exactly 20 min after `Finalised`: to the second for 11397, 11404 and 11408, and within the 30 s tick of 11405's row. The archive's own `Ends` is earlier (`Finalised` + 0 to 13 min) and is not what the feed sends, so the values for 11405–11407 are the archive's `Finalised` + 20 min. On its own, a practice therefore stops 56–59 min past `date_end`. A 2.5 h gap puts the next session's lead at +90 min, which only a late `Ends` (as in 11408) or a missing one reaches: hence the handover (A§11.2).
  - **Values on the recordings** (owner, 2026-11-08, P4d; key paths, codes and counts only, from `node scripts/feed-keys.ts --topic TrackStatus --codes` (and `SessionStatus`, `RaceControlMessages`) on 11397 FP1, 11404 race and both segment sets of the São Paulo qualifying 11408, a P2c recorder directory read as `<(zcat <dir>/feed-*.jsonl.gz)`): `SessionStatus.Status` is `Inactive`, `Started`, `Aborted`, `Finished`, `Finalised` or `Ends`. `TrackStatus.Status` is `1` (Message `AllClear`), `2` (`Yellow`), `4` (`SCDeployed`), `5` (`Red`), `6` (`VSCDeployed`) or `7` (`VSCEnding`); code `3` never appears. `RaceControlMessages` `Category` is `Flag`, `Other` or `SafetyCar`. The session light (A§13) names a code it does not know "Track status unknown", never clear.
- **Race 11404 (2026-11-01), counts only, 4 h recorded (P2a: go):** `native:token` 99,080 feed messages and 4 connects, the library with a token 99,167 and 3, anonymous 75,617 and 5; 0 duplicates and 0 unparsable lines on all three; 7.8 MB/h raw, 3.9 MB/h gzip; peak 1,047 messages per minute. All three connections closed with 1006 once before the session (within 1 s of each other) and again 2–3 min after the start (within about 30 s of each other), and each reconnect took under 6 s. Each client missed a few messages the other received (native 162, the library 75), so a reconnect loses roughly 0.1 % of the deltas whichever client it is, and the next subscribe's full state restores the state. Over both sessions: native 8 connects, the library 9. The F1 TV login ran unattended at the start of the run.

### 11.2 Recorder (`live` role)

- **Loop** (`apps/server/src/live/recorder.ts`): a tick every 30 s that always touches the heartbeat. It reads the next session, also while recording (not cancelled, `date_end` + 30 min in the future, no complete recording yet, neither the one being recorded nor one this process already stopped, earliest `date_start`), re-reads the token file, logs in when A§12.3 says so, checks the disk and writes `recorder_status`. One recording at a time: the next session's lead ends the running one (the handover, below), and a session that truly overlaps starts late.
- **Start:** 60 min before the session's `date_start` (FastF1: "recording may need to start up to one hour before"). The directory is `/srv/f1/live/<session_key>-<startedAtMs>/` and its `recording` row is relative to `/srv/f1/live`.
- **Line format:** one JSON array per line, `[receivedAtMs, topic, payload, f1Timestamp]`, and `[receivedAtMs, "_subscribe", result]` for the full state that a `Subscribe` returns. The 3-element line of the first plan is not enough: `parseFeedLine` (`apps/server/src/live/read.ts`) takes the event time from the 4th element and a `full` event per topic from `_subscribe`, and the duplicate check in `createFeed` keys on that time. Without both, the duplicates a reconnect replays would not be recognised. Every line goes out with one `writeSync`, so there is no buffer to lose.
- **Files:** `feed.jsonl` is the current segment. After an hour it is renamed `feed-<firstReceivedMs>.jsonl` and gzipped in the background to `feed-<firstReceivedMs>.jsonl.gz`; the plain file goes only after the gzip succeeded. `snapshots/<ms>.json.gz` holds the full state every 30 s (written through a temporary name, skipped while the previous one runs; `<ms>` is wall-clock time). Files are `0600`, directories `0700`, owned by 62532.
- **Stop** (`decide`, run every tick on the session row read again from the database):
  - Cancelled, or moved so that now is before `date_start` − 60 min: stop, incomplete (it is not marked finished, so the moved session is recorded when its time comes).
  - `SessionStatus: Ends` seen after a `Started` seen in this recording: stop at `Ends` + 30 min. An `Ends` before any `Started` is ignored, because the subscribe's full state can still hold the previous session's status (A§11.1).
  - Now ≥ `date_end` + 30 min and no `Started` seen: stop. Once one was seen, the recording waits for `Ends`: past the planned end the status can be `Finished` or `Inactive` between qualifying segments, so no status short of `Ends` means the session is over. That covers a red flag too.
  - Now ≥ `date_end` + 3 h: stop whatever the status.
  - **Handover** (owner, 2026-11-09, #94): when the next session starts at or after this one's `date_end`, the recording stops at max(next `date_start` − 60 min, `date_end`). It logs `handover` with both session keys, and the next session starts on the following tick. The rules above still stop it earlier when they apply. This is a normal stop (`complete` as below): a late `Ends`, or none, never delays the next session's start, and the next lead never cuts a session before its planned end. A next session that starts before this `date_end` truly overlaps and changes nothing here: it starts late, once one of the rules above ends this recording.
- **Reconnect:** backoff 1 s doubling to 30 s, back to 1 s after a connection that lasted over 60 s. Every connect subscribes again and writes a `_subscribe` line; the merge logic tolerates the duplicates (`apps/server/test/feed-recorder.test.ts` replays after a drop against a fake SignalR server). A negotiate answered 401 or 403 is `feed_unauthorized` (A§12.3). A feed that goes silent while the pings go on, once a message has come, closes as `feed_timeout` after 60 s (A§11.1) and reconnects the same way. The silent stretch itself does not count as a gap for `complete`, only the reconnect after it does.
  - **Measured** (A§11.1): F1 closed all three race connections with 1006 about 1 h after their previous connects, while the FP1 connections held for 2.45 h, so there is no proven fixed limit. The merge recovered every lap and sector time across the race drops: all three race recordings equal the archive on laps 2 and later (A§11.3).
- **`complete`** is true when the recording reached a normal stop (not a cancel or move, not a disk failure, not a SIGTERM), saw `Started` (a postponed session, or a feed with only `Heartbeat`, is not complete), was subscribed at least once, carried a token on every connection that overlaps `date_start` … `date_end`, and had no disconnect over 60 s between `date_start` and `date_end`. The time from `date_start` to the first subscribed connection counts as a gap, so a late start is not complete; flaps before `date_start` (the −60 min window) do not count. Without a token at session time the recorder still records, anonymously (17 of 19 topics), and `complete` stays false.
- **Disk:** free space under `/srv/f1/live` is read every tick; under 5 GiB the status warns `disk_low`. A failed write (a full disk), or under 1 GiB free (the floor: the disk may be shared with PostgreSQL, so the recorder stops before a write fails), ends the recording with `disk` as `last_error` and `complete = false`. No recording starts under the floor, nor for 5 min after a disk end or the last tick under the floor.
- **Restart:** at boot every `recording` row without `ended_at` is closed (`ended_at` = now, `complete = false`) and its leftover plain segment gzipped. A restart in a session therefore gives a new directory and row; one past `date_end` + 30 min is not resumed. Such a recording starts mid-session: its laps start at each driver's current lap with no `date_start` (A§11.3, the mid-session start), never a phantom lap 1, and the laps before are not in it (#95; São Paulo qualifying 11408's second directory starts 20 min in). SIGTERM closes the socket, gzips the last segment and closes the row (`stop_grace_period: 60s`).
- **Logs:** pino with fixed codes and non-secret fields only: never a token, password, cookie, URL, payload or the server's close text.

### 11.3 Parser and playback

- **One parser** (`apps/server/src/live/`) merges topic deltas into a typed state. F1 deltas are partial objects; arrays arrive as index-keyed objects (`"Lines": {"1": {...}}`). The same code reads archive `.jsonStream` files in P3 (ADR-0006). Pure code: `read.ts` (input → events), `merge.ts` (`createFeed`: events → state, with the duplicate filter), `laps.ts` (deltas → `lap`/`stint` rows). `merge()`, `Obj` and the typed views (`view`, `Views`) live in `packages/contracts/src/live.ts` since P2e, because the web client applies the stream's deltas with the same code; browser-safe, zod is the only import.
- **Input** (`read.ts`): every input becomes `FeedEvent {t, topic, payload, full?}`.
  - A P2a `feed.jsonl` line `[receivedAtMs, topic, payload, f1Timestamp]` is one event at the F1 timestamp, or at `receivedAtMs` when that is missing (the native client writes it as `"undefined"`, the library client as null). `[receivedAtMs, "_subscribe", result]` is the `Subscribe` completion's full state: one `full` event per topic at `receivedAtMs`, SessionInfo first (SignalR Core has no classic `R` message, P2a).
  - An archive file has the line format of A§6.2. A `.z` payload stays a raw string. `CarData.z` and `Position.z` are recorded, never parsed. A line with a bad time or bad JSON is skipped and counted.
  - Durations (`duration()`, FastF1 `to_timedelta`): `h:m:s.f`, `m:s.f` or `s.f`; the fraction is cut to µs, then rounded to ms.
- **Merge semantics** (`merge()` in `packages/contracts/src/live.ts`; the duplicate rule is `createFeed`'s):
  - Objects merge key by key. An array in a delta is an index-keyed object (`[a, b]` is `{"0": a, "1": b}`), so the state holds no arrays. A leaf replaces; an object replacing a leaf, or the reverse, replaces too.
  - `merge` returns the **effective delta**: only what changed, as fresh objects that never alias the state. A new object counts as changed even when empty, so merging the effective deltas from empty rebuilds the state.
  - An exact duplicate (same topic, `t` and payload) within 5 min of feed time is dropped, because a reconnect replays messages.
  - A full state is applied as its effective delta (what it changes, or nothing) and is never deduplicated.
  - `_deleted: [keys]` removes those keys from the object it sits in. The effective delta lists the keys really removed. The stream needs it (11392: `"_deleted":["Lap"]` under `BestLapTime`) and the final `.json` states hold none.
  - A null or undefined payload is ignored. A payload that is no object (a `.z` string) replaces the topic's value.
  - Input is untrusted: state objects have no prototype, reads are own-property only, the keys `__proto__`, `constructor` and `prototype` are dropped, and an object nested deeper than 32 levels is dropped.
- **Typed views** (`packages/contracts/src/live.ts`): one zod 4 `looseObject` per topic (DriverList, TimingData, TimingAppData, TimingStats, SessionInfo, SessionStatus, SessionData, TrackStatus, LapCount, RaceControlMessages, ExtrapolatedClock, WeatherData). Every field is optional, a bad leaf becomes undefined (`.catch`), so one odd value never hides a topic, and unknown fields pass through. Index-keyed collections are records.
- **Gap fields per session type** (2026-11-07, the design review's [U] in `docs/design/2026-11-redesign/README.md`, "Found in passing"): TimingData lines carry different gap fields per `SessionInfo.Type`. Checked on FastF1's frozen F1 archive responses (the public `theOehrly/fastf1-test-data` at `5d4c45d`; key names and counts only, nothing copied), read with our `parseJsonStream` and `merge`: 5 practice sessions (2020–2024) plus FastF1's live recording of Bahrain FP3 2021, 9 of type Qualifying (2019–2024, the 2023 Sprint Shootout and a 2024 Sprint Qualifying among them) and 19 of type Race (2018–2026, two of them sprints). Each key path below appears in every session of its type and in none of the other two types:
  - **Race:** `GapToLeader` and `IntervalToPositionAhead.Value` (and `Catching`). The leader's are `LAP <n>`, a lapped car's gap `<n>L` or `<n> L`.
  - **Practice:** `TimeDiffToFastest` and `TimeDiffToPositionAhead` on the line, empty for the fastest driver and for a driver without a lap time. The live recording carries the same two.
  - **Qualifying:** per part only, `Stats.<i>.TimeDiffToFastest` and `Stats.<i>.TimeDifftoPositionAhead` (a lower-case "to": `TimeDiffToPositionAhead` never appears in qualifying), with the part's best lap in `BestLapTimes.<i>.Value`. `<i>` is `SessionPart − 1`: 2,835 of the 2,838 indexed `Stats` deltas and all 1,065 `BestLapTimes` deltas write that entry, the other 3 the previous part's. `SessionPart` is 0 in the first state of 7 of the 9 sessions, for under 10 s, then 1 to 3. `NoEntries` lists the cars per part (three counts); the tower does not read it.
  - Sprint Shootout and Sprint Qualifying are of type `Qualifying`, sprints of type `Race`.
  - **The tower on the same data** (`towerRows` every 10 s of feed time, a one-off run outside the repository): in practice (6 sessions, 41,070 rows) Gap shows on 64.3 % of rows, 0 % before this fix; `—` says "No lap time yet" on 31.1 % and "Fastest lap" on 4.7 %. In qualifying (50,040 rows) 48.0 %, 0 % before: "No time in Q<n> yet" 27.2 %, "Knocked out" 20.8 %, "Fastest in Q<n>" 4.0 %, and the generic "No gap in the feed" on 3 rows. In races (223,887 rows) 80.6 %, as before: "Retired" 3.2 %, "No gap in the feed" 16.2 %, 60 % of them before any car has completed a lap. Int shows on 64.3 %, 47.9 % and 79.6 %.
  - **[box] (owner, 2026-11-07):** the same on 2026 data, from the F1 archives with `node scripts/feed-keys.ts fixtures-private/archive/11392 fixtures-private/archive/11397 fixtures-private/archive/11404` (or a recording's `feed.jsonl`; key paths and message counts per session, no values). Of the TimingData messages, qualifying 11392 (8,274) carries `Stats.<i>.TimeDiffToFastest` in 86, `Stats.<i>.TimeDifftoPositionAhead` in 93 and `BestLapTimes.<i>.Value` in 90; practice 11397 (16,453) `TimeDiffToFastest` in 137 and `TimeDiffToPositionAhead` in 138; race 11404 (68,291) `GapToLeader` in 27,078 and `IntervalToPositionAhead.Value` in 28,281. None carries another type's gap keys.
  - **[box] on recordings (owner, 2026-11-08, P4d):** `node scripts/feed-keys.ts fixtures-private/live/p2a/11397-fp1-native-token <(zcat fixtures-private/live/11408-1794070823333/feed-*.jsonl.gz) <(zcat fixtures-private/live/11408-1794075638034/feed-*.jsonl.gz)`; `feed-keys.ts` reads a P2c recorder directory only through `<(zcat <dir>/feed-*.jsonl.gz)`. Practice 11397 (from the P2a recording): `TimeDiffToFastest` in 137 and `TimeDiffToPositionAhead` in 138 of 16,453 TimingData messages, no other gap key. Qualifying 11408 (the P2c recorder's two recordings, one subscribe state of 562 messages and the session's 10,534): `Stats.<i>.TimeDiffToFastest` in 1 and 175, `Stats.<i>.TimeDifftoPositionAhead` in 1 and 180, `BestLapTimes.<i>.Value` in 1 and 180; `SessionPart` in 1 and 4, `KnockedOut` in 1 and 4, `Cutoff` in 2 and 2. Same result as the archives above.
  - **[box] WeatherData on recordings (owner's machine, 2026-11-09, P5b; counts and timings only, no values):** `node scripts/feed-keys.ts --topic WeatherData fixtures-private/live/p2a/11397-fp1-native-token fixtures-private/live/p2a/11404-race-native-token`. Every WeatherData message is a full object: `AirTemp`, `Humidity`, `Pressure`, `Rainfall`, `TrackTemp`, `WindDirection`, `WindSpeed` and `_kf` are in every message (11397 Practice 79 of 79, 11404 Race 209 of 209; the previous session's state before the switch 3 each). One a minute: the gaps between receive times have a median of 60.0 s (p10 59.9 s, p90 60.0–60.1 s, max 61.6 s) in 11397, 11404 and the two P2c recordings of 11408 (34 and 126 messages); one 4.1 s gap in 11404 follows a Subscribe state. Rainfall and WindDirection are integer strings and the other five decimal strings in every message (82 of 82 in 11397's recording and 212 of 212 in 11404's, Subscribe states included), which is what `weatherRow`'s integer rule for those two rests on. A plain delta's `createFeed().apply` returns the payload itself and a Subscribe state its effective delta, so the stream has a row per message and one per Subscribe state that changed the topic.
- **Lap reconstruction** (`laps.ts`) from `TimingData`/`TimingAppData` produces the `lap` and `stint` rows OpenF1 builds, incrementally. **FastF1 is the base** (MIT, notice kept in the ported files). **Where it disagrees with OpenF1, OpenF1's behaviour wins** (owner, 2026-10-29): OpenF1 is the oracle and the v1 source. Each rule below says its origin.
  - **Licence:** OpenF1's code (`br-g/openf1`) is CC BY-NC-SA 4.0. It was read at `a33391239e4e` for behaviour only and is never copied: no code, no comments. The rules below are our wording of observed behaviour.
  - **Session gate:**
    - A feed joined before a session carries the previous session's full state until F1 switches: SessionInfo `Key` and `Type`, `NumberOfLaps`, Stints, and race control including the chequered flag. In Mexico FP1 (11397) the three `Subscribe` states held the Austin race, 11396.
    - The switch is a `_kf` keyframe: SessionInfo with the new `Key` first, then TimingAppData with empty Stints and TimingData lines without `NumberOfLaps`. It came about 12 min before the start in 11397 FP1.
    - `createLaps` counts only events from a SessionInfo with its own `Key` until one with another `Key`. `parseFeedLine` puts SessionInfo first in a `Subscribe` state, so the gate opens before the other topics are read. Without the gate the first FP1 run matched 40.16%.
    - A race is a session of `Type` "Race", sprints included (OpenF1 behaviour).
  - **Lap numbering and start:**
    - A driver seen for the first time has lap 1 (except a mid-session start, below). A `NumberOfLaps` count above the current lap's number starts a new lap, without filling gaps. In a race the lap is count + 1, because the start crosses no line. A count that is no integer skips the rest of that driver line. (OpenF1 behaviour; FastF1's own lap counter is not used.)
    - **Mid-session start** (#95, owner 2026-11-12): a driver first seen in a `Subscribe` state (a `full` event) whose line carries a count naming lap n ≥ 1 (race: count + 1) starts at lap n, with `date_start` null; a race joined on lap 1 takes the last `Started` instead, as the race lap 1 rule below does. That line's end-of-lap values (the lap time, S2, S3 and their segments: another lap's) are dropped. S1, its segments and the speed traps go to lap n, and a stale one is overwritten once lap n sets its own. Its `PitOut` marks lap n only when `true`, because a state always carries one. An honest gap (A§2.4) instead of a phantom lap 1 with another lap's time. Outside a race a driver joined on lap 1 keeps the whole line and the usual `PitOut` rule, only without a `date_start`: no lap came before lap 1, so the values are its own. 11408's second directory opens with all 20 drivers in the garage at count 1, and dropping the values there cost 11 of 391 laps against OpenF1; keeping them gives 391 of 391, with 19 `date_start`s null ([box] 2026-11-13). Such a line brings a sector's segments at once, so the lap shows the whole list. OpenF1 built the lap from deltas and puts a row out only on a sector's first segment, so it keeps that first status alone, unless a later time puts the row out again (11408: S3 segments on 18 lap 1s; `main` gives the same). A race keeps the stricter rule; no recording has joined one on lap 1. Only a state triggers it: a driver's first delta is its pit exit, which already carries its first count (`{InPit: false, PitOut: true, NumberOfLaps: 1}`). It relies on TimingAppData coming before TimingData in a state (the `Subscribe` request order, which F1 keeps), so the stint gate is open when the state's lines are read.
    - A lap's `date_start` is the time of the first count that names it. Only `NumberOfLaps` sets it, and `Started` in a race (below).
    - Laps ignore TimingData until a TimingAppData line has non-empty Stints (OpenF1 behaviour). Stints are not gated.
  - **Times:**
    - An end-of-lap value (lap time, S2, S3 and their segments) that arrives less than 10 s after the current lap started belongs to the lap before, and is dropped when there is none. S1 and its segments go to the current lap. (OpenF1 behaviour; FastF1's 5 s window is not used.)
    - A sector is read only as an object keyed 0 to 2: the list form carries no values (FastF1 and OpenF1). Its value must be plain seconds (`33.456`); `1:05.123` is no value (OpenF1). A time over one day is no value.
    - A new sector time fills a missing lap time with the sum of the three sectors, when all three exist (OpenF1 behaviour; FastF1's checks on it are not used).
    - Speed traps I1, I2 and ST go to the current lap; FL is not stored (OpenF1).
    - Any `PitOut` but null marks the current lap as an out-lap, `false` included (OpenF1 behaviour; FastF1 ignores `PitOut`). In-laps come from `markPitIn`, shared with the ingest (A§9).
  - **Segments:** the first segment status of a sector on a lap creates the list (holes are null) and counts as a change. Later ones are written in place and do not count as a change (OpenF1).
  - **Race lap 1:**
    - A `Started` status in SessionData gives every driver whose only lap is lap 1 its `date_start`, at the status's `Utc`, overwriting (OpenF1). A 2018–2019 archive has no SessionData; its rebuild from the SessionStatus topic gives the same `Started` (A§6.2). A lap 1 that is created after that `Started` also starts there, with the archive source only (P3a): a 2018–2019 archive sends no driver data before the start, so all 20 lap 1 of the 2019 race 5498 were created after it (checked on 5498), and lap 2's `firstLap` then derives lap 1's time and S1 as before.
    - When lap 2 starts: a missing lap time is lap 2's start minus lap 1's, then a missing S1 is the lap time minus S2 and S3. Both only when positive. They are set directly and do not mark lap 1 (OpenF1).
  - **Chequered flag:** the time of the event whose race-control text equals "CHEQUERED FLAG" (the whole text, any case), the last one winning. A race lap that started after it never shows (OpenF1).
  - **When a row shows:** a lap's row is its copy as of the last time it is emitted: after each driver line of a TimingData message, for the laps with a changed value (OpenF1). So a value written in place later (a segment) or set directly (race lap 1) shows only once the lap changes again.
  - **Stints** (OpenF1 behaviour):
    - A driver's first TimingData line creates stint 1, and `NumberOfLaps` is the last stint's `lap_end`, raw (no race +1).
    - TimingAppData: the key + 1 is the stint number, in key order as text. A list (the feed's form at the start of a session) all goes to stint 1, where FastF1 numbers it by position.
    - The compound is taken when given. `tyre_age_at_start` takes every `StartLaps` but null, the latest winning, and `TotalLaps` only while it is still null.
    - A new stint starts at the last stint's `lap_end` + 1. When it comes less than 10 s after the driver's last `NumberOfLaps`, that lap is the new stint's and the last stint ends one lap earlier.
    - A stint shows from its first changed value, with its current values.
  - **Progressive colours** (`progressiveColours`, `apps/server/src/timing/colours.ts`): the tower colours as of the moment the caller shows. Per field the values go in the order they were set (`at` on each reconstructed row; ties by driver, then lap; a value with no time goes last). A value of a deleted lap, out-lap or in-lap is yellow. Otherwise strictly below every earlier eligible value in the session: purple (the first to set a time holds it); strictly below the driver's own earlier eligible values: green; else yellow. No value: null.
  - **Timebase:** recording events use F1's own message timestamp, which equals OpenF1's `date_start` exactly (0 ms on 11397). Archive events used t0 from the first Heartbeat (A§6.2), which ran late against OpenF1 by +2021, +2018 and +2239 ms on 11392, 11397 and 11404. **Since P3a (owner decision O3, 2026-11-10) they use the SessionData t0 (A§6.2), which is those three offsets exactly, so the archive's timebase is OpenF1's;** ExtrapolatedClock (equal to it to the ms, O4) and then the Heartbeat are only the fallbacks. OpenF1 takes its archive t0 from the `.z` topics. Re-run numbers: limit B below.
  - **The oracle has holes.** OpenF1 serves live-ingested rows. In race 11404 it lost about 30–45 s of messages around 20:08:2x–20:09:06 UTC, which gave 9 drivers' lap 5 merged into their lap 4, a null S2 or S1 on lap 4 for 13 drivers, and a compound stuck at UNKNOWN for one driver. The F1 archive and our three recordings agree with each other there. An OpenF1 mismatch needs the archive as referee.
- **[box] results** (`scripts/compare-laps.ts`, laps within 1 ms; the bar is 99%):
  - 11392 Austin qualifying (archive, 2026-10-30): 280 of 280, stints 90 of 90.
  - 11397 Mexico FP1 (three recordings and the archive, 2026-10-30): 589 of 589 each, stints 92 of 92 each. No difference in pit flags, speeds or segments.
  - 11404 Mexico race (2026-11-02): each recording 1338 of 1369 (97.74%) raw and 1338 of 1338 (100%) without the OpenF1-gap class. The archive 1316 of 1369 raw and 100% without the OpenF1-gap class and the lap-1 timebase class (limit B). Re-run 2026-11-13 (#95, the native-token recording): 1356 of 1369 (99.05%), stints 80 and 80 with one difference (the UNKNOWN compound). Our rows are identical to `main`'s, so OpenF1 has since refilled part of its gap; the 13 misses left are its null sectors on lap 4.
  - **Owner decision (2026-11-02):** the bar excludes an OpenF1-defect class that the archive referees.
- **Known limits** (owner decisions, 2026-11-02):
  - **(B) An archived race's lap 1 was about 2.2 s long. Fixed in P3a** (owner decision O3, 2026-11-10). Re-run (`compare-laps` on the 2026 archives, after the fix): 11392 280 of 280 (100%), 11397 589 of 589 (100%), 11404 1338 of 1369, where all 31 misses are class A (OpenF1's live-ingest gap on laps 4–5) and lap 1 now matches; before the fix 11404 was 1316 of 1369, with 22 extra lap-1 mismatches from the Heartbeat t0. Lap 1's start comes from the absolute `Started` Utc, while later laps used the Heartbeat t0. The 2.2 s landed in lap 1 and its S1 (22 laps on 11404) and could shift lap-1 segment statuses through the 10 s window. The SessionData t0 (A§6.2) is OpenF1's timebase to the ms, with no `.z` download, so both now sit on one clock.
  - **(C) A lap start can carry a Subscribe state's receive time.** When the start was first seen in a Subscribe state, its date is that state's receive time (+1.7 to +4.7 s on 4 laps in one recording).
  - **(D) A Subscribe state can roll back newer values.** The state can be older than messages that arrived on the new connection just before its completion, and merging it afterwards rolls those values back (seen as one segment status). A fix needs message ordering: a follow-up, kept out of P2d (owner, 2026-11-04).
  - **(E) A `_kf` keyframe is merged as a delta**, so the previous session's lines stay in the merged state (32 TimingData lines instead of 22 right after the 11397 switch). Lap rows do not depend on it; typed views and a later full state's effective deltas do. **Fixed for the playback stream in P2e** (owner, 2026-11-05; the switch reset below). `createFeed` itself is unchanged: moving the rule there is still open. An archive stream holds only its own session (`SessionInfo.Key` is constant from line 1), so E does not affect the archive job's imports.
- **Playback (ADR-0007; P2d, amended P2e; `apps/server/src/live/playback.ts`):** the client asks for "state at session time T". The server replays the recording from its first line up to T through one `createFeed` and one `createLaps` (`recording.sessionKey`, source `f1-live`), sends one `snapshot` SSE event, then streams `delta` and `laps` events at `speed`. The route and its bounds are in A§10.
  - Live: T = now − delay. Replay: T = `at` + elapsed wall time × `speed`.
  - Pausing or seeking = a new SSE request. Moving the live delay is one only when it goes below the open stream's own delay (#101, A§13): above it the page holds the open stream's events instead.
  - Recordings stay replayable forever, in sync with the F1 TV replay.
- **Time axis** (P2d): the line's `receivedAtMs` (element 0), which is also the clock of the recorder's snapshot names. `at` is epoch ms on that axis, written `at=<ms>|live`, and so is every event's `t`. `FeedEvent.t` (F1's own timestamp) is used only to recognise duplicates.
- **The seek replays from the first line** (P2e, owner decision 2026-11-05; ADR-0007 amended). Playback no longer reads snapshot files and no longer primes the duplicate filter: one `createFeed` sees every line, so a reconnect's duplicate is dropped exactly as in the recorder's own merge. The recorder is unchanged (it still writes snapshots). Why: a P2d snapshot holds no lap history, so the nearest-snapshot seek (snapshot + 10 min of priming) gave only the current state, and a lap table could not be rebuilt from it.
  - **Cost** (local P2a recordings, WSL, `main`'s parser; counts and timings only): a full replay in process takes 1.1 s for the 4 h Mexico race (11404: 99k lines, 1369 laps) and 0.37 s for FP1 (11397). Over HTTP through the local `api`, the first complete snapshot arrives after 0.98–1.20 s at the race's end (641 KB, of which lap rows 541 KB; 1369 laps, 80 stints) and after 47–61 ms at its start. A `ponytail:` note in the code names the ceiling: a full scan and about 0.65 MB of uncompressed SSE per open for a full race (Caddy leaves the route uncompressed), bounded by the 8-stream cap. The VM has 1 CPU, so its time is unmeasured. Each committed paused seek is now a still-frame open (P4e), so the same full-scan snapshot per open; the 400 ms debounce and one stream per tab bound it. A live delay change costs no open unless it goes below the open stream's delay (#101, the delay slider below). Fixes if it hurts: a per-recording scan cache, slimmer or compressed lap rows. **Measured per open since #101:** the api's `playback open` line (A§10) gives `scan_ms`, `snapshot_bytes`, `lines` and `mode` for every stream, so P2f's [box] reads the VM's own numbers. [box] after a weekend (owner; p50 and p90 per mode from those lines, counts only): not yet measured. The weather rows cost little (P5b, in process on WSL, `playback()` on the 11404 native-token recording at its last line, 3 runs each, 2026-11-09): before 641,322 bytes and 910–1,029 ms to the first snapshot; after 675,834 bytes (+34,512, 5.4 %; 207 weather rows of 34,501 bytes) and 896–1,013 ms, the same within noise. The 207 rows are the 209 messages after the switch less 2 Subscribe states that changed nothing.
  - **The session switch resets the state.** Before a `SessionInfo` whose `Key` differs from the state's current one is applied, the stream clears the state and the weather rows (below), and it sends a fresh `snapshot` instead of the delta. The laps engine keeps going (it holds the recording's session only and has its own gate, above). Evidence: FP1 11397's merged state ended with 32 TimingData/DriverList/TimingAppData lines for 22 drivers and 171 race-control messages instead of 67; with the reset it has 22/22/22 lines and 67 messages. The lap and stint rows are byte-identical with and without the reset on 11397 and 11404, so the [box] results above hold. This fixes limit E for the stream only. The unit test "a session switch clears the state" covers it with a synthetic switch.
  - **The weather rows** (P5b, owner, 2026-11-07): they come from the same one replay pass. Each applied `WeatherData` delta adds `weatherRow(view(state, 'WeatherData'), receivedAt)` with `withWeatherRow`, a new array that keeps at most the last 1,440 rows (P5b review: a hostile or corrupt recording with a flood of WeatherData lines would otherwise grow a stream's memory, its snapshot and the client's copy without bound; real sessions send about one a minute, above); the session-switch reset clears the rows together with the state; every `snapshot` (the seek, the back-pressure re-snapshot and the switch) carries them. The playback property covers it (the snapshot's weather at a random T equals a full replay; the client's rows from the deltas equal the server's, reconnect duplicates and Subscribe states with WeatherData, changed or not, included), as do the switch test and the stall test.
  - Measured on the 2026 Mexico race recording: its red flag arrives only in `SessionData.StatusSeries` (Aborted, TrackStatus Red; no RC `Flag: RED`), and after the switch its series still holds a `Started` from the day before; markers therefore come from the series alone and are drawn only inside the recording.
  - **StatusSeries words and incident messages** (P4e, owner's machine, 2026-11-08; counts and enum words only, nothing copied): the `TrackStatus` words of `SessionData.StatusSeries` match the TrackStatus topic's `Message` one to one. Session 11392: AllClear 1, Yellow 1. 11397: AllClear 3, Yellow 4, VSCDeployed 1, VSCEnding 1. 11404: AllClear 10, Yellow 8, SCDeployed 3, Red 1, VSCDeployed 1, VSCEnding 1. The order in 11404 is … SCDeployed, `SessionStatus` Aborted, `TrackStatus` Red, AllClear (2 min later), Yellow, AllClear, `SessionStatus` Started (the restart, 39 min after the red flag), SCDeployed, AllClear …: the TrackStatus clears 37 min before the restart while the session stays Aborted. So (P4e review) a red-flag band runs from the first of Aborted and Red to the next Started or Finished, Aborted closes an SC band, a red flag without Aborted (practice) ends at the next TrackStatus outside Red, and VSCEnding belongs to the VSC band (A§13 Markers). In RaceControl each incident opens with one `… INCIDENT INVOLVING … NOTED …` message (13 in 11404), followed by UNDER INVESTIGATION, WILL BE INVESTIGATED, NO FURTHER ACTION and PENALTY messages for the same incident, so the NOTED message counts the incident once.
  - **The `laps` event:** after each applied line the stream marks a TimingData, TimingAppData, RaceControlMessages or SessionData change (`LAP_TOPICS`); at most once per second of wall time, and after the last line of a `receivedAt`, it runs `createLaps`' rows through `progressiveColours` and sends the rows whose JSON differs from the last send (a `Map` of row key → JSON). A `snapshot` (the seek, the back-pressure re-snapshot and a switch) resets that map and carries every row. The pending rows are flushed before `end`. A lap's compound is that of the driver's stint covering it (the later of two); `seg1`–`seg3` are null.
  - **A§9 on the stream rows** (#95): `lapRows` runs `markLaps` on the engine's rows with the state at T, when that state is the recording's own session (its `SessionInfo.Key`), so `deleted`, `deleted_reason` and `quali_phase` are set as on the session page. The inputs are every `RaceControlMessages.Messages` entry at its `Utc` (read by `utc`: the feed's has no Z), `SessionInfo.Type`, and the `SessionData.Series` `QualifyingPart` changes (`qualifyingParts` in `laps.ts`, shared with the archive's rows) as message-less phase rows, in a session of type `Qualifying` only: after a switch the series can still hold the previous day's entries (above), and A§9 gives practice and races null. Spoiler-safe: the state at T holds only the messages aired by T, so a lap is struck from its ruling's line on and a `REINSTATED` restores it; `progressiveColours` then skips the deleted lap. RaceControlMessages and SessionData changes mark the rows for the next `laps` event too, so a ruling shows within a second even when TimingData is quiet.
- **Streaming:**
  - **Heartbeat:** the event `ping` `{t}` (T at that moment) after 15 s without a write, under Cloudflare's 100 s idle limit. It was the comment `: ping` until the P2e review; a comment never reaches the page's JavaScript.
  - **Back-pressure:** the stream is an async generator, so a write that has not drained stops it advancing. Once a resumed write finds the next line over 5 s of wall time behind, the lines up to T are applied silently and one fresh `snapshot` is sent. Nothing queues: the memory per stream is one state, the laps engine's rows and one read chunk.
  - **Tail:** at the end of `feed.jsonl` a 1 s poll. A rotation is detected by inode. Plain segments win over `.gz`, with a `.gz` fallback when the plain file vanishes between the listing and the open, and no segment is read twice.
  - **End:** `end` is sent once `ended_at` is set (checked at most every 30 s) and a final drain is done.
  - **Rotation:** a seek that finds the recording mid-rotation (the plain segment renamed to `.gz`, the next not yet created) waits for the new segment, as the tail does, instead of ending early.
  - **Limits:** a feed line is at most 8 MiB; beyond it the stream ends and logs the code `playback_too_large`. (The 16 MiB snapshot limit went with the snapshot load in P2e.) Recording files open with `O_NOFOLLOW`: a symlink is refused.
  - **Disconnect:** the client closing aborts the generator, whose `finally` closes the file handle and gunzip and clears the timers. Shutdown calls `server.closeAllConnections()` after `server.close()`, which would otherwise wait for open streams.
- **`CarData.z` and `Position.z` are not streamed** (owner, 2026-11-04): they are never applied, sent or kept in a stream's state. P3 can add them back behind a parameter. The same goes for the pit topics (`UNPARSED` in `playback.ts`, #96) until a pit-stop view parses them.
- **Known and left** (owner, 2026-11-04): a reconnect's `Subscribe` state can roll back deltas that arrived just before it. It stays a follow-up, not part of P2d.
- **Known and left** (#95): after a switch to a *later* session inside the same recording, the stream reads A§9 only from the recording's own session, so the earlier session's rulings and phases fall off its rows (the later session's never strike them). The recorder stops at `Ends` + 30 min and F1's switch came about 12 min before its session (11397, above), so it takes two sessions closer than about 45 min. Keep the session's last own rulings if a recording ever shows one.
- **Delay slider (live only):** 0–120 s in 1 s steps plus ±1 / ±5 / ±30 s buttons, remembered per device in `localStorage` (`f1.delay`). Default 0 (the owner sets it). A replay ignores it, so its stored value never shifts a replay (owner, 2026-11-05).
  - **Without a reopen** (#101, ADR-0007 amended): a live stream keeps the delay it opened at, and the page holds each of its events for the shown delay minus that one, counted from the event's arrival (`openStream`'s `hold`, A§13). The server is unchanged: it paces the stream at its own T = now − delay, so the hold is the only client-side state, bounded by 120 s of one stream's events (over 20,000 held events the stream fails and reopens at the shown delay, holding nothing). Counting from the arrival, not from `t` against the phone's clock, keeps a phone whose clock is off from adding delay. A decrease below the stream's delay needs events the page has not got: one new stream at the new delay, after the 400 ms debounce. So syncing to F1 TV with nudges that add delay opens nothing, and each decrease past the open stream's delay opens one. [weekend] five nudges against F1 TV open at most one or two streams (owner): not yet checked.
- **Replay sync (P2):** the seek bar over [`started_at`, `ended_at`] with the session markers (A§13), plus ±1 / ±5 / ±30 s position nudges. The owner presses play on both the replay and the F1 TV replay, then nudges the position (the [owner] criterion: in sync after one adjustment within ±2 s for 10 min).

## 12. Security

### 12.1 Invariants

1. No secret in git, environment variables, image layers, logs or error messages. Secrets are files under `/etc/f1/secrets`, `root:<gid> 0440`, and only the consuming container has that GID.
2. Nothing is reachable without Cloudflare Access. The origin publishes only `127.0.0.1:8180` for the update smoke test, and `/api` checks the Access JWT itself.
   - **Offline cache on the device** (owner, 2026-11-13, #121, D1): the service worker (A§10, A§13) keeps the JSON of the final sessions the owner opened, readable on the phone without Access. Accepted, bounded: only final sessions (a `no-store` answer, a non-200 and a redirect are never stored), only the ones opened, and all of it dropped on the next release (each release is a new web build, A§13). The phone's lock is the guard. The HTTP cache already holds the same bodies for 24 h without Access (A§10).
     - **Accepted risks** (owner, 2026-11-13, #121; P-review):
       - The copy has no time limit of its own. It goes on the first online load, with a valid Access session, after the next release. Until then it stays, even if Access is later revoked.
       - Logging out of Access (`/cdn-cgi/access/logout`) does not clear it. Removing the home-screen app or clearing the site data does.
       - Removing the worker later needs a kill-switch `sw.js` that deletes the `f1-` caches and unregisters. Deleting the file leaves the old worker and its data in place.
       - A one-time script injection could persist in the cache only for offline loads (every path is network-first), until the next web build.
3. Data from the F1 TV feed, the archive or the FIA timing PDFs (owner, 2026-11-10) is never committed, and neither is the Jolpica dump. Public fixtures for those parsers are synthetic, and real files stay in the gitignored `fixtures-private/` folder and on the VM.
4. CI never calls OpenF1, F1, Jolpica, the FIA, Open-Meteo, Overpass or hc-ping.com.
5. The F1 stack shares nothing with Waterheight: networks, volumes, secrets, GIDs, ports, subnets, the nftables table, timers and the healthchecks group are all separate. Nothing touches `/etc/rws`, `/srv/rws` or the `rws` project.
6. Strict CSP as in Waterheight: `default-src 'none'; script-src 'self'; style-src 'self'; img-src 'self' data:; connect-src 'self'; …`, with `worker-src 'self'` since #121 (A§13 pins the whole string). The browser makes no third-party requests (no driver headshots from formula1.com).
7. Containers are hardened: `read_only`, `cap_drop: ALL`, `no-new-privileges`, non-root UID in its own range, memory/CPU/PID limits.

- **Third-party map and rain data in the page** (owner, 2026-11-07, P5c; ADR-0008): the circuit outlines and the OpenStreetMap layers are static JSON bundled into the web build and drawn as SVG path attributes, and the rain frames come from our own `/api`. The browser makes no request to Open-Meteo, OpenStreetMap, GitHub or anyone else: the credits are plain `<a href rel="noreferrer">` links that fetch nothing. The `ingest` daemon is the only new outbound caller (A§14.3).

### 12.2 Cloudflare

- Its own tunnel, with its own `cloudflared` reaching only `caddy:80` (ADR-0004).
- An Access application on `<F1 hostname>`: an allow policy for the owner's email only, a session of 24 h–7 days, and email one-time PIN login.
- The `api` (`:8080`, inside the compose network only) checks the Access JWT itself (A§10). For that it makes outbound HTTPS to `<team>.cloudflareaccess.com` (Cloudflare's published ranges, A§14.3) for the team's certs, and reads the non-secret `CF_ACCESS_TEAM`/`CF_ACCESS_AUD` from `/etc/f1/f1.env`.

### 12.3 F1 TV credentials and token (ADR-0005)

- `f1tv_username` and `f1tv_password` are file secrets. The account has no 2FA, so automatic login works.
- The login is a TS port of FastF1's OAuth2 Authorization Code + PKCE flow against `id.formula1.com` (PingOne DaVinci, `response_mode=pi.flow`, all JSON calls). The access token's `subscriptionToken` claim is the Bearer token. It expires in ~4 days and has no refresh token.
- The token is cached in a file (0600, the live UID, written through a temporary name and a rename). On the host it is `/srv/f1/state/live/f1tv-token`; in the `live` container it is `/srv/f1/state/f1tv-token`, because only the subdirectory `/srv/f1/state/live` is mounted, as `/srv/f1/state` (owner, 2026-10-30). The state directory also holds `current.env`, which picks what root deploys, so a writable mount of all of it would let a compromised recorder choose that. The token itself is never logged; only its expiry is. The reader accepts only a regular file of at most 64 KiB with no group or other permission bits.
- **When it logs in** (owner, 2026-10-30): when a session starts within 48 h (or is running) and the token does not cover that session's end + 1 h. Not on a schedule, so a token that expired between weekends is no fault. The warning `token_expiring` shows when the token has under 24 h left, but only while a session is due within 48 h; `login_failed` shows on every failed login, whenever it happens.
- **Limits** (P2a security review, L2; P2c security review, M1): at most one login per 10 min, and one per 6 h after 3 failures in a row. A failure is a login that throws; one that returns a token that fails `checkToken`, equals the value the feed refused or still does not cover the session (`login_claims`; a token that is good for now is still saved and used); and one whose token the feed then answers with `feed_unauthorized` (which stays the last error). `login_failures` goes back to 0 only when a connection carrying a token that covers the session has subscribed, never when `login()` resolves, and that holds for a pasted token too. `login_failures` and `last_login_at` are in `recorder_status`, and the attempt is written before it starts, so a restart or a crash cannot turn into a sign-in storm. A failure sets the status and never exits the process. The login code sends the credentials only to `id.formula1.com`.
- A token the feed rejects (`feed_unauthorized`) is dropped, and the recorder goes on anonymously until the token file holds a different value.
- A token that cannot be saved (`token_file`) is no failed login: it stays in memory for this process, is used, and is written again every tick until that works or the file holds one that lasts as long. A token file that cannot be read for any reason but a missing file also shows `token_file`.
- **A second reader** (P3a): the `archive` job reads the same file, read-only (`/srv/f1/state/live` as `/srv/f1/state`), and has no F1 TV password. It sits on `f1-egress` too, so the accepted risk below covers the token in a second container.
- **Egress is limited by port, not by host:** `live` can reach tcp/443 on any public IPv4 (A§14.3). The F1 hostnames are fixed only in the code (`redirect: 'error'`, the origin check on the login's `href`), so code running in `live`, a malicious dependency of the shared server image say, could send the password to any public host on 443. Accepted (owner, 2026-10-31): a filter by host behind CDN ranges costs more than it protects here.
- Fallback if the login breaks (bot protection, a flow change): the owner pastes the `login-session` cookie value from formula1.com, or a raw token, into the token file (as undercut-f1 does; `docs/runbooks/update.md`). The recorder reads the file every tick (30 s). The status panel warns as above, and a wrong owner or mode of the file shows as `token_file`.

### 12.4 Agents and CI (P0 security review, 2026-10-25)

- **Trust anchors:** the lockfile, the hand-maintained sha256 pins of the CI binaries (Dependabot does not bump them), and the required checks, which the PR itself defines. A diff under `.github/`, `scripts/`, `pnpm-workspace.yaml` or `.claude/` gets a line-by-line owner read.
- **`main`:** branch protection requires `ci` and `security`, enforced for admins, with no force-push or deletion, and a pull request before merging (0 approvals). Merge commits only.
- **Agent identity:** agents use the owner's admin OAuth token until P1f, when they move to their own fine-grained token without the Administration permission (A8). Until then "agents never merge" rests on the deny rules in `.claude/settings.json` and on the branch protection. The A8 token has contents, pull requests and issues read/write and no Workflows permission, so the owner pushes any diff under `.github/workflows` (owner, 2026-10-28). Agents switch to it before the first deploy from `main`.
- **Secrets:** `db_f1_migrator` is the first one. It lives in the `migrate` process and in the dbmate child's environment (readable through `/proc/<pid>/environ` by the same UID in the one-shot container). `DATABASE_URL` overrides it in dev and test; Compose never sets it, which the `hardening` check in `ci / deploy` enforces.
- **CI egress (CI only):** GitHub release assets, registry.npmjs.org, Docker Hub, nodejs.org (setup-node), api.github.com (zizmor), gcr.io (distroless base image), ghcr.io (the `release` job pushes there), deb.debian.org and the Ubuntu archive (nftables in the nft-check container and on the runner). Production gains no host.
- **CI deploy test (`ci / deploy`, e2e):** the runner has Docker 28.0.4 (client and server) and the nft-check container has nftables 1.1.3 (Debian trixie); the VM runs Docker 29, so the [box] commands in `docs/runbooks/install.md` confirm there. The run proved: a second `f1-install` makes `0 change(s)`, `systemd-analyze verify` passes on the units, local tags (`f1-server:ci`) work as image refs through `current.env`, `api` → `https://www.google.com` is blocked (`TimeoutError`) with the f1 table loaded, a broken image rolls back and the known-bad pair is skipped, and `f1-tick` reloads a deleted table. Since #104 it also proves the alert paths without a request to hc-ping.com: the restart loop, the recorder watch's three codes once each and cleared again (step 11a), `OnFailure=` through the sandboxed `f1-alert@`, and an absent, empty or malformed key.
- **Accepted risks (owner, 2026-10-25):**
  - The `git diff *`, `git log *` and `git show *` allow rules stay, although `--output=<path>` lets them write a file without a prompt.
  - `npm`, `yarn` and `bunx`, `git push --prune` and `git -c … push` are not denied.
  - GitHub does not itself enforce SHA-pinned or allow-listed actions; `scripts/check-workflows.sh` and zizmor do, in files a PR can change.

### 12.5 Deploy path (P1f security review, 2026-10-29)

- **GHCR write access is code execution on the box:** `f1-update` runs whatever `:main` is, as containers with the db and its secrets. Hence the `production` environment on the `release` job (required reviewer: the owner; deployment branches: `main`), and the A8 agent token without Workflows. The `release` job holds `packages: write` behind that gate. Agents cannot merge: branch protection, the deny rules in `.claude/settings.json` (`gh api …/merge`, `gh api graphql`, `gh auth token`) and the environment approval.
- **`cf4`** lets the `api` reach any Cloudflare-fronted host on 443. Accepted: the api only fetches the team's certs.
- **`127.0.0.1:8180`** is reachable by local VM users: it serves the static site and `/healthz`; the api still checks the Access JWT.
- **New secrets:** `cf_tunnel_token` and `hc_ping_key`. **New egress:** hc-ping.com (`ingest`, tcp/443) and tcp+udp/7844 to the tunnel ranges (`cloudflared`).
  - **P2f amendment (2026-11-06):** `cloudflared` is pinned to `--protocol http2` and the udp/7844 egress is removed (A§14.3), so the tunnel needs tcp/7844 only. No new host, port, secret or GID.
  - **P2f: a new trust edge.** The root deploy timer reads `recorder_status`, which a container writes, through `docker exec f1-db-1 psql` (the first host script that execs into a container). Data a container writes now steers a host decision: `ingest` and `live` share the `f1_ingest` login (O3), so a compromised one can hold deploys, and a wrong `date_end` from OpenF1 does the same through the recorder's hard stop. The bounds are root-side: the query takes only a row under 5 minutes old and not more than 1 minute in the future, and `f1-update` deploys anyway after 14 h of unbroken deferrals (`held-since` in `/srv/f1/state`, `error: deploys held for over 14 h …`). So O3's shared login can delay a deploy by 14 h at most, not for ever.
  - **P2f: patch latency.** While `live` records, a deploy waits (before: 10 minutes at most): until the recording ends, at most 14 h. `sudo docker stop f1-live-1` ends the recording at once (`complete = f`) and lets the next run deploy.
- **P2c:** new secrets `f1tv_username` and `f1tv_password` (`root:62007 0440` and `root:62008 0440`, `live` only). New egress for `live`: the code talks to `account.formula1.com` and `id.formula1.com` (the login), `livetiming.formula1.com` (negotiate and the WebSocket) and hc-ping.com, all tcp/443 on `f1-egress`. The firewall does not hold it to those hosts: it allows tcp/443 to any public IPv4 (A§12.3, A§14.3), so there is no nftables change. The `live` container mounts `/srv/f1/live` and `/srv/f1/state/live` only; `hardening.sh` pins that bind list exactly (the `api`'s one bind is below).
  - **Core dumps are off** on every service (`ulimits: core 0` in the hardening anchor, `no_core_dumps` in `hardening.sh`, `docker inspect` in e2e): a dump of `live` would hold the F1 TV password and token, and a piped `core_pattern` would put it on the host.
  - **`ingest` shares the login that writes `recorder_status` and `recording`** (owner decision O3), so a compromised `ingest` could reset the login limits. A CHECK holds `recording.path` to `<digits>-<digits>`, because `live` joins it to a directory at boot.
  - **`/srv/f1/live` has no retention** (recordings stay replayable forever, ADR-0007) but a 1 GiB floor (A§11.2). The floor bounds the disk; a large buffer is contained by `mem_limit`.
- **P2d:** the `api` now reads the recordings, read-only (A§14.2). They are F1 TV data, private but not secrets, and the `api` is the only reader besides `live`. No new secret, egress host, port or privilege. The path comes from the `recording` row, never from the URL (A§10). The resource bounds: at most 8 streams per process, a speed of at most 16, and a memory per stream bounded by back-pressure (A§11.3). Error bodies are fixed, and the SSE route has no `onError`, because hono's `streamSSE` would write the error's message to the client.
  - **A new file trust boundary:** the `api` parses files that `live` wrote (the feed lines; since P2e not the snapshots). A compromised `live` could write hostile files, so the parsing is bounded by the limit of A§11.3 (a line 8 MiB) and the files open with `O_NOFOLLOW`.
  - **Accepted Low, stream lifetime:** the Access JWT is checked at connect only, and a stream is neither capped in lifetime nor ended at the JWT's `exp`. Accepted: the owner is the only Access user. A stalled client is bounded by the 60 s write timeout (A§10).
- **P2e:** no new secret, egress host, port or privilege, and the CSP is unchanged (the web has no new third-party request; the stream is a same-origin `EventSource` to `/api`, behind Access). The `api` now replays a recording from its first line on every stream open (A§11.3): a full scan per open, bounded by the 8-stream cap, the 8 MiB line limit and the 60 s write timeout. `/api/v1/recordings` returns the `recording` table joined to session and meeting, behind the same Access check. The browser renders feed text (driver names, race-control messages, the track status) as text only, never as HTML.
- **P3a:** no new secret, GID, port, egress host (the job calls `livetiming.formula1.com/static` only, which `live` already uses) or CSP change.
  - **A new data store:** `/srv/f1/archive` (62532, 0700) holds F1 TV-derived files in plain text, with no retention. The job's 5 GiB free-disk floor bounds the disk. Its memory is bounded per session, not by the 64 MiB file cap (10 such files would be 640 MiB): a session over 16 MiB of topic files fails as `too_large` before any is read (A§6.2).
  - **A third holder of `f1_ingest`:** after `ingest` and `live` (O3), `archive` holds the login too, so code that parses remote archive data could write `recorder_status` (the P2f deploy-hold edge, bounded at 14 h). The F1 TV token is now read by two containers (A§12.3).
- **#105:** `ingest_status` is one more table that every holder of `f1_ingest` (`ingest`, `live`, `archive`) can write. It is display only, and no host decision reads it (unlike `recorder_status`, above). No new secret, GID, port or egress host: `hc-ping.com` is already allowed.
- **#104** (owner, 2026-11-09; D1, D2 2026-11-14): host alerts through `f1-ingest/fail` (A§14.5). The edges:
  - **New host egress:** `hc-ping.com`, tcp/443, from root's `curl` in `f1-alert`. `table inet f1` has no output hook, so it does not filter host output (A§14.3). **[U]** on the VM: the host shares Waterheight's network, so the first `curl https://hc-ping.com/` from the host proves it (`docs/runbooks/install.md`, A6). If the host cannot reach it, the fix stays out of Waterheight's firewall (invariant 5); until then a failing alert path shows only as `alert_failed` in the journal and alerts nothing.
  - **Root reads `hc_ping_key`** (`root:62006 0440`, through the owner bits) and sends it only from `f1-alert`; `f1-update`'s readiness check and `f1-install` read it through `secret_matches` without printing it. The key reaches `curl` through a pipe (`--config -`), never argv, the environment, a file or a log. A missing, empty or malformed key (not 22 characters of `A-Za-z0-9_-`) sends nothing (`alert_no_key`, `alert_key_invalid`). The log holds fixed codes and the body only.
  - **`f1-alert@.service`** (the `OnFailure=` template) runs as root with `CapabilityBoundingSet=` empty, `ProtectSystem=strict`, `ProtectHome=yes`, `PrivateTmp`, `PrivateDevices`, `ProtectKernelTunables`, `ProtectKernelModules`, `ProtectControlGroups`, `RestrictAddressFamilies=AF_UNIX AF_INET AF_INET6 AF_NETLINK`, `NoNewPrivileges` and `UMask=0077` (`hardening.sh`'s `alert_unit_sandbox` asserts the empty bounding set, `ProtectSystem=strict`, `NoNewPrivileges` and the address families). Capability-less root is no containment boundary by itself (UID 0 keeps its owner rights, such as on the Docker socket), hence the extra limits; it never drives Docker. The direct path, `f1-update` and `f1-tick` calling `f1-alert`, runs `curl` as full root (accepted: TLS to hc-ping.com, the body to `/dev/null`, no redirects, and `env -i`, so no proxy or `SSLKEYLOGFILE` variable of the caller applies). It writes nothing, and `f1-alert` accepts a code of `^[a-z][a-z0-9_]{0,31}$` and a detail of `^[A-Za-z0-9_.@-]{1,64}$`, else exit 64.
  - **A new trust edge:** the recorder watch (`/etc/f1/recorder-watch.sql`, run by `f1-tick` through `docker exec f1-db-1 psql` in a read-only transaction) reads rows that containers write: `recorder_status`, `recording` and `session`, all through `f1_ingest` (A§14.5). A compromised holder of that login can trigger or mute `recorder_missing`, `recorder_stale` and `login_failed`, and nothing else: root takes only those three codes and a numeric session key from the output, so no row can name another code, a body or a command, and root takes no other action. The watch (and `f1-update`'s hold query) runs as `f1_api`, which may only SELECT, with a 15 s `statement_timeout` in the server (the 20 s `timeout` kills only the docker CLI). Each code alerts once per session key, so a compromised holder of `f1_ingest` can send at most three alerts per tick by rotating session keys.
  - **Accepted edge, a shared key:** `ingest` and `live` hold the same ping key and tcp/443, so a compromised container can send any body to `/fail`, or a success ping that turns `f1-ingest` green over a host alert. Host alerts are only as trustworthy as those containers (the same held for #105's codes). A separate check with a root-only key would close it; the owner chose no third check (2026-11-09).
- **Root:** the systemd units that drive Docker as root are `f1-firewall`, `f1-update` and `f1-tick`. Since #104 `f1-alert@` (the `OnFailure=` template of the last two) runs as sandboxed root too, but never drives Docker. Their scripts are root-owned copies in `/usr/local/bin`; the timers never run a file of the checkout.

## 13. Frontend

- **Routes** (no router library; `routeOf(location.pathname)` plus `history.pushState`, as in Waterheight; `apps/web/src/lib/routes.ts`): `/` (the start, below) · `/:year` · `/:year/:meeting/:session/{laps|order|compare|minisectors|stints|weather}` (`weather` since P5a, owner, 2026-11-07; `order` since #115, owner, 2026-11-13) · `/status` · `/live` · `/replay` · `/replay/:recording` (`/replay` added in P4c). (owner, 2026-11-07, P4e): `/replay/:recording` takes an optional `?t=HH:mm:ss`. `routeOf` keeps the path rules and ignores the query; the query belongs to the player (URL state, below).
  - A meeting or session segment is `<key>-<name>`, such as `/2026/1290-singapore/9965-race/laps`, and parses as `^(\d{1,9})(?:-[a-z0-9-]*)?$`. The key decides; the name is decoration (lower case, diacritics stripped, other runs → `-`; meetings drop a trailing ` Grand Prix`). Once the session is loaded, a wrong or missing name, year or meeting key is replaced by the canonical URL (`history.replaceState`). `/:year/:meeting/:session` goes to `…/laps`.
  - **`?d=`** (owner, 2026-11-07, P4f): the one query the routes know. It belongs to the session view's `compare` and `minisectors` and holds the picked drivers (Driver picker below). `routeOf` still reads the pathname only; the canonical-URL `replace()` keeps `location.search`, and `navigate()` moves focus to the h1 only when the path without its query changes.
  - **`/`** (owner, 2026-11-07, P4c, D1): goes to `/live` while `/live` (A§10) names a recording with `ended_at` null; else to `/:year` of the newest season whose `/seasons` row counts a non-cancelled session; else "No seasons yet.". It never lands on a lap table, so a result is not the first thing on screen. It waits for `/seasons` and `/live` (an error in `/live` counts as nothing open) and redirects with `replace()`. This replaces the old walk to the last started session's lap table. It never jumps to next year during a season: the schedule sync (A§8) lists a year only from 1 January, as it syncs up to the current UTC year. The trade-off: while a recording is open (until `SessionStatus: Ends` + 30 min, A§11.2), `/` opens `/live`, whose tower is in running order; that is the owner's rule (D1), and it is not a lap table.
  - **Header** (owner, 2026-11-07, P4c; `views/Header.tsx`): a session switcher (`nav` "Session switcher"): ‹ previous and › next session (the season's non-cancelled sessions by `date_start`, then `session_key`, across meetings, keeping the current view; absent at either end of the season); the season as year links in a native `popover="auto"` (`ui/Menu.tsx`, opened under its button, see Popovers; Escape and light dismiss are native, a link click hides it); on a session route also the meeting menu (each meeting links to its session of the same name, else its first non-cancelled one, keeping the view; a cancelled meeting is struck through and not linked) and the session menu. Then the links Live · Replays · Status, always mounted, with `aria-current="page"` on their own route. This replaces the season `<select>` and the links `Live` and `Status`. (owner, 2026-11-13, #100): on `/live` and `/replay/:recording` the switcher shows the player's session (`playerSession`: the replay's `/recordings` row, or the row of the recording `/live` names; `/recordings` is asked on those two routes only, and on `/live` once more for a recording id `/live` names that the loaded list lacks, one that started after it was read), with ‹ › and the menus to its Laps. The header polls `/live` every 30 s and `/status` every 60 s on every page (their errors show nothing): while `/live` names a recording with `ended_at` null the Live link is the player's red pill (a dot, "Live" and the words ", recording now" for screen readers, D1); while the recorder has a `warning` or is `stale`, Status has an amber-bordered "WARNING" pill (`.pill.warn`) and its `title` is that warning's sentence (`recorderWarning` in `views/Status.tsx`).
  - **Popovers** (owner, 2026-11-08, #72; the `.pop` rule in `styles.css`): every popover (the header menus, the cell notes, the stints rows) opens at its `popovertarget` button, its implicit anchor (CSS anchor positioning): below it, left edges aligned, 4 px apart and 8 px clear of the window's edge, flipped to right-aligned and then above when the window has no room; a menu keeps scrolling inside its `max-height`. A browser without `position-area` keeps the browser's centred popover.
