  - Since P1e the `compare` and `minisectors` views exist, and since P2e `/live` and `/replay/:recording` (`^\d{1,9}$`); `/replay` (P4c) is the recordings list. The session tabs read Laps · Order · Compare · Mini-sectors · Stints · Weather (Weather since P5a, owner, 2026-11-07; Order since #115, owner, 2026-11-13, shown for `Race`-type sessions only, sprints included; on another session type its route says "The running order is for races and sprints.", and the weekend pills and the header's session switcher link such a session's Laps instead of Order). In the player, Order on a session that is not (or no longer) a race shows the Tower, its button pressed.
  - Links are real `<a href>`s; only a plain left click navigates in place, so a modified or middle click opens a new tab.
  - **Page titles** (owner, 2026-11-07, P4c; `titleOf` in `lib/routes.ts`): `document.title` is "… · F1 Timings" with names only, never a result: "2026", "<meeting> · <session> · <view>" (the year until the names load), "Status", "Live", "Replays", "Replay: <meeting> · <session>", "Not found".
  - **Focus on navigation** (owner, 2026-11-07, P4c; `useHeadingFocus`): after an in-app navigation (`navigate()`, a plain left click on a `Link`) focus moves to the page's h1 (every h1 has `tabIndex={-1}`), waiting for it to mount. Never on the first load, a `replace()` or back/forward; a pending focus survives a `replace()` redirect and lands on the redirected page.
- **Views:**
  - **Season** (`/:year`, owner, 2026-11-07, P4c; `views/Season.tsx`, `lib/season.ts`): spoiler-safe, no position, time or gap anywhere (a test asserts it). It requests `/seasons/:year`, `/live` and `/recordings` only, never `/sessions/:key`.
    - **This weekend** first: the meeting in progress (from its first non-cancelled session's start to its last one's end, and on to now while `/live` names an open recording of one of its sessions, so an overrunning race keeps it), else a meeting in its grace day (owner, 2026-11-13, #97, D1: a finished meeting stays until its last session's end + 24 h, so its replays stay in reach; a meeting in progress beats it), else the next to start, else none (after the last race, once the grace day is over, and on past seasons). Eyebrow "This weekend · Round N of M · country code", the meeting, and each session with its Amsterdam time, the track time beside it and its state in words, by precedence: cancelled (struck through, not linked) > live now · Follow (`/live`; `/live` names an open recording of this session, also after `date_end`) > recorded (the newest ended recording of it in `/recordings`) > laps ready (`ingest_state` `fetched` or `final`) > laps pending (started, none of the above; a default) > next (the season's next session to start) > scheduled. Every session of a cancelled meeting is cancelled. **Replay first** (owner, 2026-11-09, #97): the name of a recorded session links to `/replay/:recording` with a "Laps" link beside it, here and on the cards; any other session links its name to its laps, and "live now · Follow" is unchanged. The state of a recorded session reads just "recorded" (owner, 2026-11-13, #97, D2: the "· Replay" link beside it is gone, the name is the link).
    - **Up next:** a countdown (`fmtCountdown`: "M min", "H h M min", "D days H h", "now"), the session and an Open Live link; shown only when the next session belongs to this weekend, so during a grace day it is absent when the next session belongs to another meeting.
    - **The rest of the season** as cards in season order: round, country code, circuit, and every session with its time (on a second row, as a card is too narrow for one) and its state word (the same `sessionState` precedence and the same replay-first links as This weekend); a cancelled session (every one of a cancelled meeting) is struck through and not linked, without the visually hidden " (cancelled)" because the state word says it. The cancelled meeting's `h3` keeps the hidden word.
    - **States that move** (owner, 2026-11-13, #97): `Season` owns `now`, ticking every 60 s, and passes it to the pure `SeasonView`, so the states, This weekend, Up next and the countdown move together without a reload (a page left open across a session's start shows "live now" and moves Up next on). While the weekend has started (in progress or in its grace day; `started()` in `lib/season.ts`), `/seasons/:year` and `/recordings` refetch every 5 min (`refetchInterval`), `/recordings` too because a page left open through a session's end would otherwise show "laps pending" instead of "recorded"; otherwise neither refetches. The page still requests only those three endpoints and shows no result.
    - **Round numbers:** a meeting counts when it is not cancelled and has a non-cancelled session of type `Race` (a sprint weekend counts once; testing never), numbered by its first non-cancelled session's start. "Round N of M" with M the count; absent, never guessed, otherwise.
  - **Session head** (owner, 2026-11-07, P4c; `views/SessionHead.tsx`): an eyebrow (year · Round N when known · `meeting.location`), the meeting as the h1 with the session as a boxed tag (`.kind`), the meta line (start · circuit) with a Replay link to the session's newest recording when `/recordings` has one, and the weekend's sessions as pills (FP1, FP2, FP3, SQ, Sprint, Quali, Race; `shortName`) that keep the current view, `aria-current` on the current one, a cancelled one struck through and not linked. Since P5a (owner, 2026-11-07) a weather line sits under the meta line on every tab, from the Weather tab's own query (`/sessions/:key/weather`): "Air 24–27 °C · Track 38–45 °C · Dry", or "· Rain at times" when any sample had rain (`headLine` in `lib/weather.ts`; whole degrees, one value when both ends round the same, a part left out when unknown). It is absent while there are no rows, and when no sample has a temperature or a rainfall reading. (amended, #99, owner 2026-11-13) The meta line adds " · data final" for a `final` session and " · data provisional" once `fetch_count` > 0 and the session is not final (nothing before its first fetch), then " · Live now · Follow" (a link to `/live`) while `/live` names an open recording of this session.
  - **No data** (amended, #99, owner 2026-11-13) (`noDataText` in `lib/session.ts`, `ui/NoData.tsx`): a tab without data says why, by the first rule that holds, with "laps", "stints" or "weather" as the noun:
    1. cancelled: "No laps: the session was cancelled.";
    2. live now (`/live` names an open recording of this session): "Live now: follow it.", "follow it" a link to `/live`;
    3. after a fetch that succeeded (`fetch_count` > 0), whatever the state: "No laps in the OpenF1 data.", by `source` (as in 9); the head's "data provisional" says the rest (a later fetch, a back-off, a re-queue);
    4. in back-off (`due` with `fail_count` > 0, before any fetch succeeded): "The fetch failed (unexpected data); next try at 14:05." (`next_fetch_at` in Amsterdam time, `fmtHm`), "The fetch failed (unexpected data); a retry is due." once `next_fetch_at` ≤ now, "The fetch failed (unexpected data)." without one;
    5. `failed` (before any fetch succeeded): "The fetch failed 9 times (unexpected data) and is not retried.";
    6. `fetched` or `due` more than 24 h after `date_end` before any fetch succeeded (a backlog the tick has not reached, A§8): "Queued for a new fetch; check back later.";
    7. before `date_start`: "Not started yet. Laps arrive about 35 min after the end." ("Stints arrive…", "Weather arrives…");
    8. `scheduled` or `due`: "Laps arrive about 35 min after the end.";
    9. else (`final` without a fetch: the F1 archive, Jolpica): "No laps in the OpenF1 data.", by `source`: "the F1 archive", "the F1 live feed", "the Jolpica data".

    The error is fixed words per CHECK code (`errorWords`): "rate limited", "timed out", "network error", "unexpected data", "empty answer", "database error", "HTTP 503" for `http_503`, and "unknown error" for `unknown`, null or anything else; no server text reaches the page. Laps, Compare, Mini-sectors (with the laps' text) and Stints show it when the session has no laps, and Compare and Mini-sectors then show no driver picker (no dead end); Stints also when it has laps but no stints; Weather when it has no rows. The player's `LapTable` keeps "No laps yet.". The clock of the rules is the session query's `dataUpdatedAt`, so the text moves on with each poll (Refresh, below).
  - **Refresh** (amended, #99, owner 2026-11-13) (`pollMs` in `lib/session.ts`): only `/sessions/:key` polls, every 60 s while the session is not cancelled and is `scheduled`, `due` or `fetched` (`refetchInterval`, asked after each fetch; no bound on the clock), else not. When its `fetch_count` changes (not on the first answer the page sees), the page invalidates the session's laps, stints and weather once, so they refetch (`useRefetchOnFetch` in `lib/api.ts`); they never poll themselves, so a page left open shows each new fetch for one extra request each. `/live` (every 30 s) is asked only while the session is `scheduled` or `due` and not cancelled, which a live session always is (its first fetch comes after `date_end` + 35 min, and the tick waits during a live window, A§8); otherwise the page treats the session as not live, a cached `/live` answer included. `/rain` polls on its own rule (Rain map, below). A text that depends on the clock takes it from its query's `dataUpdatedAt`, not from `Date.now()` at render: a component re-renders on a poll only when what it reads changes, and reading `dataUpdatedAt` makes every refetch such a change.
  - **Lap table** (owner, 2026-10-28): a drivers × laps matrix with one time per cell. A segmented control `Lap | S1 | S2 | S3` picks the metric, and a toggle puts the drivers in rows (default: a sticky driver column with a 4 px team-colour bar, lap numbers as the header row) or in columns (a sticky lap column, drivers as the sticky header row). Both are component state, not URL state.
    - The colour is the cell's text colour, for the bests only: "other" values (the server's yellow) are plain text (owner, 2026-11-07, O1). Since P4b (owner, 2026-11-07) the cues are: other plain, purple boxed (a 2 px outline on a `--purple-wash` tint) and bold, green bold, in- and out-laps in `--dim`, a deleted lap struck through. Colour is never the only cue: a screen reader hears ", overall best" or ", personal best" after a ranked purple or green value, and a legend under the metric switch shows the styles ("overall best", "personal best", plain "other", in-lap with its IN badge, a new stint, deleted, and the SC/VSC or red-flag lap when the session has one; in the Δ view the four heat steps instead). On a ranked lap the cell's `title` names its colour (overall best, personal best, slower than personal best); a deleted lap, an in-lap or an out-lap has no such title, because the server colours all its values yellow without ranking them (A§9). The table has an accessible name per metric ("Lap times", "S1 times", …).
    - A deleted lap is struck through in every metric; a missing value shows `—`. The reason sits behind the cell in a tap popover opened at its cell (Popovers; `ui/Note.tsx`: a `<button popovertarget aria-describedby>` plus a native `popover`, so a tap works on a phone with no JS state; the `title` serves a hover). It is the deletion reason, or why the value is missing: an out-lap (S1, and the lap time on lap 1), an in-lap, race lap 1 from the grid, else "No time in the OpenF1 data". These are the cases the P1d null probe over every session since 2025 shows to be real.
    - PIT IN/OUT badges (owner, 2026-11-07, P4b): IN on an in-lap, OUT on an out-lap only where no compound dot starts the run (the dot already says the set is new). The compound dot (S/M/H/I/W, T for a test tyre; 2018's letters in **Compounds by season** below) marks the first lap of each run per driver (a new compound, or new tyres out of the pits), not every cell. It is `aria-hidden`, with "soft tyres, " ("test tyres, ") beside it for a screen reader and the season's name ("Soft", "Ultrasoft") as its title (the laps carry no tyre age, so no "used set" here). An unknown compound gets no dot. A run start after the driver's first lap also gets a 2 px `--line` rule on the cell's leading edge (left in rows mode, top in columns mode).
    - **Frozen columns** (owner, 2026-11-07, P4b): in rows mode the Driver column (72 px) and a Best column stay in view, Best sticky at `left: 72px` with `min-width: 112px` (80 px under 600 px, where its lap number is hidden and kept in the `title`). Best is the driver's own best valid value of the metric (not deleted, in or out, A§9) in the server's colour (purple, green or plain), with its lap number small beside it ("L12"); without one it is `—` with a reason behind a tap ("No valid lap time", "No valid S2 time"). Columns mode puts Best in a second `thead` row under the driver names; the whole `thead` sticks. `scroll-padding` keeps a focused cell clear of them: rows mode 190 px on the left (158 px under 600 px), columns mode 88 px on top.
    - **Track bands** (owner, 2026-11-07, P4b): the lap number in the header gets a 3 px band and its label (`SC`, `VSC`, `RED`) for each lap inside a period of `trackPeriods` (A§6.1): `--amber` for the safety car and VSC, `--live` for a red flag, on the left of the lap number in columns mode. Where periods overlap on a lap the most severe wins the band and the label (red, then safety car, then VSC), and the `title` lists every kind most severe first ("Safety car, virtual safety car"). The bands come from `race_control`, fetched for races and sprints only and not at all when the session has no laps; the table shows at once and gains its bands when they arrive, and a failed fetch says "Race control did not load: no SC, VSC or red-flag bands.". Rows in a period form without a lap number are listed under the table ("No lap number in the data, not banded:", time and message as text), never banded. A band marks the leader's lap (A§6.1), so a lapped car's cell can be a lap off.
    - **Times | Δ to own best** (owner, 2026-11-07, P4b; D3): a second segmented control, component state like the others. Δ replaces each value with its gap to the driver's own best for the metric, `+0.41` (two digits; three under 10 ms, `+0.003`, so a slower lap never reads `+0.00`), or "best" on that lap (boxed in its colour); a lap without a value keeps `—` and its reason, and a lap that is no valid time shows a word instead: "deleted", "in", "out", or "SC", "VSC" or "RED" on a banded lap. The cell's background takes one of four heat steps from the gap as a share of the own best (`--heat-1..3`): none below 0.5 %, step 1 below 1 %, step 2 below 2 %, step 3 at 2 % or more, the same for the lap and S1–S3. The text carries the value, so the step is never the only cue. The table is named "<metric> Δ to own best", and the legend shows the four steps ("< 0.5 %", "< 1 %", "< 2 %", "≥ 2 % slower than own best").
    - Drivers by result position (nulls last), then number; drivers without laps are left out. A session without laps says why (No data, above) (amended, #99, owner 2026-11-13).
  - **Session tiles** (owner, 2026-11-07, P4b; `views/Tiles.tsx`, `lib/bests.ts`): above the lap table on the Laps tab, six tiles in a grid (`dl.tiles`, "Session bests"): Fastest lap, Best S1, Best S2, Best S3, Sum of best sectors and Top speed. Each shows its value, then the holder and lap ("ANT · L55"), by A§9's own rules (valid laps only: not deleted, in or out; the same tie order as the purple colour), so a tile and the boxed cell name the same lap. The sum is the best S1 + S2 + S3 and is called "sum of best sectors", never "ideal lap", with its Δ to the fastest lap behind it ("+0.31 to the fastest lap"). Top speed is the largest speed trap `st_kph` on a valid lap, with its holder and "speed trap". A tile without a value shows `—` and a fixed reason behind a tap: "No valid lap with a time", "No valid S1 time" (S2, S3), "Needs a valid time in S1, S2 and S3", "No speed-trap reading on a valid lap".
  - **Qualifying by phase** (owner, 2026-11-07, P4b; D1 to D4; `views/QualiTable.tsx`, `lib/quali.ts`): for Qualifying-type sessions (sprint qualifying and the 2023 Sprint Shootout included, their phases named SQ1–SQ3) the Laps tab opens on a `By phase | Every lap` switch (component state, By phase first), Every lap being the lap table. Without any `quali_phase` it says "No qualifying phases in the data: see Every lap.". One row per driver of the results (and of the laps), one column per phase, then To pole and Timed laps.
    - **Cells:** the driver's best valid lap in that phase (by `quali_phase`, A§9), with a margin to the phase's cut-off in small text under it: `−0.212 inside`, `+0.105 out` or "on the cut-off". The phase's fastest time is boxed purple, with ", fastest of the phase" for a screen reader (`.vh`, #102). A `DEL` badge behind a tap lists the driver's deleted laps of that phase (time, lap and reason). Without a time the cell is `—` with a reason: "Knocked out in Q2" for a later phase, "No valid lap in Q2", or "No laps in the data" for a driver with no laps, who sorts last.
    - **Through rule** (D1): a driver went through phase p if they have any lap row with `quali_phase` p + 1, timed or not, so a Q3 crash on the out-lap counts. The limit: a driver who goes through but never leaves the garage shows as knocked out (a `ponytail:` note in `quali.ts`; the upgrade is the official classification).
    - **Cut-off:** the slowest best among the drivers who went through, with the count of them as the position ("Q1 cut-off (P16)"); nothing is hard-coded to 15, 16 or 10, so a field of 20 or 22 works. The last phase has none.
    - **Knock-outs** (D4): a 2 px `--fg` line under the last driver through each phase, with a label row (it keeps the 44 px row height) in the 12 px label style, "Q3 above · knocked out in Q2 below" (`--live` would break the token rule above). The rows are ordered by the highest phase reached, then by the best in it, then by result position, then by number. To pole is the driver's best in the last phase minus the pole time ("pole" for the pole sitter, else `—` with "Not in Q3"); before a third phase has laps the column is To fastest, against the last phase's fastest ("fastest", "Not in Q2"), because the three-phase format is assumed; Timed laps counts laps with a time that are neither in nor out, deleted ones included.
    - **Tiles** (D2): above the six session tiles, a "Qualifying" row with Pole (time, driver and phase; "Fastest in Q2", or SQ2, until a third phase has laps) and each phase's cut-off but the last ("Q1 cut-off (P16)", the holder's acronym; `—` "Known once Q2 has laps" before the next phase has laps).
  - **Driver picker** (owner, 2026-10-28; reworked owner, 2026-11-07, P4f): Compare and Mini-sectors share one selection, held by the session view (`views/Session.tsx`) in pick order and passed to both, so it survives a tab change. The first pick is Compare's reference. It defaults to the first two drivers of the lap-table order (fewer when the session has fewer) and resets on a session change (`App.tsx` keys the session view by its key) and on a reload, unless `?d=` names the picks. Compare with fewer than 2 drivers says "Pick at least 2 drivers."; Mini-sectors with none says "Pick a driver.".
    - **Picker** (`views/DriverPicker.tsx`): a `fieldset.picked` "Drivers" (Biome's `useSemanticElements` refuses a div with `role="group"`) of one chip per picked driver in pick order, each with the team bar, the acronym and a Remove button ("Remove VER"); on Compare only, the first chip also says "reference". Then "Add driver", a native `<details>` holding the full toggle grid, a `fieldset.picker` "All drivers" with one `aria-pressed` toggle per driver in the lap-table order, each with its acronym and team bar. The Remove buttons, the summary and the toggles are at least 44 px. A Reference choice on Compare (below) moves a driver to the front.
    - **`?d=`** (C4; `parseD` and `formatD` in `lib/routes.ts`): the picks in pick order as comma-joined driver numbers, the first the reference. It is read once, at mount. `parseD` drops unknown and repeated numbers and keeps the order; it returns null, meaning the default top two, for a missing or malformed value (`^\d+(,\d+)*$`), one over 100 characters, or one with no known number. It is written with `history.replaceState` (no history entry), on Compare and Mini-sectors only, and only once picks are chosen, by the user or from the URL: the default and a bad value write nothing. The canonical-URL redirect keeps it, and so do the Compare and Mini-sectors tab links while picks are chosen; the Laps and Stints links carry none.
  - **Order** (owner, 2026-11-09, #115; tab O3 owner, 2026-11-13; no positions chart O4; `views/Order.tsx`, `lib/crossings.ts`): who held each place at the line after each lap, from the lap rows' crossings (a driver's crossing after lap n is lap n + 1's `date_start`, else lap n's `date_start + lap_ms`, else unknown; A§6.1 gives the error of `date_start`). One table in `.scroll`, "Running order", a sticky lap column and a sticky head row (Lap, P1…Pn, n the widest row), one driver cell per place: the acronym after the 4 px team bar, compact (about 50 px, 44 px under 600 px). `runningOrder` ranks by laps completed, then by the crossing time, so a lapped or retired car falls behind by laps; a lap counts as completed once its crossing is known or the next lap has a row. Trailing rows that no driver completed are dropped: the lap a live replay is on (it would repeat the row before) and OpenF1's cool-down rows after the flag (A§6.1: 50 rows in 21 of 113 races, each a `date_start` without a `lap_ms`). A known limit: a finisher whose last lap has no lap time and no following row looks like a retirement (one lap down); it never happens on the dev DB (0 of 1,990 classified finishers, 2026-11-13). A car whose latest completed lap has no crossing is never ranked into a gap: its row's lap cell carries a note "k drivers without a crossing: order incomplete" ("1 driver"), the ranked cells are muted with the words ", order incomplete" for a screen reader, and one cell per such car follows, its acronym and `—` with the note "No crossing for VER after lap n". The first row, Grid, lists the drivers by grid position (integers from 1 only); without one its single cell says why ("Grid not in the OpenF1 data" on a session page, "No grid position in the feed" in the player), never the qualifying order. Lap 1's row also shows each driver's places gained (`lapOneGains`, grid − position; none when that row is incomplete or the driver has no grid position) with the tower's cue and words (▲ n, ▼ n, –; "gained n places", "lost n places", "1 place"). The caption reads "The order at the line, from the timing data. It can differ from the classification where a time penalty applies."; with no `date_start` in any lap the view says "No line crossings in this session's data.". In the player it is the third tab, on the stream's lap rows up to T, the DriverList's drivers (so the Grid row names a car that never set a lap) and `GridPos` from TimingAppData; on a session page the Grid row comes from `results[].grid` (#115, A§8; integers from 1 only, so Jolpica's pit-lane 0 takes no place).
  - **Comparison** (owner, 2026-10-28; redone owner, 2026-11-07, P4f): `div.cgrid`, two columns on wide screens and stacked under 640 px: the Lap times card, beside a side column of the gap chart's card and the duel's (the lap card fills its row, its chart growing from 20rem, so no empty band sits under it); then a Reference choice and the sector deltas. Race control (races only, the same query as the Laps tab, so it is cached) bands both charts once it arrives, and a failed load says "Race control did not load: no SC, VSC or red-flag bands.".
    - **Chart:** ECharts in a lazy chunk. `lib/echarts.ts` is the only runtime import of it, `views/Chart.tsx` its only importer, and the chart views `views/Compare.tsx` and, since P5a, `views/Weather.tsx` its only users (`build.test.ts` checks both; owner, 2026-11-07). The chunk also loads on the player's Weather tab, through `views/Weather.tsx` (P5b), so Compare and Weather are still the only importers of `Chart`. `Chart({ label, option })` loads it with `import()` and shows "Loading…" meanwhile, or "Chart failed to load: reload the page."; the canvas renderer, full width with its height from CSS (`.chart`); the lap chart and the gap chart share the one chunk. The option is a pure function (`views/lap-chart-option.ts`, `ChartTokens` from the CSS tokens `fg`, `muted`, `line`, `bg`, `amber`, `live` and, since P5a, `rain`, plus whether the light theme is on). The tokens are read when the chart starts and again whenever `data-theme` on `<html>` changes (`subscribeTheme`, a `MutationObserver`), so the header's moon or a system switch redraws a chart in place.
      - **Series:** one dense series per driver of `[lap, ms]` pairs over a value axis of laps (F2 below); a lap missing or without a time is null and breaks the line (`connectNulls: false`, `clip: true`). Every series has `symbol: 'none'` and `showAllSymbol: true`; the laps below override the symbol. The line has the team colour (else `--muted`; on the light theme `clampForLight`) and a line type (C2, below).
      - **Window** (a "Window" switch Fit | 107 %, component state, Fit by default): both start at `min = floor(best − 1 s)` to a whole second, where `best` is the fastest valid (purple or green) lap of the picked drivers. Fit (owner, 2026-11-07, P4f, C1) ends at `max = min + 4 s`; 107 % keeps its old end, 107 % of `best` ceiled to a second. The y axis ticks every second, labelled "1:33". A lap above `max` runs off the top and stays in the tooltip; without a valid lap the axis scales by itself.
      - **Symbols:** the own best (the lap coloured purple or green, as `DeltaTable`'s `bestOf`) is a 10 px circle in the team colour with a 2 px `--bg` ring (the stroke sits on the edge, so it reads as an 8 px dot inside a ring). A deleted lap is a 10 px circle filled with `--bg` and ringed 2 px in the line colour. A lap above the window is an 8 px triangle on the top edge, lifted by half its height (`symbolOffset`), in the driver's colour; it is a second series per driver, with a width-0 line, the driver's name (so the legend toggles both) and `clip: false`. A key above the chart reads "● own best · ○ deleted · ▲ above the window".
      - **ECharts 6.1 facts behind that** (checked in its source and by the 390 px render test): an `emptyCircle` is filled `#fff` (`tokens.color.neutral00`), a white disc on the dark theme, hence the circle filled with `--bg`; a `markArea` on a category axis runs from category centre to centre, so a one-lap period would have no width, hence the value axis (0.5 … last + 0.5, labels on whole laps only) and a band from `from − 0.5` to `to + 0.5`; `showAllSymbol: 'auto'` thins symbols only on a category axis, so no symbol is thinned on the value axis and `true` stays as a backstop; the line `symbolSize` default is 6 (the symbols here are 10 px and 8 px); `endLabel` plus the `LabelLayout` feature's `moveOverlap: 'shiftY'` keeps the end labels apart; and a markArea on a driver's series goes when the legend hides that driver, so the bands sit on their own series.
      - **Bands:** one `markArea` per `trackPeriods` period (A§6.1), from `from − 0.5` to `to + 0.5`: SC and VSC in `--amber`, red in `--live`, at 15 % opacity, labelled from `KIND_LABEL` (our own text, never data). They live on a data-less series of their own, silent and outside the legend and tooltip, so hiding a driver keeps them.
      - **Legend and labels:** the legend lists the driver names only (a driver's two series are one entry), 36 px wide so a dash shows, text in `--fg`. Each line ends in its acronym (`endLabel`, `--fg`, `moveOverlap: 'shiftY'`), inside a 48 px right margin, but only when the driver's last lap with a time is inside the window: ECharts draws an end label unclipped, so one at a lap above or below the window would sit off the plot (seen in the 390 px render test), and the legend still names the driver. The gap chart needs no such rule: its y axis scales to its data. The grid uses `outerBoundsMode: 'same'` and `outerBoundsContain: 'axisLabel'`, ECharts 6's replacement for the deprecated `containLabel`.
      - **Tooltip:** rich text, not HTML (`renderMode: 'richText'`), `confine`, the axis pointer snapping. "Lap 12", then per driver in pick order "VER 1:33.456"; for the others " · +0.321" (the Δ to the reference, when both laps have a time), " · Medium" (the compound in its season's words, none when unknown), and " · deleted, IN, OUT, above the window, below the window" for whichever apply (below: a deleted or invalid lap more than 1 s faster than the best valid lap, clipped with no edge marker); "VER —" without a time. `{`, `|` and `}` are stripped from data strings (rich-text markup).
      - **Line types** (C2, owner, 2026-11-07, P4f; `lineStyles`, shared with the gap chart): each driver's colour as drawn in the current theme (the team colour on dark, `clampForLight(team)` on light, `--muted` without a team colour). Two colours are close when ΔE < 15 (normal vision) or < 8 (deuteranopia or protanopia), in OKLab × 100 with Machado 2009 (`lib/colour.ts`). In pick order, a driver takes the first of solid, dashed and dotted that no earlier close driver uses; when all three are taken it takes the one at the number of close earlier drivers, modulo 3 (round again). This replaces "each repeat of a colour takes the next line type".
    - **Gap chart** (owner, 2026-11-07, P4f; `views/gap-chart-option.ts`): the card "Gap to VER", "seconds at each line crossing", one line per non-reference driver, each with its lap-chart colour and line type, on the same lap axis, bands and end labels. `gapFromCrossings(reference, driver)` gives the gap after lap n in ms at index n − 1. A driver's crossing after lap n is lap n+1's `date_start`, else lap n's `date_start + lap_ms`, else null; the gap is its crossing minus the reference's at the same lap count, so a lapped driver shows a gap of over one lap and its line ends at its own last lap. Nothing is interpolated: a null breaks the line. The y axis is in seconds, labelled "+10", "0", "−10" (positive: behind the reference); the tooltip reads "Lap 34", then "RUS +3.2 s". The accuracy is A§6.1's: the crossings agree with the official gap at the flag to a median of 73 ms, but not where a time penalty applies. (#102) The chart's accessible name (`gapChartLabel`) ends with each other driver's last known gap and its lap, in pick order: "Gap to VER: NOR +3.2 s at lap 57, PER −1.0 s at lap 57", and "NOR no gap" without a crossing, so how the gap ended is readable without the canvas.
    - **Duel** (owner, 2026-11-07, P4f, C7; `Duel` in `views/DeltaTable.tsx`): the card "Best laps, sector by sector", one block per non-reference driver. Its heading reads "RUS L41 1:21.316 vs VER L55 1:21.756" (the lap time coloured purple or green as the server coloured it, with ", overall best" or ", personal best" for a screen reader, #102; the other driver first). Then S1 to S3 rows, from `sectorDeltas` of the two best laps (the laps coloured purple or green, paired as lap 0): a bar grows from a centre line toward the faster driver, in that driver's colour (`teamStyle`, `--tc`), `--w = min(50, |Δ| / 300 ms × 50) %`, with the value from `fmtDelta`; a missing sector shows `—` and a zero shows no bar. An axis line reads "◀ VER faster" and "RUS faster ▶", and the foot "Lap −0.440" (the other driver's best minus the reference's, when both have a time). A driver or reference without a best lap gives one muted line, "RUS vs VER: no best lap to compare.". (amended, #99, owner 2026-11-13) A sector's `—` has its reason behind a tap (`ui/Note.tsx`, `deltaReason`): "No S2 time for NOR" ("for NOR and VER" when both are missing), or "Deleted lap".
    - **Reference** (owner, 2026-11-07, P4f): a "Reference" `fieldset.segmented` of the picked drivers (`aria-pressed`) above "Sector deltas"; a choice moves that driver to the front of the picks (the Session view's `setReference`), so `?d=` follows.
    - **Delta table** (`views/DeltaTable.tsx`): the first selected driver is the reference; every other driver gets a Δ S1/S2/S3/Lap column group, that driver − the reference, headed "NOR Δ vs VER" (the Reference choice above the table changes it). A Best-lap row always comes first (A§9; `—` where a driver has no best lap), then one row per lap both drivers have; a reference without laps gets a note above the table. Two drivers fit 390 px without scrolling (the Best-lap label on two lines, 6 px cell padding; still so with the Lap column, #102, checked in a 390 px render); more groups scroll inside `.scroll`. (#102) The Lap column is the `lap_ms` difference from `sectorDeltas` (`lap`), null by the same rule as a sector (a missing value or a deleted lap), with the same bar; on the Best-lap row it is the duel's foot. Values are signed seconds (`+0.123`, `−0.045`, `fmtDelta` in `lib/time.ts`); a delta `sectorDeltas` leaves null (a missing value or a deleted lap) shows `—`. It scrolls in `.scroll` and is named "Sector deltas". Since P4f (owner, 2026-11-07) each non-zero delta cell carries a bar (`i.db.neg|pos`, `aria-hidden`): 4 px in `--muted` from a centre hairline (`td.dc::after`), to the left when faster than the reference, `--w = min(50, |Δ| / 500 ms × 50) %`; a zero and a `—` cell have none. (amended, #99, owner 2026-11-13) Each `—` has its reason behind a tap (`deltaReason` in `views/DeltaTable.tsx`, the other driver first): "No lap 12 for NOR" (on the Best-lap row "No best lap for NOR"), "Deleted lap", or "No S2 time for NOR" ("for NOR and VER" when both are missing; "No lap time for NOR" in the Lap column, #102); the ids are unique per row, driver and column.
    - **Pit cycles** (owner, 2026-11-09, #115; `pitCycles` in `lib/crossings.ts`): in a race, a card "Pit cycles" in the side column lists, for every pair of the picked drivers, one sentence per cycle. A stop is a race in-lap n followed by a new stint from lap n + 1, or from n itself (A§6.1: OpenF1 numbers some sessions' stints from the in-lap). A stint starting on lap 1 is never a stop; an in-lap 1 followed by a stint from lap 2 is one, but it has no line before it, so its cycle reads "not comparable (a line crossing is missing)", found by `stops` from `/stints` and the lap rows. A stop of one car pairs with a stop of the other at most `PIT_WINDOW` = 3 in-laps apart; the first in-lap decides who is first. `before` is the gap (first − second, at the line) after lap firstIn − 1, `after` the gap after the second car's out-lap (secondIn + 1). The sentence: "VER pitted L22, NOR L24: VER 1.2 s behind before, 0.8 s ahead after (undercut)", "level" under 50 ms, " (undercut)" when the first was behind and is now ahead and " (overcut)" for the reverse, neither when both pitted on the same lap ("VER and NOR pitted L22: …"; a swap there is pit-lane speed). A cycle is not comparable, and says so ("…: not comparable (safety car in the window)"), with an SC, VSC or red-flag period (A§6.1 `trackPeriods`) overlapping the window, the cars' laps firstIn − 1 (whose end is the before line) … secondIn + 1; the periods are the leader's laps, so the window is checked as leader laps firstIn − 1 … secondIn + 2, one lap of margin at the top (a lead-lap car's lap m ends in the leader's lap m or m + 1), a missing crossing at any of the four lines, or a PENALTY message naming either car (`CAR n (TLA)`) between those lines. Never a "would have"; no projection. While the stints or race control load it says "Loading pit cycles…", if either failed "Pit cycles need the stints and race control, which did not load.", with no cycle "No pit cycles within 3 laps for the picked drivers.".
  - **Weather** (owner, 2026-11-07, P5a; `views/Weather.tsx`, `lib/weather.ts`, `views/weather-chart-option.ts`): every sample OpenF1 lists for the session, the hour before a race's start included (owner, 2026-11-09; A§6.1), each read through `reading` (owner, 2026-11-09): a 0 air or track temperature, humidity or pressure is a dropout and no reading (it never sets a minimum and breaks the chart's line), and a calm sample (0 m/s) has no direction. It renders without waiting for the laps; in a race the laps add the leader's lap when they arrive.
    - **Tiles** (`dl.tiles` "Weather"): Air and Track as min–max in °C with one decimal ("22.2–28.0 °C"), Humidity in % and Pressure in hPa (OpenF1's mbar) as whole numbers, Wind as the mean speed in m/s and the prevailing compass point ("0.5 m/s S", "mean · max 1.6 m/s" beneath), and Rain. A value null in every sample is `—` with a fixed reason behind a tap (`ui/Note.tsx`): "No air temperature in the OpenF1 data" and the like.
    - **Rain:** "Dry", or "Rain at times" with each period beneath in Amsterdam time ("16:25–16:46"), the tile then a full row. A period is a run of samples with rainfall > 0 (`rainPeriods`); a dry sample, a sample without a reading or a gap over 5 min ends it. In a `Race`-type session each period adds the leader's laps: "· laps 3–12", "· lap 3", or "· before the start" when it ended before lap 1; rain from before the start counts from lap 1.
    - **Leader's lap** (`leaderLap`): the highest `lap_number` any driver had started (`date_start` ≤ the sample), the A§6.1 meaning of race control's lap; null before the first lap, outside `Race`-type sessions, and while the laps load. After the flag it stays on the last lap.
    - **Compass** (`compass`): 8 points of 45° each, centred on N, NE, …, NW, so 0° and 359° are N and 22.5° is NE; the prevailing point is the most frequent one, a tie going to the point seen first.
    - **Chart** (the card "Air and track temperature", "°C, Amsterdam time"): on the lazy ECharts chunk through `views/Chart.tsx`, a box with `role="img"` named "Air and track temperature"; the tiles and the rain periods are its text alternative. Air is a solid `--fg` line and track a dashed `--muted` one, so colour is never the only cue, each with its end label and a legend. The x-axis is a time axis with `useUTC: true` and labels from `fmtHm` (Amsterdam `HH:mm`): its ticks fall on round UTC minutes, which are round in Amsterdam too, so the labels are right in any browser time zone (the 390 px render test in `weather-chart.test.ts` passed under `TZ` UTC, America/Los_Angeles, Asia/Kolkata and Asia/Kathmandu, 2026-11-09). Rain periods are `markArea` bands in `--rain` at 20 %, from half a sample (30 s) before the first wet sample to half a sample after the last, on a data-less series of their own; a key above reads "━ air · ╍ track · ▮ rain". The tooltip is rich text (`renderMode: 'richText'`): the time, "Air 22.4 °C · Track 28.2 °C", "Humidity 86 %", "Wind 0.4 m/s S", "Rain" or "Dry" and, in a race only, "Lap n" or "Before the start"; every line is stripped of `{`, `|` and `}`.
    - **Messages** (amended, #99, owner 2026-11-13): without rows the tab says `noDataText(…, 'weather')` (No data, above), such as "Weather arrives about 35 min after the end." or "No weather in the OpenF1 data.". The tile reasons name the session's source (`sourceWords`): "No air temperature in the OpenF1 data", "… in the F1 archive".
    - **Rain map** (owner, 2026-11-07, P5c; `views/RainMap.tsx`, `lib/rain.ts`; D1 and D2 owner, 2026-11-09): where the rain was around the circuit, and when, as a card "Rain around the circuit" ("mm/h, Amsterdam time") under the panels on the session's Weather tab (so it shows without OpenF1 rows) and under `WeatherPanels` on the player's Weather tab. It is Open-Meteo's modelled 7 × 7 field, not radar (A§6.3).
      - **Data:** `useRain(key, live)` (`rainQuery`) reads `/sessions/:key/rain` (A§10) and polls it every 60 s (`refetchInterval`) while `live` (the player's live mode) or, (amended, #99, owner 2026-11-13) on both pages, while the answer's grid is `live`; the session tab does not poll a `final` or `failed` grid, nor a session without one. The circuit files come from `useCircuitMap(map_id)`: bundled JSON through `import.meta.glob` with `import: 'default'` for both (`loadCircuitMap(id)` in `lib/api.ts` reads `.outline` from the outline file's default export; Vite 8's `json.stringify: 'auto'` gives a file over 10 kB a default export only, so a named export would work for the small files and break for a big one; `map-loader.test.ts` runs the loader), so a map costs no request.
      - **The field:** an SVG with `viewBox="-6 -6 12 12"` in km from the circuit's centre (x east, y south), `role="img"` and the summary as its accessible name. Layers, bottom to top: OSM water (even-odd fill), the rain cells (`rect.rain-1|2|3`, 1.5 km squares, cell k row-major from the north-west; none for a dry cell or a null), the OSM coast and roads, 1 km rings to 5 km labelled "1 km" … "5 km" (`--muted`), the outline (a 3 px `--fg` line over a 6 px `--bg` halo), a north arrow and a 2 km scale bar. Lines keep their pixel width through `vector-effect: non-scaling-stroke` on each stroked element (the `circle`s of the rings, not their `<g>`: the property is not inherited, so on the group each 1 px ring scaled to 1 km and drew a solid disc; found on the screenshots, 2026-11-09, and pinned by `rain-map.test.tsx`), and text is 0.42 km.
      - **Steps:** the frame's mm per 15 min × 4 = mm/h (`rainStep`): under 0.1 nothing, **light** 0.1–2.5, **moderate** 2.5–7.6, **heavy** from 7.6. The legend (`ul.rainlegend`, "Light 0.1–2.5 mm/h", "Moderate 2.5–7.6 mm/h", "Heavy ≥ 7.6 mm/h", swatches `aria-hidden`) sits beside the field and under it below 640 px, so colour is never the only cue.
      - **Slider and summary:** a native range, `aria-label="Rain map time"`, `aria-valuetext` "HH:mm Amsterdam time" (`fmtHm`), its value shown beside it, one step per frame, at least 44 px high. It opens on the first frame at or after `date_start` (else the last). Under it, as visible text and the field's name, the summary: "No rain data for this time" (every value of the frame is null), "No rain within 5 km", or "Rain in 12 of 49 cells, heaviest 3.2 mm/h, 2 km north-west of the circuit" (the distance rounded to km, one of 8 compass words, "at the circuit" for the centre cell; a tie goes to the first cell row-major). The summary is a polite live region (`aria-live="polite"`) and the range's `aria-describedby` points at it, so a screen reader hears the new frame as the slider moves (P5c review).
      - **States:** nothing for a cancelled session; "No circuit outline for this venue." for a circuit without a mapping (no cells, C5); (amended, #99, owner 2026-11-13) "Rain map fetch failed: no rain data." for a `failed` grid without frames (failed is never retried, A§8); "Rain map not fetched yet." without a grid, or a `live` or `final` grid without frames; above the map, "Rain data incomplete: the fetch failed after HH:mm." for a `failed` grid with frames (the last frame's time) and, outside the player, for a `live` grid "Frames still arriving (every 15 min)." until `date_end` + 30 min (the last live refresh, A§8), then "Rain data provisional: the final frames come 24 h after the end." (the clock is the rain query's `dataUpdatedAt`, which its 60 s poll moves on); in the player "No rain frame up to this time yet." while T is before the first frame; a loading or failed query is `Pending`. A circuit whose map file is missing (a test keeps the files and `CIRCUITS` in step) shows "No circuit outline for this venue." above the cells on the plain km field. Where `native15` is false: "Coarse model here: the field may look uniform." (A§6.3 U4).
      - **Credits** (ADR-0008 §4, word for word, all links `<a rel="noreferrer">`, inline in the sentence: `.rainsrc a` is `display: inline` with no 44 px minimum, which WCAG 2.5.8 exempts for a link in a sentence, where the global `a` rule's `inline-flex` 44 px box padded each one on a phone, "(MIT      )", found on the screenshots, 2026-11-09): "Rain: Weather data by Open-Meteo.com (CC BY 4.0). Modelled, not radar; 7 × 7 points binned into three steps. Circuit outline: bacinger/f1-circuits (MIT), simplified." (the "MIT" link is `/third-party-licences.txt`, shipped from `apps/web/public/` into `dist`, so it is a same-origin link); "© OpenStreetMap contributors, ODbL." only when OSM layers are drawn; "Includes UK Met Office model data (CC BY-SA 4.0)." only for `gb-1948` (Silverstone).
      - **In the player** (P5c): `cap = T`, so only frames dated at or before T are drawn and the slider ends at the newest of them. While the slider is on the newest frame it follows it as T crosses frames; moved back, it stays until it is moved to the newest again or T seeks back past it. A replay never shows a frame from after T.
      - **Lazy chunks:** the map JSONs are not in the initial bundle. Rolldown makes one chunk per JSON module, each a dynamic entry (`build.test.ts`: the module count equals the number of files, one module per chunk, none in the initial set); the outline chunks are 0.31–0.53 kB (the default export holds the outline string alone, as `var e={outline:`…`}`), so a map costs one tiny request when the card opens.
      - **CSP and forced colours:** classes and attributes only, no `style=` and no `<style>`. Under forced colours `forced-color-adjust: none` keeps the field and the legend swatches in their token colours.
  - **Mini-sectors + speed traps** (owner, 2026-10-28; `views/MiniSectors.tsx`): one table in `.scroll`, per selected driver in pick order a header row with the team bar, then one row per lap: the lap number (struck through when deleted, with "Deleted: …" (the reason, or just "Deleted") behind a tap, `ui/Note.tsx`, as in the lap table, since a screen reader does not announce `<s>` and a phone cannot hover a title, #102; with the IN/OUT badge), one box per segment in S1, S2 and S3, then I1, I2 and ST in km/h (`—` when null; (amended, #99, owner 2026-11-13) with "No speed-trap reading in the OpenF1 data" behind a tap, the source by `sourceWords`). Each sector has as many boxes as the longest array of the session; a shorter or null array is padded with "no data" boxes. Codes (A§6.1, `segClass`) and, since P4a (owner, 2026-11-07), a shape each: 2048 "other", half height in `--ms-other`; 2049 "personal best", full height in green; 2051 "overall best", full height in purple with a ring (an outline); 2064 "pit lane", full height, hatched `--pit` with a thin frame; 0 and a null element "no data", a dashed outline with no fill; every other code "unknown code <n>", half height in grey (`--muted`). Each box has that `title` for a hover, and a legend of the shapes (`MsLegend`, its boxes `aria-hidden`) sits above the grid, so colour is never the only cue. For a screen reader the boxes are hidden (`aria-hidden`) and each sector cell has one `aria-label` with the counts in legend order, such as "S1: 4 personal best, 2 other", so a race is not tens of thousands of images. A `Race`-type session (sprints included) shows a banner: "Mini-sector colours unreliable in races".
  - **Stints** (owner, 2026-11-07, P4f; `views/Stints.tsx`, `stintLayout`): a lane per driver in the lap-table order, on a real lap scale: a stint starts at `(lap_start − 1) / total` and is `laps / total` wide, with `total` the session's highest lap number (a stint running past it counts), so a hole in the data shows as a gap and no row is stretched to fit. Each lane has a track line (`.trackline`) to the driver's last lap, so a hole after its last stint shows as bare line.
    - **Scale:** tick labels (`.laxis`) every 10 laps and at the last lap, a ten-lap tick within 3 laps of the last one dropped (51 laps: 10 … 40, 51), with hairlines (`.rules`) behind the lanes. The axis, the rows and `.rules`' inset share one set of `em` columns at the same font size (the 11 px is on the tick labels only), so labels, hairlines and segments line up. In a race, the safety car and VSC periods of `trackPeriods` (not red flags, owner, 2026-11-07, C6; the charts band those) are two dashed `--amber` lines in `.rules`, at the period's first and last lap edges, and the legend lists each as "SC, laps 34–35"; a failed race-control load says "Race control did not load: no SC or VSC lines.".
    - **Segments:** a `.seg` per stint with its compound letter and, beside it, its length in laps ("24"), or "24 · used" when `tyre_age_at_start > 0` (a null age is unknown and not marked). A container query on the segment (no padding of its own, so it is exactly its laps − 2 px wide; the letter's 4 px margin gives the inset) hides " · used" under 80 px, the number under 48 px and the letter under 16 px (two letters, 2018's HS, US, SS and SH, under 24 px), so nothing clips or overflows. Compound letters are black on the compound fill (an unknown compound gets a grey fill), 4.5:1 or more in both themes. A stint without `lap_start` or `lap_end` is listed in the popover and not drawn. The stops are the driver's stint rows − 1 ("1 stop", hidden under 640 px).
    - **Row and popover:** each row is a `button.row` (at least 44 px, `popoverTarget`, so a tap works on a phone, D10) named like "VER, 1 stop: medium laps 1 to 24, hard laps 25 to 71". Its native popover (`div.pop`, as `ui/Note.tsx`) lists each stint in words: "Medium, laps 1–24 (24 laps), new set", "Medium, laps 1–3 (3 laps), used set, 3 laps old at the start (may be wrong for a used set)" (A§6.1, #473), or "Hard, laps unknown". There are no segment `title`s. A legend names the compounds in use (the season's order, softest first, then an unknown one), the periods and "number: laps in the stint".
  - **Status** (`/status`): an Ingest section (session counts per `ingest_state` plus the cancelled count, and a table of the sessions whose last fetch failed) and, since P2c, a Recorder section: the state (`idle`, `connecting`, `recording`), the session while recording, the F1 TV token's expiry, the last error (a fixed code) and the time of the last report. A warning line shows for `token_expiring`, `login_failed` and `disk_low`, and a notice when the recorder has not reported for over 5 minutes. Before its first report the section says so.
    - **#105** (owner, 2026-11-13; `views/Status.tsx`): the page polls `/status` every 60 s (`useStatus(60_000)`), so `stale` changes without a reload.
      - **Ingest:** the daemon's last sync, whether it is waiting out a live window, and the time of its last report, with a `role="alert"` notice "The ingest daemon has not reported for over 5 minutes" when `ingest.stale`, and "The ingest daemon has not reported yet." before its first write.
      - **Last fetch failed** is a table shared with the **Rain** section (`FailedTable`): the session name is a link to `/:year/0/:session/laps` (`/${year}/0/${key}/laps` resolves to the canonical URL), and a rain grid's to its `/weather`. **Rain** shows the counts per state (`live`, `final`, `failed`) above its own table of grids that failed at least once, headed **Grids whose last fetch failed** so it does not repeat the Ingest heading.
      - **Race control in an unknown form** lists year, meeting, session (a link to its laps) and the row count of `rc_unmatched` (A§10), or "None.".
  - **Error states** (owner, 2026-11-13, #98; `errorText`, `ui/ErrorView.tsx`): a 401 or a TypeError (Access's login redirect) says "Your Access session may have expired: reload to sign in."; a 404 "Not found."; a ZodError `NEW_VERSION`, "A new version is out: reload the page."; anything else "API error". A query keeps its data when a later refetch fails. A differing `F1-Revision` keeps the data and raises one app-wide `role="alert"` banner, `NEW_VERSION` (`NewVersion` in `ui/ErrorView.tsx`, rendered once at the top of `<main>` in `App.tsx`; `markStale()` and `useStale()` in `lib/api.ts`), so a polled view (`/live` every 30 s, `/status` and the live rain every 60 s) that keeps its old data after a deploy still tells the owner to reload (owner, 2026-11-13, D4). A ZodError raises the banner too and also shows the text in place of the view's data. The flag is never cleared: a reload is the fix. The player's stream event that fails its contract raises it as well, closes the stream, never reopens it and releases the wake lock; the player adds no line of its own. Render errors (owner, 2026-11-13, #100): an `ErrorBoundary` around the page in `App.tsx`, reset by a new pathname (its `reset` prop, so a session's tab switch does not remount the page), so the header and the banner stay and a navigation resets it; one around each player tab, keyed on the tab, which catches a tab's own render (the Tower with its trend, Laps, Order, Weather), so a crash in one leaves the strip, the bar, the stream and the other tabs; a throw in what the player itself derives per flush (the strip, the pit wall's panel values, the status series, the Laps table's stream drivers, the Order tab's drivers and grid, and the focused driver) still reaches the page boundary; and the outer one in `main.tsx`, the last net for the header itself.
  - **Live and replay** (P2e, `views/Live.tsx`, `Player.tsx`, `Tower.tsx`; the engine is A§11.3). `/live` and `/replay/:recording` share the player; the lap table is the existing `LapTable`, fed from the stream. (owner, 2026-11-13, #100, D2): above it a head (`PlayerHead`), the meeting as the h1 with the session as a boxed tag (`.kind`), as the session head's, and once `/sessions/:key` has loaded one link "Results: laps, compare, stints" to that session's Laps. While that query has failed (no detail) its error (`ErrorView`) shows under the tower; the tabs still need the session.
    - **`/live`:** the recorder warnings first (`RecorderWarnings`, shared with Status, polled every 60 s). With an open recording (`/live` of A§10 names one without `ended_at`) the player follows it at `live`. Otherwise (P4c: the recordings list moved to `/replay`; `/live` links to it as "All replays") "Next: <meeting> · <session> starts <time>" with a Replay link when that session has a recording, or "No session is scheduled." (`/live` is refetched every 30 s in every state, so a starting session appears and an open recording's `ended_at` reaches the player. The player stays for the recording it follows while `/live` still names it, ended or not, so that refetch neither closes nor remounts its stream.)
    - **`/replay`** (P4c, `views/Live.tsx`): the recordings list: a link to `/replay/:id`, the start in Amsterdam time and a tag (recording, complete, incomplete), newest first.
    - **`/replay/:recording`:** the row from `/recordings` (not found when it is absent), and the player in replay mode starting at the session's `date_start`, clamped into the recording.
    - **Tower** (`towerRows`, a pure function of the state): one row per TimingData line ordered by `Line`; Pos, driver (team bar and Tla from the DriverList, else the number), Gap, Int, Last (purple for the overall best, green for a personal best, as in the lap table), Best, S1–S3 each with its value in the same colours and its mini-sector boxes (`segClass`), Tyre (the last stint's compound dot and `TotalLaps`) and Pits (`NumberOfPitStops` with IN PIT and OUT badges). Gap and Int follow `SessionInfo.Type` (A§11.3: a race's `GapToLeader` and `IntervalToPositionAhead.Value`, practice's `TimeDiffToFastest` and `TimeDiffToPositionAhead`, qualifying's `Stats[SessionPart − 1]`) and show what the feed sends (the race leader's `LAP 12`). Without a value the cell is `—` with its reason behind a tap (`ui/Note.tsx`), from the cases the check found: the fastest driver ("Fastest lap: no gap", in qualifying "Fastest in Q2: no gap", for Int "…: no car ahead"), no time yet ("No lap time yet", "No time in Q2 yet"), "Knocked out: no time in Q3", "Retired", "Q1 has not started" (SessionPart 0), else "No gap in the feed"; a state without a known type or part says so ("Unknown session type", "No session type in the feed", "No qualifying part in the feed"). Sprint Qualifying's parts read SQ1–SQ3. A retired or stopped driver is muted, and (#102) its Gap cell ends with a visible `RET` or `STOP` badge (`aria-hidden`, with ", retired" or ", stopped" for a screen reader; Retired wins over Stopped), since forced colours turn `--muted` into CanvasText; a retired row without a gap keeps "Retired" behind its `—` as well. It scrolls in `.scroll` with a sticky driver column. While the state's `SessionInfo.Key` is not the recording's session it says "Waiting for the session: the feed still shows the previous one."; with no lines, "No timing yet.". Feed text is rendered as text only (React text nodes).
    - **Tower since P4d** (owner, 2026-11-07; `towerRows` and `Tower.tsx`): in a race, Gap's leader cell reads `Leader` and Int's `—` carries the reason "Leader: no car ahead". A `±` column (races only) shows the places gained since the start from `GridPos` (TimingAppData): ▲ n in `--fg`, ▼ n in `--muted`, – for none, with screen-reader words "gained n places" / "lost n places" ("1 place" singular); `—` with "No grid position in the feed" when the driver has none; the column is hidden outside races and when no driver has a grid position. Each sector shows its value over a 6 × 8 px mini-sector strip with the P4a shapes; the legend above the tower gains "▲ places since the start" and "used set". Tyre: the compound dot, new or used from the stint's `New` (a used set has a dashed `--fg` outline; the words "new set" or "used set" for screen readers), then `TotalLaps`; without a stint `—` with "No stint in the feed" behind a tap (amended, #99, owner 2026-11-13); the Pits cell shows IN PIT or OUT. A race Int under a second (`close` in `towerRows`) is bold with a dot and the words ", within a second". The driver cell is a toggle button (pressed also underlined) when the player passes `onFocus`: it focuses that row, and the focused driver is page state in the player (used by P4e).
    - **At the line** (owner, 2026-11-09, #115; `closingAt` in `lib/crossings.ts`): in a race, with the player's `trend`, two columns right after Int, "1 lap" and "3 laps", each headed for a screen reader "Interval change at the line over 1 lap". Each cell is the change of the car's interval to the car ahead (tower order) over its last 1 or 3 laps up to n, its last lap completed by T (`runningOrder`'s rule: its crossing is known by T, or the next lap has a row by T): ▼ 0.4 when it closed, ▲ 0.2 when the car ahead pulled away, one decimal in seconds, "0.0" without an arrow when it rounds to nothing, with the words "closing 0.4 s on the car ahead", "car ahead pulling away 0.2 s" and "no change". `—` with the note "No crossing for both cars on lap n" where a crossing is unknown (also the car's own at n, so the trend and the Order tab agree); blank for the leader, for a retired or stopped car (a muted row), across an in- or out-lap of either car, across an SC, VSC or red-flag band (open bands run to T), and before enough laps. The legend gains "At the line: ▼ closing on the car ahead, ▲ car ahead pulling away, over 1 and 3 laps". No projection of a catch; TimingData's `Catching` is left to #113.
    - **Tabs** Tower · Laps · Order · Weather (Order in races only, #115, owner, 2026-11-13; the switch shows once the session has loaded from the API). The lap table's drivers and results come from the stream's DriverList and TimingData positions, because OpenF1 has no drivers during a live session. It is `memo`ised and rebuilt only when the lap rows change (`snapshot` or `laps`), not on deltas.
    - **Weather tab** (owner, 2026-11-07, P5b; the player's third tab): P5a's tiles and chart (`WeatherPanels` in `views/Weather.tsx`) on the stream's weather rows up to T, with the race laps from the stream's lap rows when `SessionInfo.Type` is `Race`. It is gated as the strip is (the feed must show this session, so nothing from the previous one), else "No weather in the feed yet."; a missing value's reason names the feed ("No humidity in the feed"). Nothing after T by construction: the stream holds nothing past T, and a seek's snapshot replaces the rows (until that snapshot the old rows stay, as the tower does). P5c adds the rain map under the panels, from `/api` frames up to T (below).
    - **Clock line** (owner, 2026-11-07, P4e; inside the player bar, as its first row): T as Europe/Amsterdam `HH:mm:ss` in the display face (`fmtClock`, with an ISO `dateTime`), advanced locally every second between events; then the time left (P4d's `leftAt` and `fmtLeft` on the strip's clock, "H:MM:SS left"), the lap (`LapCount`, "Lap N/M") and the mode pill: "LIVE, N s behind" (N the delay), "LIVE · Delay not set" at 0 s, "Replay" while a replay plays, "Paused" otherwise. The track status word left it for P4d's strip (the session light). Without the pit wall (the feed still shows another session) the line has only T and the mode. It ticks each second, as do the seek bar, the Back to chips and P4d's LEFT cell (Render throttle, below), each from values derived per store flush: no tick parses the state.
    - **Session light** (owner, 2026-11-07, P4d; `lightOf` in `lib/pitwall.ts`): ten states, each a word: Track clear (TrackStatus 1), Yellow flag (2), Safety car (4), Red flag (5), VSC (6), VSC ending (7), Stopped (SessionStatus `Aborted`), Chequered flag (`Finished`, `Finalised` or `Ends`), Not started (any other SessionStatus, including none) and Track status unknown. While SessionStatus is `Started` the TrackStatus code decides, and a code without a meaning here (3, which never appears on the recordings, A§11.1, or none) is unknown, never clear. Three lamps (class `light lamp-r|y|g|chq`, `aria-hidden`) stand in fixed positions red, yellow, green, and the word sits beside them in `span.lw`; the chequered flag is a checker. Lit red is `--live`, yellow `--amber`, green `--green`; the lamp's place and the word carry the state, not the hue. Under `forced-colors: active` a lit lamp is CanvasText and the word is always beside it.
    - **Tab title** (owner, 2026-11-08, D1): while `SessionStatus` is `Started` or `Aborted` and the feed shows the recording's session, the light's word goes in front of P4c's title, e.g. "Safety car · Replay: Mexico City Grand Prix · Race · F1 Timings". The player writes it into a title-word store (`setTitleWord` in `lib/routes.ts`), and `App` stays the single writer of `document.title`. The word is cleared on unmount.
    - **Status strip** (owner, 2026-11-07; `stripOf`, `PitWall.tsx`): above the Tower · Laps · Order · Weather switch, a `dl` "Session state" with the light, SESSION, LAP (`LapCount`, "n / total"), LEFT (`ExtrapolatedClock`; counts down locally while `Extrapolating`, held otherwise, clamped into [0, remaining] by `leftAt`; `H:MM:SS` from one hour), WEATHER (from `weatherRow` + `reading` in `lib/weather.ts`, as the Weather tab reads it, P5b, owner, 2026-11-07: air and track temperature with one decimal, a 0 temperature a dropout and left out, `Rainfall` above 0 rain, 0 dry, else left out; numbers only, never feed text) and RACE CONTROL (the latest message, with its lap). A cell without data is left out. On a phone the strip wraps and nothing is hidden (owner, 2026-11-08, D3); the message is one line with an ellipsis and its full text in `title`.
    - **Panel** (owner, 2026-11-07; an `aside` "Session details"): beside the tower from 1100 px, below it on a phone. Race control, newest first (`raceControl(state)`: it takes no filter, each item carries its `kind`, and the panel filters by its own state), with the chips All · Flags · SC/VSC · Incidents · Track limits and their counts (owner, 2026-11-08, D2; chips keep 44 px). A message's kind is matched first match wins: Category `SafetyCar` is SC/VSC (tag VSC when the text names a virtual safety car, else SC); Category `Flag` or a "RED FLAG" text, Flags; "TRACK LIMITS" or "DELETED", Track limits; "INCIDENT", "INVESTIGAT", "PENALTY" or "STEWARDS", Incidents (incidents, track limits and the 2026 red-flag text are Category `Other` on the recordings); else no kind, shown under All only. An empty filter says "No messages of this kind yet."; the list is a `ui/Scroll` region "Race control messages". Below it, in races, the cars within a second of the one ahead (`withinASecond`, from the tower's `close` rows), and the fastest lap so far from `TimingStats` Position 1. Card headings are `h2`.
    - **While the feed shows another session** the strip, panel and tab-title word wait like the tower.
    - **Controls** (owner, 2026-11-07, P4e) in a bar at the bottom, one control model: every nudge moves T, so a negative one is always earlier. The bar holds the seek bar, the transport and the "Back to" chips. On a wide screen (from 641 px) its rows are the head (the clock line and the Controls toggle, Minimize below); the transport beside the seek bar, which takes all the remaining width (the chips never narrow it); the chips, with the announce toggle at the right end of their row; and the delay slider (live). A phone stacks them. The transport is seven buttons: −30 −5 −1, Pause/Play, +1 +5 +30 (D3), labelled "30 seconds earlier", "5 seconds earlier", "1 second earlier", "1 second later", "5 seconds later", "30 seconds later". They are a compact cluster on wide screens and one row of equal buttons on a phone, about 47.7 px wide and 44 px tall each at 390 px. In replay mode a nudge of s seeks by s. In live mode it changes the delay by −s, clamped to 0–120: −5 adds 5 s of delay, +5 takes 5 s away, never below 0; only a decrease below the open stream's delay reopens it (Live delay, below). In live mode the delay slider (0–120 s, 1 s steps) stays, on its own row; it is not shown in replay mode. In replay mode of a recording that is still open, a Live button (D2) stays: a mode switch, always enabled, not an event jump. The seek bar spans [`started_at`, `ended_at`] in 1 s steps; for an open recording its end is now − delay in live mode and now in replay mode (a replay ignores the stored delay). A seek or a Back-to chip moves T and takes a live stream into replay mode; a live nudge changes the delay. A seek while paused shows a still frame at the new T (below), and the sync flow stays: pause, seek, Play on both screens. Pausing freezes T and closes the stream; Play reopens at the frozen T (clamped into the recording), also in replay mode. Play and Live restart the local clock at the T they open at (P4e review), so T does not jump by the length of the pause before the new stream's first event. At `end` the player pauses and never reopens.
    - **Minimize** (owner, 2026-11-08, #77): the bar's first row is its head, the clock line with a "Controls" toggle (a chevron and the word, `aria-expanded` and `aria-controls`; the name never changes) at the right end. Minimized, the bar keeps only the head, with Play/Pause between the clock line and the toggle. The rest (seek bar, transport, Back to chips, delay slider, announce toggle) is not rendered, so nothing hidden can take the focus or ticks, inside a wrapper (`.barbody`) that stays, `hidden` (`display: contents` while expanded, so its controls stay items of the bar's grid). The toggle focuses itself when used: Safari focuses no clicked or tapped button, and a focused control in the part that goes (the Position range, the head's Play/Pause) would drop the focus to the page. The minimized head is one 44 px row: on a phone the clock line drops the time left and the lap (owner: they stay in the status strip at the top only), so T and the mode pill stack beside the buttons; from 641 px the clock line does not wrap. The pill gives way with an ellipsis where it would not fit (a phone under 390 px, just above 640 px), its full text kept in the markup. That is a 61 px bar at 390 px and on a wide screen. The 44 px toggle makes the head row taller than the 28 px clock line, so the expanded bar grew with #77, from 238 to 264 px on a phone in replay and by 16 px on a wide screen (owner, 2026-11-08: accepted). Toggling never touches the stream, T or the delay; a delay change pending its 400 ms still commits when the bar is minimized meanwhile. The bar is sticky in the page flow, so at the end of the page it covers nothing. The choice is stored per device in `localStorage` (`f1.minimized`, every access in try/catch, only '1' minimizes, default expanded).
    - **Markers** (owner, 2026-11-07, P4e) on the seek bar, positioned with `--x` (a custom property, CSP-safe) on a track inset by half the native range thumb (8 px) on each side, so a marker sits under the thumb's centre at that T. They come from the series alone, not from RaceControl (A§11.3), and are drawn by shape: a tall tick for the start (a `SessionData.StatusSeries` `SessionStatus` `Started`, unless the next entry with a `SessionStatus` follows within 60 s, which is the race's 2 s blip, A§11.1); a band for a safety car, VSC or red-flag period, left open up to T while it lasts. The bands come from one pass over the series entries at or before T (P4e review) that keeps the latest `TrackStatus` and whether the session is aborted (set by `SessionStatus` Aborted, cleared by Started or Finished). After each entry the kind in force is red if the TrackStatus is Red or the session is aborted, else SC for SCDeployed, else VSC for VSCDeployed or VSCEnding, else none; a band is a run of one kind, from the entry where it comes into force to the entry where it stops. So an SC closes at Aborted or Red, the race's red flag runs from Aborted to the restart's Started (its TrackStatus clears 37 min earlier, A§11.3), and a red flag without Aborted (practice) ends at the next TrackStatus outside Red; and a checker for the chequered flag (every `SessionStatus` `Finished`). A band is positioned with `--x` and sized with `--w`. `markersUpTo(series, T, span)` (`lib/replay.ts`) returns only the marks at or before T and inside [`started_at`, `ended_at`]; the series is parsed once per store flush (`statusSeries`), so the seek bar's 1 s tick parses nothing. The page-visit union of every marker seen is gone: after a backward seek a later period is no longer drawn, and the stream delivers nothing ahead of T, so none is a spoiler. Since P4a (owner, 2026-11-07) the start marker is `--fg` and taller than the chequered-flag one (green now means a best), and a red band uses `--live` (SC and VSC `--amber`). The Position range has `aria-valuetext`: `HH:mm` while playing (it changes per minute), `HH:mm:ss` while paused (`fmtClock(T)`).
    - **Back to** (owner, 2026-11-07, P4e): chips of at least 44 px under the transport, in a `fieldset` (a group) named "Back to" by its `aria-label` (the visible "Back to" label is `aria-hidden`, so the name is read once), that seek to the latest start, SC, VSC, red flag and chequered flag at or before T, each reading its `HH:mm`, and to the focused driver's last stop (`lastStop(laps, driver, T)`, `lib/replay.ts`: the driver's latest pit-out lap after lap 1 with a `date_start` at or before T; the chip reads "<TLA> stop L<in-lap>"; the focus is P4d's tower focus). A kind with no mark at or before T has no chip. No control jumps forward to an event (R1): a forward jump's enabled state would reveal the future.
    - **Skipped line** (owner, 2026-11-07, P4e): after a forward seek, one dismissible line above the view switch, never a modal. `skipped(old, next)` (`lib/replay.ts`; `old` and `next` are the feeds before and after the seek) counts within (old T, new T] the SC, VSC and red periods that started, the pit stops (pit-out laps after lap 1), the laps newly deleted (deleted in the new rows, not in the old) and the incidents noted (RaceControl messages matching "INCIDENT INVOLVING … NOTED", one per incident, A§11.3). The line reads "Before this point" and then `skippedText`, e.g. "1 SC · 21 pit stops", non-zero counts only. Nothing after the new T is counted. There is no line when every count is 0 or after a backward seek. Dismiss moves the focus to the Position range before the line goes, or to the Controls toggle while the bar is minimized (no Position range then).
    - **Render throttle:** the store between the stream and React notifies at most every 250 ms (a trailing timer), so a burst of deltas is one render. It rebuilds the lap array only when the lap rows changed. It builds a new weather pair (`rows` and the lap rows as of then) only when a weather row arrives or a snapshot comes (P5b, owner, 2026-11-07), so the Weather tab's chart option is rebuilt only when the rows change (about once a minute), not on each `laps` event. Four things tick each second on every tab, and a fifth on the Weather tab, as their own components, each from values derived per flush, so no tick parses the state: the clock line (T, and the time left from the strip's clock values), the seek bar (`markersUpTo` on the parsed series), the Back to chips (the same, and `lastStop` on the lap rows), P4d's LEFT cell (the strip's clock values) and, on the Weather tab only, the rain map (P5c, owner, 2026-11-07: `PlayerRain` reads T each second, but the index of the last frame at or before T is memoised and `RainMap` is a `memo`, so it re-derives only when T crosses a frame, about every 15 min). The tower, the wall data (strip, panel, light), the clock line's state part and the status series (`statusSeries`) are derived once per store flush (keyed on its snapshot object, because the state is mutated in place), so an idle second parses nothing.
    - **One stream at a time:** an effect keyed on (recording, the open: `at`, a still frame, and in live mode the delay it opens at) closes the old stream in its cleanup before it opens the next. A seek shows at once and opens the new stream after 400 ms without a further change (debounce). The delay is stored per device in `localStorage` (`f1.delay`, every access in try/catch, default 0, only an integer 0–120 is read).
    - **Live delay** (#101, owner, 2026-11-13): a live stream opens at the delay shown then (Live, the first open, a reconnect). A later change at or above that delay opens nothing: at once, the stream holds every event for the difference after its arrival (`hold` in `lib/stream.ts`; the heartbeat is never held, and the watchdog counts arrivals), and the clock line shifts by the change in hold. After an increase T stands at the value it showed until now − delay reaches it, then runs; it never steps back, and it never passes a held event, so nothing after T reaches the page. A change back down within the hold releases the events then due at once. A change below the open stream's delay drops the hold and opens one stream at the new delay 400 ms after the last change, unless a pause, a seek, Live or a reconnect came in between (those open at the shown delay themselves, and Pause and Live cancel it); the closed stream's held events are dropped, and the new snapshot covers them. A held `end` closes the transport and the watchdog on arrival (the server closes the response after it, and EventSource's `error` then is no failure) and is applied after the events held before it, so the player pauses there and never reopens. A seek or an error drops them the same way, and the reconnect opens at the shown delay. [CI] `player.test.tsx`, "live delay without a reopen (#101)" and "openStream hold (#101)".
    - **Still frame** (owner, 2026-11-07, P4e): a seek while paused, after the 400 ms debounce, opens the stream at T (with `&still=1`, for the server's open log only, A§10, #101), applies its snapshot and closes it, so the tower, strip and panel stand at the new T while the player stays paused (it replaces the old "Press Play to load this position."). A newer seek closes a pending still first, so there are never two streams from one tab (A§10's cap of 8 per server). An error during a still retries with the reconnect backoff, still a still.
    - **Reconnect rule:** EventSource's own retry reconnects to the same URL, so a replay would reuse its stale `at`. On `error` the client closes the EventSource itself and the player reopens after a backoff of 1 s, doubling to 30 s (reset by the next `snapshot`), at the current T (clamped into the recording), or at `live` when it was live. (owner, 2026-11-13, #98, D3): an event that fails its contract (or does not parse) is not an error: a reconnect cannot fix a mismatch between build and server, so the stream closes, the player shows "A new version is out: reload the page." in place of the reconnect lines and opens no new EventSource. After `end` it never reopens.
    - **Watchdog** (P2e review): a half-open connection (a network hand-over, a phone page resumed from the background) can raise no `error` for minutes, so with no event of any kind for 40 s (about 2.5 server heartbeats, A§11.3) the stream fails as on `error`. It is checked every second against the wall clock, so a resumed page with a dead stream reopens within a second, and a healthy stream, whose events kept arriving, is not reopened. The stream has no `visibilitychange` handler (the wake lock below has its own).
    - **Reconnect feedback** (owner, 2026-11-07, P4e): "Reconnecting…" while a stream or a still is open, after an error until the next `snapshot`; after 5 errors in a row without one, "The stream keeps failing. Reload the page (your Access session may have expired)." (EventSource cannot see the HTTP status, so an expired Access session looks like any error). It keeps retrying. (owner, 2026-11-13, #100, D3): the 3rd error in a row sends one probe, `fetch(streamUrl, { method: 'HEAD', redirect: 'error' })` (`probeStream` in `lib/stream.ts`; A§10's HEAD answer, no body read), and its answer replaces those lines until the next `snapshot`: a 401 or a TypeError "The stream keeps failing: your Access session has expired. Reload the page to sign in." (offline, the offline line, whatever the probe answered), and it keeps retrying; a 404 stops the player at T (clamped as a retry is) with "The recording's files are missing on the server: playback stopped. Press Play to try again.", and Play tries again; a 2xx "The stream keeps failing, but the server has the recording: still retrying." (a HEAD 200 means the route's checks passed and the recording's directory exists, A§10), any other status "The stream keeps failing: the server answered with an error. Still retrying.", and both keep retrying. Until it answers the lines above stand. A snapshot, a Pause and an unmount abort a pending probe; Pause, Play and Live also start the error count again, so the 3rd error of the next streak probes. The wake lock rule is unchanged, but since Play and Live restart the count, they take the lock again after the reload hint, as the next snapshot does.
    - **Wake lock** (#97; `useWakeLock(playing && fails < FAILING && !stale)` in `views/Player.tsx`): while the player plays (live or replay; not paused, not at the end, not a still frame) and the page is visible, it calls `navigator.wakeLock.request('screen')`, so a phone on the stand does not lock. The browser drops the lock when the page is hidden, so it asks again on `visibilitychange` to visible, and it releases on pause, at the end and on unmount. A second grant while one is held, or one that lands after the cleanup, is released at once. The lock holds through the stream's error backoff (`playing` stays true while it reopens) until the reload hint shows ("The stream keeps failing. Reload the page …", `FAILING`, 5 errors without a snapshot); then it is released, so a phone whose Access session expired mid-race locks as usual (owner, 2026-11-13), and the next snapshot or a Play or Live (#100) takes it again. A stale stream (an event that failed its contract, A§13) releases it too and never takes it again, since the page needs a reload.
