A refusal (iOS Low Power mode, Battery Saver) or a missing API changes nothing: the screen locks as usual and the stream is reopened by the reconnect rule and the watchdog. No CSP or header change: `Permissions-Policy` in `deploy/web/Caddyfile` restricts only `geolocation`, `camera` and `microphone`, so `screen-wake-lock` keeps its default allowlist of `self`.
    - **URL state** (owner, 2026-11-07, P4e): on `/replay/:recording` the player writes `?t=HH:mm:ss` (Amsterdam, `fmtClock`) with `history.replaceState`, so the route does not change, on pause, on a seek and every 15 s while playing; `/live` never writes. On load `parseT` (`lib/time.ts`) resolves `t` to the one instant in [`started_at`, `ended_at` ?? now] whose `fmtClock` it is; a recording across midnight or the DST change leaves zero or two candidates, which are null. A valid `t` opens paused at `t` with a still frame and the note "Resumed at 21:44:05: press Play when the video is there." until Play, Live or a seek commit (the note would name an old time); a bad or out-of-range `t` is ignored (the default start). A `replaceState` that throws (a browser's rate limit, `SecurityError`) is ignored, and a seek opens its stream before it writes the URL, so a throw never loses a seek. (owner, 2026-11-13, #100): the replay also keeps its tab, `?t=…&tab=laps|order|weather` (`parseTab` and `replayQuery` in `lib/routes.ts`), written with `replaceState` on a tab click and kept by every `t` write; the tower is left out, a bad value is the tower, and `/live` neither reads nor writes it.
    - **Announcements** (owner, 2026-11-07, P4e): an opt-in "Announce events" checkbox in the bar, stored per device in `localStorage` (`f1.announce`, every access in try/catch, default off). It feeds one polite `role="status"` region, mounted from the start so that only its text changes. Per store flush the parts that changed are joined: "Start" (a new start marker), the session light's word (P4d's `LIGHT`: the track status, and the finish as "Chequered flag") and "Fastest lap: <TLA> <time>" (P4d's `fastestSoFar`). After a seek there is one line, "At HH:mm:ss." plus the skipped line's text. At most one line is announced per 5 s, and the newest is kept. A `Started` that another SessionStatus follows within 60 s (the race's 2 s blip, then Inactive, A§11.1) can announce "Start" before that entry is known (the marker rule's own limit).
- **Look** (owner, 2026-11-07, P4a; from the review's mock-up, `docs/design/2026-11-redesign/`): dark F1 timing-screen style by default, plus a light theme (tokens on `:root` and `:root[data-theme="light"]` in `apps/web/src/styles.css`). `main.tsx` sets `data-theme` before the first render: the pick saved by the header's moon (`localStorage` `f1.theme`, `lib/theme.ts`), else `prefers-color-scheme`, followed live while nothing is saved. The moon, after Live · Replays · Status, is an `aria-pressed` toggle ("Dark theme"), filled when dark is on. Until `data-theme` is set, a light system gets the light `--bg` on the empty page, so it never flashes dark.
  - **Tokens:** surfaces `--bg`, `--panel` and `--raised`; lines `--line` and `--line-soft`; text `--fg`, `--muted` and `--dim`; the timing colours `--purple` and `--green` (light `#0f7553`); `--pit` and `--ms-other` for the mini-sectors; `--live` (live, a red flag or a failure only); `--amber` (SC and VSC only, and the border of the header's Status warning pill, #100); `--purple-wash`, the tint behind a boxed purple value (P4b); the heat steps `--heat-1` to `--heat-3` (P4b, the Δ view); the compound fills (today's `--soft`, `--medium`, `--hard`, `--intermediate`, `--wet`; 2018's `--hypersoft` `#feb1c1`, `--ultrasoft` `#b34da8`, `--supersoft` `#ff5050`, `--soft-2018` `#ffd23f`, `--medium-2018` `#ececec`, `--hard-2018` `#8fe3ff`, `--superhard` `#ff9a3c`, #126; `--unknown`; the same in both themes) and `--cmp-ring`; `--rain`, the weather chart's rain bands (P5a, owner, 2026-11-07: dark `#4da6ff`, light `#1f5fbf`); the rain map's `--rain-1` to `--rain-3` (P5c, owner, 2026-11-07: dark `#2f8f9d`, `#4f7cff`, `#ffa3f0`; light `#2a8a96`, `#2f5bd6`, `#a3189e`) and its context `--map-water` (dark `#1f2c3d`, light `#d6e4f0`) and `--map-road` (dark `#3a3a48`, light `#cfcfd8`). Colour is data: there is no brand accent, and `accent-color` is `--fg`.
  - **Compounds by season** (#126, owner, 2026-11-13; `lib/compound.ts`): the feed's compound name, stored as it comes (no migration), maps to a class key (`cmp-<key>`, fill `--<key>`), a name and a letter through the session's `meeting.year`. In 2018 each name was one fixed compound with its own colour: Hypersoft pink (HS), Ultrasoft purple (US), Supersoft red (SS), Soft yellow (S, `soft-2018`), Medium white (M, `medium-2018`), Hard ice blue (H, `hard-2018`), Superhard orange (SH). Pirelli publishes the colour names, not values (owner, 2026-11-14): Hypersoft and Ultrasoft follow FastF1's 2018 table (MIT; `#feb1c1`, and `#b24ba7` lightened 1 % to `#b34da8` so black letters reach 4.5:1); its Hard `#00a2f5` is ΔE 3.7 from `--wet` and its Superhard ΔE 9.2 from `--supersoft`, so those keep their own values. From 2019 Soft, Medium and Hard keep today's map. Intermediate (I) and Wet (W) are the same in both. The map is strict per season: a 2018-only name in a later session is unknown, as is any other name (`UNKNOWN`, a null). The test tyres `TEST` and `TEST_UNKNOWN` read "Test tyre" with T on the grey `--unknown` fill in every season; an unknown compound is grey with `?` in the tower (no longer `U`) and the stints view, the lap table draws no dot for it and the lap chart's tooltip leaves it out. A player without its session detail yet uses today's map (recordings start in 2025). The VM's 2018–2021 import (Step 0 on #126, 2026-11-14) sends HYPERSOFT, ULTRASOFT, SUPERSOFT, SOFT, MEDIUM, HARD, INTERMEDIATE, WET and null in 2018 (no SUPERHARD, kept for the full range; some weekends skip a step, such as China's Medium, Soft and Ultrasoft), no 2018 name from 2019, and TEST (2019), TEST_UNKNOWN and UNKNOWN (2020–2021).
  - **Checked by a unit test** (`contrast.test.ts`, with its own WCAG maths; OKLab and Machado 2009 come from `src/lib/colour.ts`, which the app shares, pinned to the README's figures, 13.8 and 4.6 for deuteranopia between `#18803a` and `#8a6400`; every token is classified, so none skips it): every text token reaches WCAG AA 4.5:1 on `--bg`, `--panel` and `--raised` in both themes, the compound letters 4.5:1 on their fills (and each season's fills with `--unknown` at least ΔE 10 apart for normal vision: the closest are 11.2 in 2018, `--hypersoft` against `--unknown`, and 15.9 from 2019, `--wet` against `--unknown`; under simulated deuteranopia and protanopia some pairs are close, 2.8 for 2018's `--hypersoft` against `--unknown` under protanopia, and the letters tell them apart, #126), `--purple` on `--purple-wash` and `--fg` on `--heat-1..3`, each composited over `--bg`, at 4.5:1 or more in both themes, and every mini-sector fill 3:1 on `--bg` (no longer 4.5: a fill is a shape, not text), and a chart band (`--rain`, P5a) 3:1 on `--bg` and `--panel`, the frame of its key's swatch (the band itself is drawn at 20 % under the lines, named by the key's words). Purple, green and plain text are at least ΔE 15 apart (OKLab × 100) for normal vision and 8 under simulated deuteranopia and protanopia, in both themes. The rain map's steps (P5c, D2 owner, 2026-11-09): `--rain-1..3` are fills and reach 3:1 on `--bg`; every pair among `--rain-1`, `--rain-2`, `--rain-3` and `--map-water` is at least ΔE 8 apart for normal vision and under deuteranopia and protanopia, in both themes (the closest, 8.9, is `--rain-1` against `--rain-3` under deuteranopia on light); `--map-water` and `--map-road` are decoration (context under the rain, no 3:1 bar).
  - **"Other" is plain text** (O1): only the bests carry colour, and `--yellow` is gone (the old light green and yellow were ΔE 4.6 apart for a deuteranope).
  - **Type:** the system font stack with tabular numbers, no font files; one app icon (owner, 2026-11-13, #121, D2: `icon-512.png`, drawn at build time by `apps/web/icon.ts`, no binary in git). One label style (12 px, bold, upper case, +0.06 em, `--muted`) for table heads, the status labels and `.label`. Body and tables 14 px, the tower 15 px, section heads 17 px, titles 26 px, nothing under 11 px (badges and compound letters). The compound dot is an 18 px circle for one letter and widens to a pill (`min-width: 18px`, 3 px side padding, `border-radius: 9px`) for two, at the same 11 px (#126). Titles and the clock use the display stack of installed fonts, `"DIN Alternate", Bahnschrift, system-ui`.
  - **Team colours** come from `drivers.team_colour` (on the tower, the DriverList's `TeamColour`), used only when it is six hex digits (else `--muted`). `teamStyle()` sets `--team` and `--team-l`, a copy darkened to at least 3:1 on the light `--bg` with its hue kept (`clampForLight` in `lib/session.ts`: a colour of luminance ≤ 0.3 as it is, else linear RGB × 0.2999 / Y, floored; a fast-check test). Every use goes through `.drv` and `--tc`, which is `--team-l` on the light theme; the bar is a 4 px border. Since P4f (owner, 2026-11-07) the lap and gap charts clamp too: on the light theme a line is drawn in `clampForLight(team)` (the theme as `data-theme` on `<html>`), and the line-type rule (C2, above) judges the colours as drawn in the current theme.
  - **Mini-sector shapes:** as in the Mini-sectors view above; the tower shows the same legend above its scroll box.
  - **Forced colours:** cues are outlines and borders, never a box-shadow (a test). Under `forced-colors: active` the mini-sectors, seek-bar markers, compound dots and stint bars keep their own colours (`forced-color-adjust: none`), and a pressed button is `HighlightText` on `Highlight`.
  - **Screen-reader words** (`.vh`): ", overall best" or ", personal best" on a ranked purple or green lap-table value and on the tower's Last and sector values; the compound dot is `aria-hidden`, with its words beside it ("soft tyres, ", on the tower plus "new set, " or "used set, " from the stint's `New`: "true" or "false", neither otherwise). P4d adds "gained n places" / "lost n places" on the tower's ± column and ", within a second" on a race Int under a second. #102 adds ", fastest of the phase" on qualifying's boxed time, ", overall best" or ", personal best" on the duel's lap times, and ", retired" or ", stopped" behind the tower's `RET` and `STOP`. The session light's word is always text beside its `aria-hidden` lamps. The `—` reasons stay in `ui/Note.tsx`.
  - Colour is never the only cue (the lap table's styles and legend above). A render exception shows "Something went wrong: reload the page." from an error boundary instead of a blank page: the page's, a player tab's, or the outer one (Error states above).
- **CSP** (A§12.1 invariant 6): the build has no inline script or style and no `data:`/`blob:` URL (`build.assetsInlineLimit: 0`, plain CSS imported from the entry). React `style` props carry only custom properties (`--team`, `--x`, `--w`): a client render sets them through CSSOM, which `style-src 'self'` does not block; only `style=` attributes in markup and `<style>` elements are blocked. **The ECharts chunk** (P1e, a grep of the built chunk; re-grepped by P4f, owner, 2026-11-07, after `lib/echarts.ts` gained `MarkAreaComponent` and `LabelLayout`: the chunk grew from 510,629 to 532,060 bytes raw and every count below is unchanged): no `setAttribute("style"`, `insertRule`, `createElement("style")`, `eval(` or `new Function`, and no `blob:`, `createObjectURL` or `data:` URL (its 9 `data:` hits are object keys), so the dist scan passes with no allowance. Its 3 `cssText` writes are CSSOM (zrender's canvas root and a measuring helper on our path, the HTML tooltip off it). Its 9 `innerHTML` hits are the SVG export paths (never called), the HTML tooltip (bundled but never instantiated: `renderMode: 'richText'` builds the rich-text tooltip instead; the only `innerHTML` that parses HTML is there) and zrender's canvas painter clearing its root with an empty string. `views/Chart.tsx` is the only importer of `lib/echarts.ts`, and only the chart views `views/Compare.tsx` and `views/Weather.tsx` import `Chart` (`build.test.ts`), so the chunk loads on Compare and Weather and nowhere else (owner, 2026-11-07, P5a). P5a added no ECharts component (the time axis comes with the grid): the chunk stayed at 532,060 bytes raw, and a re-grep on 2026-11-09 gave the same counts. **The worker** (#121): `sw.js` is a classic script with no `import` or `export`, proved by running the built file in a `node:vm` context (`build.test.ts`); that test also pins the whole CSP read from `deploy/web/Caddyfile`, so a change to any directive fails it. The dist scan covers `sw.js` and the manifest.
- **The app** (owner, 2026-11-13, #121; installable, with an offline copy of opened final sessions):
  - **Manifest:** `apps/web/public/manifest.webmanifest` (`display: standalone`, `/` as id, start and scope, `#15151e` for both colours, the one icon `any maskable`); `index.html` links it with `crossorigin="use-credentials"` (a manifest is fetched in CORS mode without credentials, and Access would redirect it) and the `apple-touch-icon`.
  - **Worker:** `src/sw/` (`handlers.ts` is pure, `main.ts` the entry), the second build entry, emitted as `/sw.js`. It is registered only under `import.meta.env.PROD`, on `load`, so the precache does not compete with the first paint. Its cache is named by a hash of the web build (D0). The build embeds `F1_REVISION` (#98, A§10), so each release is a new build and drops the offline data. That is right: the stored answers are keyed under the old `?v=`, which the new page never asks for. It precaches the whole build (D6: `/` and every `.js` and `.css` file; `/` plus 55 files, 56 entries, 1,525,685 bytes raw): a failed install is retried on a later load. So every tab's **code** is precached, but only data the owner fetched is stored: Stints and the rain map are stored only once their tab was opened online. `activate` deletes every other `f1-` cache (a cache under another name is not the worker's, and `lib/saved.ts` reads only `f1-` caches), and `skipWaiting`/`clients.claim` let a new worker take over an open page, which is safe because every path is network-first and assets are hashed. What it stores for the API, and why every path is network-first: A§10.
  - **Saved on this device** (D4; `lib/saved.ts`): when `/seasons` fails, `/` shows the error and under it the sessions whose detail the worker holds, newest first, each linking to its laps. A body that fails `SessionDetail` is skipped. It is empty without the Cache API.
  - **Offline wording** (D5): `errorText` says "You are offline: this page needs the network." for a `TypeError` while `navigator.onLine === false` (Node has no `onLine`: compare with `false`); online the Access text is unchanged. The player says the same after 5 failures while offline.
- **Mobile:** must work on a phone (`width=device-width` viewport). Tables scroll inside their container (`.scroll`, never the page; it also bounds the height, so the sticky header row holds), links, buttons, selects and table rows are ≥ 44 px, and charts are full width. At 390 px (owner, 2026-11-07, P4f) the lap chart still draws every deleted lap, own best and triangle and keeps the two end labels apart, checked by a server-side render in the node environment (A§15); Compare's grid is one column under 640 px.
  - **Focus and regions** (owner, 2026-11-07, P4a): a 3 px `:focus-visible` outline in `--fg`. A scroll box's own ring sits outside it (offset 2 px, inside the page gutter), because its sticky header row and first column would paint over an inset one; a ring on an element inside a box is inset, so the box's overflow never clips it. Every scroll box is `ui/Scroll.tsx`, a focusable, named region (`tabIndex=0`, `role="region"`, the table's name: "Lap times" and the like, "Mini-sectors and speed traps (km/h)", "Timing tower", "Race control messages" (P4d, the panel's list), "Sector deltas", "Last fetch failed"), so a keyboard can reach and scroll it (WCAG 2.1.1). Its `scroll-padding` keeps a focused cell clear of the sticky header row and columns (44 px top and 80 px left; the lap table 190 px left for its frozen Driver and Best columns, 158 px under 600 px (P4b); the tower 126 px for Pos 48 and Driver, the deltas 88 px top for their two head rows and 100 px left, columns mode 50 px left, and 88 px top in the lap table for its Best row (P4b); each a few px over the measured frozen width, and the tower's Pos is a fixed 48 px so the Driver column's `left` agrees in the head and the body).
- **Times** (owner, 2026-10-28): shown in Europe/Amsterdam through `Intl.DateTimeFormat`, not Temporal, with no polyfill. All formatting lives in `apps/web/src/lib/time.ts`: instants as "Sun 11 Oct 2026, 14:00", the track's local time (P4c, `fmtTrack`: from the meeting's `gmt_offset`, with the track's weekday added when it differs from Amsterdam's, as in Las Vegas; one offset per meeting, so a DST change at the track during the weekend would show later sessions an hour off), lap times as `m:ss.SSS`, sectors as `ss.SSS` (`m:ss.SSS` from 60 s).

## 14. Deployment

### 14.1 Host

- The Waterheight Hyper-V VM (Debian 13 amd64) goes from 4 vCPU / 8 GB to **6 vCPU / 12 GB** (owner action). Disk: 1 TB, 960 GB free.
- Under a separate compose project `f1`. Paths: `/etc/f1/{f1.env, secrets/}`, `/srv/f1/{state, live, archive, jolpica}`, a named volume `f1_pgdata`. `/srv/f1/state` is root `0700` (`current.env` and friends). `/srv/f1/live/<session_key>-<startedAt>/` (the recordings) and `/srv/f1/state/live/` (the F1 TV token) are `62532:62532 0700`, created by `f1-install`, and so is `/srv/f1/archive` (P3a: the `archive` job's raw files, `<session_key>/<Topic>.jsonStream`, kept plain). `f1-install` also creates `/srv/f1/jolpica` (P3c), `root:62532 0750`: root unpacks the Jolpica dump there and the `jolpica` job reads it through a read-only bind.

### 14.2 Services (limits **[U]**, tuned after the backfill and the first live weekend)

The limits stay **[U]** until the P2f follow-up PR tunes them from the Las Vegas weekend (20–22 Nov 2026) with `f1-measure` (A§14.5).

| Service | Image | mem / cpus | Networks |
|---|---|---|---|
| `db` | postgres:18.6-trixie | 1g / 1.0 (`shared_buffers=256MB`, `max_connections=20`) | `f1-db` (internal) |
| `api` | f1-server `api` | 384m / 1.0 | `f1-edge` (internal), `f1-db`, `f1-api-egress` (api only) |
| `ingest` | f1-server `ingest` | 256m / 0.5 | `f1-egress`, `f1-db` |
| `live` | f1-server `live` | 384m / 1.0 | `f1-egress`, `f1-db` |
| `caddy` | f1-web | 128m / 0.5 | `f1-edge`, `f1-tunnel-edge`, `f1-publish` (caddy only) |
| `cloudflared` | cloudflare/cloudflared | 256m / 0.5 | `f1-tunnel`, `f1-tunnel-edge` |
| `migrate` | f1-server `migrate` (profile `jobs`) | 128m | `f1-db` |
| `archive` (P3a) | f1-server `archive` (profile `jobs`) | 512m / 1.0 | `f1-egress`, `f1-db` |
| `fill` (P3b) | f1-server `fill` (profile `jobs`) | 768m / 1.0 | `f1-db` |
| `jolpica` (P3c) | f1-server `jolpica` (profile `jobs`) | 768m / 1.0 | `f1-db` |

- Total limits are ~2.7 GB. Expected steady state is ~0.9 GB.
- `live` (P2c): `pids_limit` 64, `stop_grace_period` 60 s (SIGTERM closes the socket, gzips and closes the row), the heartbeat healthcheck of the other roles, `group_add` 62003, 62006, 62007, 62008. Two read-write binds with `create_host_path: false`: `/srv/f1/live` → `/srv/f1/live`, and the host's `/srv/f1/state/live` → `/srv/f1/state` (A§12.3). The whole of `/srv/f1/state` is never mounted, because it holds `current.env`. A missing host directory is an error, not a root-owned directory made by Docker. The limits are tuned in P2f.
- `archive` (P3a): a `jobs`-profile service like `migrate`, which `f1-update` never runs (the owner runs `compose --profile jobs run --rm archive archive <year>`, runbook `update.md`). `restart: "no"`, `depends_on: db: service_healthy`, healthcheck disabled, `pids_limit` 64, secret `db_f1_ingest` (`group_add` 62003) and no F1 TV password secret: it reads the token that `live` caches. Two binds with `create_host_path: false`: `/srv/f1/archive` → `/srv/f1/archive` read-write and the host's `/srv/f1/state/live` → `/srv/f1/state` **read-only** (never the whole of `/srv/f1/state`). `hardening.sh` pins both in `only_bind_allowlist`, with two negative cases (`/srv/f1/state` as the source; the token directory read-write), exempts it from the healthcheck check and lists it in the network and secret checks. The limits are **[U]** like the others; its peak RSS is 157–271 MB for one session (WSL [box], 2026-11-10; 271 MB on the 1,363-lap race 6675, A§6.2), well under the 512m limit. e2e runs it once without a token file (exit 1, `token_file`, no request).
- `fill` (P3b): a `jobs`-profile service like `migrate`, which `f1-update` never runs (the owner runs `compose --profile jobs run --rm fill fill --all`). `restart: "no"`, `depends_on: db: service_healthy`, healthcheck disabled, secret `db_f1_ingest` (`group_add` 62003) and no F1 TV token. Network `f1-db` only, so it has no egress and no nftables change (the same as `migrate`). Two read-only binds with `create_host_path: false`: `/srv/f1/archive` → `/srv/f1/archive` and `/srv/f1/live` → `/srv/f1/live` (never `/srv/f1/state`). 768m: the measured peaks are 278 MB for `fill --all` without files, 401 MB for the four-session archive `fill` (A§7 [box]) and 313 MB for the Mexico race recording alone (the round-2 review). `hardening.sh` lists it in the network and secret checks, exempts it from the healthcheck check, pins both binds in `only_bind_allowlist` and has a negative case for a read-write `/srv/f1/live`; e2e runs `fill --all` once (exit 0).
- `jolpica` (P3c): a `jobs`-profile service like `fill`, which `f1-update` never runs (the owner runs `compose --profile jobs run --rm jolpica jolpica [<year>…]`, runbook `update.md`). `restart: "no"`, `depends_on: db: service_healthy`, healthcheck disabled, `pids_limit` 64, secret `db_f1_ingest` (`group_add` 62003) and no token. Network `f1-db` only: no egress and no nftables change. One read-only bind with `create_host_path: false`, `/srv/f1/jolpica` → `/srv/f1/jolpica` (`root:62532 0750`, made by `f1-install`; never `/srv/f1/state`). 768m / cpus 1: the peak RSS is 439 MB for all years of the real dump in the S3 smoke and 494–506 MB in the [box] (1,321 sessions in 33–38 s, A§7), because the job holds the small tables and the wanted sessions' entries, laps and pit stops in memory. `hardening.sh` lists it in the network and secret checks, exempts it from the healthcheck check and pins the bind in `only_bind_allowlist`, with a negative case for a read-write `/srv/f1/jolpica`; e2e runs it once on the empty directory (exit 1, `bad_dump`). The limits are **[U]** like the others.
- `api` (P2d): one read-only bind with `create_host_path: false`, `/srv/f1/live` → `/srv/f1/live`. It runs as 62532, the owner of the `0600`/`0700` recordings, so it needs no new GID. `hardening.sh` pins it in `only_bind_allowlist` (`read_only: true`), with two negative cases: the same bind read-write, and an extra bind.
- **Caddy and the app files** (#121, `deploy/web/Caddyfile`): `/manifest.webmanifest` gets `Content-Type: application/manifest+json` from a site-level `@manifest` matcher (`file_server` keeps a type that is already set), not from the image's mailcap. `/sw.js` and the manifest go through the catch-all with `Cache-Control: no-cache`, so a new worker is found on the next load. `.png` and `.js` types come from Go's table, which defers to the image's `/etc/mime.types`, so e2e step 3a accepts `text/javascript` or `application/javascript` for `/sw.js` (a worker takes either) and checks the other two exactly. `.dockerignore` now sends `apps/web/public` and `apps/web/icon.ts` to the image build: before, the image lacked `third-party-licences.txt` (a P5c bug) and would have lacked the manifest.
- **Caddy and SSE** (P2d, `deploy/web/Caddyfile`): the site-wide `encode zstd gzip` skips `^/api/v1/playback/[0-9]+/stream$` (`@compress not path_regexp …`, `encode @compress zstd gzip`), so the stream does not rest on Caddy internals for being unbuffered. Caddy 2.11's `reverse_proxy` also flushes a `Content-Type: text/event-stream` response immediately ([`flush_interval`](https://caddyserver.com/docs/caddyfile/directives/reverse_proxy): "this option is ignored and responses are flushed immediately to the client"), and `encode` takes a request matcher ([docs](https://caddyserver.com/docs/caddyfile/directives/encode)). Checked locally with caddy 2.11.7 `validate` and `hardening.sh` (30 checks, 6 of them negative); the CI e2e runs the Caddyfile. The code reviewer also ran caddy 2.11.7 by hand in front of a stub SSE upstream: events arrived on cadence, the stream had no `Content-Encoding`, and the other `/api/v1` routes were still gzipped. There is no automated CI check of that (a follow-up). `Cache-Control: no-transform` on the stream also keeps Cloudflare from compressing it.
- Bridges are named `f1-*`, IPv4 only, with fixed subnets 10.62.1–7.0/24 (owner, 2026-10-28) in this order: `f1-db`, `f1-edge`, `f1-egress`, `f1-api-egress`, `f1-tunnel`, `f1-tunnel-edge`, `f1-publish`. `f1-install` refuses to install when one overlaps a host route or a non-`f1` Docker network (Waterheight's 172.30.0.0/16, the LAN). `enable_icc: false` on the four non-internal networks. Every container has at most one non-internal network, so its default route is unambiguous.
- `f1-publish` carries `caddy` only, for the `127.0.0.1:8180` publish (the f1-update smoke test): Docker does not publish a port for a container that is only on internal networks. The firewall drops all its egress. Proven in CI (`ci / deploy`, e2e): the only port binding is `/f1-caddy-1 80/tcp → 127.0.0.1:8180`.
- `db` runs as 62532 on the volume `f1_pgdata`: the postgres image's `/var/lib/postgresql` is mode 1777, so a new volume lets an arbitrary UID run initdb. Proven in CI: initdb as 62532 on a fresh `f1_pgdata`, `roles.sql` through initdb, `migrate` as `f1_migrator`. The healthcheck is `pg_isready -U postgres` (62532 has no passwd entry). The local socket is `trust` inside the container only; TCP is `scram-sha-256`. `roles.sql` runs once, through `docker-entrypoint-initdb.d`, on a new volume. `migrate`, `api`, `ingest` and `live` start with `depends_on: db: service_healthy`.
- `cloudflared` (2026.10.3, by digest) runs `--protocol http2` (A§14.3), reads its token with `--token-file` (never `TUNNEL_TOKEN`), binds its metrics to `127.0.0.1:20241` and has the image's `tunnel ready` healthcheck. `caddy` sets the sysctl `net.ipv4.ip_unprivileged_port_start=80` and has tmpfs `/data` and `/config`. `migrate` is a `jobs`-profile job that `f1-update` runs before `up`.
- UIDs/GIDs: containers run as 62532 (not Waterheight's 65532/65533). Secret GIDs start at 62001 (Waterheight uses 61001–61012). A consumer gets its secrets' GIDs through `group_add`; the files are `root:<gid> 0440`:

  | Secret | GID | Consumers |
  |---|---|---|
  | `db_postgres` | 62001 | `db` |
  | `db_f1_migrator` | 62002 | `db`, `migrate` |
  | `db_f1_ingest` | 62003 | `db`, `ingest`, `live`, `archive`, `fill`, `jolpica` |
  | `db_f1_api` | 62004 | `db`, `api` |
  | `cf_tunnel_token` | 62005 | `cloudflared` |
  | `hc_ping_key` | 62006 | `ingest`, `live` |
  | `f1tv_username` | 62007 | `live` |
  | `f1tv_password` | 62008 | `live` |

  `db` holds the three login GIDs because `roles.sql` sets the passwords with `pg_read_file`.
- The `migrate` contract (built in P0, owner 2026-10-25): host `db:5432`, database `f1`, login `f1_migrator`, and the password from the file secret `db_f1_migrator` (`/etc/f1/secrets/db_f1_migrator` on the host, `/run/secrets/db_f1_migrator` in the container). `DATABASE_URL` is a dev/test switch that Compose never sets. The server image has dbmate on its `PATH`.

### 14.3 Firewall

- A separate `table inet f1`, loaded by `f1-firewall.service` (oneshot, `nft -c` then `nft -f`, never `flush ruleset`).
- Forward hook on `f1-*` interfaces only:
  - DNS to the host resolvers
  - tcp/443 from `f1-egress`, to any public IPv4: the limit is the port, not the host. `ingest`, `live` and `archive` share this network, and their hostnames (OpenF1, F1, hc-ping.com and, since P5c, `api.open-meteo.com` and `historical-forecast-api.open-meteo.com` for the rain job; owner, 2026-11-07, no firewall change) are fixed only in the code (A§12.3)
  - tcp/7844 from `f1-tunnel` to Cloudflare's tunnel ranges (P2f: `cloudflared` runs `--protocol http2`; on the VM QUIC (udp/7844) fails with `tls: no application protocol` and cloudflared fell back to http2 only after ~130 s, longer than the 120 s smoke test, seen 2026-11-04)
  - tcp/443 from the `api` (`f1-api-egress`, shared with no other service) to Cloudflare's published IP ranges only, for the Access certs (owner, 2026-10-26, P1c). Port 8080 is never published, and Caddy↔api stays on the internal `f1-edge`
  - drop everything else (all of `f1-publish`'s egress included), plus host-input drops for `f1-*` (only established/related replies pass: docker-proxy's path for 8180)
- DNS is allowed only from the three egress networks (`f1-egress`, `f1-api-egress`, `f1-tunnel`), to the set `resolvers4`, and before the drop of private ranges (the resolvers may be on the LAN). The private ranges are dropped even on 443. Traffic from an `f1-*` bridge to another `f1-*` bridge is `return`ed to Docker's own internal and icc rules.
- Sets (fetched 2026-10-29): `cf4` from `https://www.cloudflare.com/ips-v4`; `cf_tunnel4` = 198.41.192.0/24 and 198.41.200.0/24 from Cloudflare's "Tunnel with firewall" documentation; `resolvers4` is filled by `f1-resolvers` from the host's resolv.conf (an `ExecStartPost` of `f1-firewall.service` and every `f1-tick`). The owner refreshes `cf4` and `cf_tunnel4` by hand (`docs/runbooks/cloudflare.md`).
- Without this table a second project's egress is unrestricted, because Waterheight's table only matches `rws-*` and `docker0`.

### 14.4 Updates

- CI on `main` (the `release` job, in the GitHub environment `production`: it waits for the owner's approval before it touches GHCR, owner 2026-10-29) builds both images, pushes `ghcr.io/mwijkhuisen/f1-server` and `f1-web` as `:main` and `:sha-<git>` (public, no secrets inside), and records the digests. The job's `concurrency` group queues runs without cancelling, so `:main` stays on the newest commit.
- `f1-update.timer` (every 10 min) runs `deploy/bin/f1-update`:
  1. take a flock; refuse (exit 1) when `table inet f1` is not loaded
  2. `docker pull` `:main`; wait (exit 0) while the two images' `org.opencontainers.image.revision` labels differ (a release half pushed); stop if the digest equals `current.env`; stop with a loud log line if the pair equals `failed.env` (a known-bad pair is never retried automatically; `rm /srv/f1/state/failed.env` to retry)
  - **Hold (P2f):** defer while `live` records. `f1-update` asks `recorder_status` (through `docker exec f1-db-1 psql` as `f1_api`, 20 s at most and a 15 s `statement_timeout`) for a row whose `state` is not `idle` and whose `updated_at` is under 5 minutes old. If there is one, it removes `candidate.env`, logs `live is recording (session N): deploy deferred to a later run` and exits 0, and the next run retries. A deploy recreates `live` and ends its recording with `complete = false` (A§11.2). It fails open: no db container, an unreachable db or a stale row (`live` is down) does not hold, and the query logs `warning: cannot read recorder_status: deploying without the hold` when it fails. The row must also be no more than 1 minute in the future, and root caps the hold: `/srv/f1/state/held-since` holds the epoch of the first deferral of an unbroken chain (its mtime is the last deferral; a gap over 30 minutes starts a new chain), and after 14 h `f1-update` logs `error: deploys held for over 14 h (session N, since <UTC time>): deploying anyway; check recorder_status` and deploys (A§12.5). It removes `held-since` when a deploy starts. A drill (`--server`/`--web`) is held too. To deploy a fix during a recording at once: `sudo docker stop f1-live-1`, then `f1-update`. `f1-install` has no hold.
  3. run `migrate`
  4. `up -d`
  5. smoke-test `http://127.0.0.1:8180/healthz`, then require every f1 container healthy (an unhealthy `ingest`, `live` or `cloudflared` fails the deploy)
  6. on failure, write the pair to `failed.env` and roll back to the previous digests in `/srv/f1/state/current.env`
- State lives in `/srv/f1/state`: `current.env` (the last pair that passed), `candidate.env` (during a run), `failed.env`, and `alert-known_bad` and `alert-hold_14h` (the pair and the hold that last alerted).
- **Alerts** (#104, A§14.5): a run that exits 1 (no firewall table, a failed deploy, a failed rollback, a failed first deploy, an error in a drill) fails `f1-update.service`, whose `OnFailure=f1-alert@%n.service` sends `unit_failed f1-update.service`. Two exit-0 outcomes alert too: skipping a known-bad pair sends `known_bad`, once per pair, and the 14 h cap sends `hold_14h <session>`, once per hold (the session key only when the hold names a session). A drill (`--server`/`--web`) sends neither (owner, 2026-11-14, #104, D1), because the owner is watching; its exit 1 fails a unit only when it runs as `f1-update.service`.
- `f1-update --server <ref> --web <ref>` deploys the given images instead (drills, CI); the flag not given keeps the image of `current.env`, and the revision check is skipped. A ref with a registry host is always pulled and recorded by digest, so `current.env` never names a movable tag; only a host-less local ref (`f1-server:ci`) is recorded as given.
- GitHub has no server credentials.
- `f1-update` deploys nothing (`not configured`, exit 0) while `f1.env` or any file in `F1_SECRETS` is empty, so after P2c adds `f1tv_username` and `f1tv_password` deploys pause until the owner fills them. The rollout order is in `docs/runbooks/update.md`: the image first, then `f1-install`, because an image from before P2c exits 2 on `live`.
- Compose, Caddyfile, nftables and units change rarely. The owner applies them with `deploy/bin/f1-install` from a checkout on the VM. It is idempotent, re-applies a changed compose file to a running deploy, and does not enable the timers: the owner does that once the secrets and `f1.env` are filled (`docs/runbooks/install.md`).

### 14.5 Operations

- **`f1-tick.timer`** (every 10 min, at :05): reloads `table inet f1` if it is missing, refreshes `resolvers4`, restarts unhealthy `f1` containers (Docker doesn't), watches the recorder and records the free disk space in `/srv/f1/state/disk`. Fail closed: when the table cannot be loaded, it stops every `f1` container and writes `/srv/f1/state/firewall-stopped`; once the table loads again, `f1-tick` or `f1-update` starts `current.env` again. Restarts and the restart of a stopped stack run under `f1-update`'s lock and are skipped for that round while a deploy or `f1-install` holds it.
  - **Alerts** (#104, owner, 2026-11-09; each goes through `f1-alert` to `f1-ingest/fail` and repeats only when its condition has ended and come back; `/srv/f1/state/alert-<code>` holds the token of the last alert):
    - **`restart_loop <name>`:** `/srv/f1/state/restarts` keeps `<name> <rounds>` for the containers restarted in the last round that ran the step. The same container restarted on 3 rounds in a row alerts with the first name. It alerts again when the set of looping names changes, and re-arms when none loops.
    - **The recorder watch** (`/etc/f1/recorder-watch.sql`, not under the lock, read-only as `f1_api`, 20 s timeout and a 15 s `statement_timeout`): `recorder_missing <key>` from `date_start` − 55 min until `date_end` when no recording of the session started since `date_start` − 65 min and no other recording is open or ended under 3 minutes ago (a truly overlapping session starts late by design, A§11.2; a crashed recorder is `recorder_stale`'s job; owner, 2026-11-14, #104, D2). `recorder_stale <key>` when no `recorder_status` row is fresh (under 5 minutes old, not over 1 minute ahead, the bounds of `f1-update`'s hold) and a session starts within 2 h or runs. `login_failed <key>` when the status warning is `login_failed` and a session starts within 24 h or runs. A session counts until `date_end` + 30 min unless cancelled. Each code alerts once per session, and re-arms when the query no longer returns it. It fails open: when the query fails, `f1-tick` logs `warning: cannot run the recorder watch: no recorder alert this round` and goes on.
    - **Disk:** `/srv/f1/state/disk` holds `<UTC time> <free bytes of /srv/f1> <free bytes of Docker's data root>`, and `f1-tick` logs `free disk: /srv/f1 N GiB, Docker data root <dir> N GiB`. Each is read alone: under 20 GiB free on a readable one alerts `disk_low`, which re-arms when both are readable and above. The file is written only when both are readable; otherwise `error: cannot read the free disk space of /srv/f1 and the Docker data root`, exit 1.
  - A run that exits 1 (no firewall, the stack not started again, the free disk unreadable) fails `f1-tick.service`, whose `OnFailure=` sends `unit_failed f1-tick.service`.
- **`f1-measure`** (P2f, [box], root only; `f1-install` installs it into `/usr/local/bin`): `f1-measure <duration> [<interval s>]` (the interval is the pause between samples; a sample takes 2-3 s) samples CPU, memory and disk of the f1 containers only (the compose project `f1`, never Waterheight's) into `/srv/f1/state/measure-<UTC>.tsv` (`0600`) and prints a Markdown summary (per service: samples, CPU mean and max %, memory max against its limit, OOM kills, exits, restarts, counted from `docker events` (`State.OOMKilled` is reset by a restart); disk growth of the recordings, the db volume and each container's logs). `--summary <file>` rebuilds it from a TSV. It tunes the limits of A§14.2; the runbook is `docs/runbooks/weekend.md`.
- **healthchecks.io:** uses the 2 free checks left on the account: `f1-ingest` (pinged once the sync requested by the daily `30 0 * * *` Cron, or its 15-minute retry, has succeeded; not after the startup sync, so a crash loop does not stay green; period 1 day, grace 1 h) and `f1-live` (P2c). `f1-ingest` also gets `f1-ingest/fail` (owner, 2026-11-09, #105; the daemon only, a POST with a fixed-code body, A§8). Since #104 (owner, 2026-11-09) the host's `f1-alert` (A§12.5) also POSTs to `f1-ingest/fail`, with the codes `unit_failed <unit>`, `known_bad`, `hold_14h [<session>]`, `restart_loop <container>`, `recorder_missing <key>`, `recorder_stale <key>`, `login_failed <key>`, `disk_low` and `test_alert` (the meaning of each: `docs/runbooks/update.md`, "Alerts from the host"). A red `f1-ingest` means one of two things: a missed daily ping means the daemon is down or its schedule sync fails; a `/fail` means a session or a rain grid failed for good, the due backlog stalled, or the host raised a code. Its body names the code (`session_failed <code>`, `rain_failed <code>`, `due_backlog`, or a host code) and `/status` names the sessions. The next daily success ping clears the check, so a host alert shows until 00:30 UTC at the latest, and its body is in the check's ping log. `f1-live`: `live` pings it once when a recording ends complete (A§11.2), and pings `f1-live/fail`, which alerts at once, when one ends incomplete at a normal stop or ends on a disk failure or the floor. A recording that a restart cut short therefore ends in a fail. Neither ping comes for a cancelled or moved session or for a SIGTERM (a deploy). Beyond the codes above, silence is the alarm (owner, 2026-11-09, #104), so the check's period suits the gaps between race weekends (owner's choice). **Known gap:** with Docker itself down, `f1-update.service` and `f1-tick.service` fail their `Requires=docker.service` as a dependency, not as `failed`, so `OnFailure=` does not fire. The missed daily ping still turns `f1-ingest` red within 25 h.
- **Logs:** pino JSON to Docker's `local` driver (host daemon defaults). Fixed codes only, never URLs with tokens.

## 15. Testing

- **Unit:** parsers, timing rules, the rate limiter (fake timers), and live delta merging (P2b, `apps/server/test/live.test.ts`, `live-laps.test.ts`; fixtures are synthetic, `apps/server/test/fixtures/live/*.jsonl`):
  - the A§15 merge property, a fast-check test: "A§15: merging a snapshot then the remaining deltas equals merging every delta from empty";
  - reconnects: "reconnect duplicates and a mid-session full state give the same rows" (`practice-reconnect.jsonl`) and the property "a reconnect at any point k, with any m recent messages repeated, gives the clean rows";
  - the stale session: "a feed joined before the session ignores the previous session and the next one" (`practice-stale.jsonl`);
  - the day prefix: "reads the day count of a .jsonStream time past 24 h, .NET TimeSpan style";
  - **Playback** (P2d, `apps/server/test/playback.test.ts`): a fast-check property that the opening `snapshot` at a random T, with its lap and stint rows, equals a full replay from 0 (`createFeed` + `createLaps`) to T, with several segments (plain and gz), snapshot files at random points (playback ignores them since P2e) and reconnect duplicates re-sent later, and that the client-applied deltas, and the rows after every `snapshot` and `laps` event, keep equal to it up to `end`; since P5b (owner, 2026-11-07) the same for the weather rows, with synthetic `WeatherData` lines of numeric and garbage strings among the duplicates and Subscribe states carrying `WeatherData` that change the topic or not (the snapshot's `weather` at T, and the client's rows from the deltas after every `delta`); a session switch with a synthetic fixture (a fresh `snapshot` without the previous session's lines, laps or weather rows, also for a seek after the switch); the `laps` event (changed rows only, at most once a second, the last ones before `end`); the delay bound on a fake clock (no event newer than now − delay); back-pressure, the heartbeat, `end` and pacing. The disconnect leak test is an integration test (`apps/server/test/playback.int.test.ts`): after a client aborts, no connection or file descriptor stays (counted in `/proc/self/fd`), and the cap counter is freed. The same file parses every event, the lap rows included, and `/recordings` (contract, newest first, `no-store`) against the contracts.
- **Integration:** real PostgreSQL 18 as a CI service container. Each file creates its own DB, applies `roles.sql` (`apps/server/test/db.ts`) and runs dbmate up → tests; the ingest tests log in as `f1_ingest`, never as the superuser. `db-check` is P0's up → down → up test (`apps/server/test/migrate.int.test.ts`) plus `kysely-codegen --verify` against a freshly migrated database (`apps/server/test/db.int.test.ts`: the committed `types.ts` matches the migrations). A unit test pins the sha256 of every migration (`test/migrations.test.ts`).
- **Fixtures:** `apps/server/src/openf1/fixtures/` holds trimmed real OpenF1 responses (public, no auth). Live/archive parser fixtures are synthetic in the repo. Real recordings stay in `fixtures-private/` (gitignored) and are used locally only. `scripts/record-fixture.ts` refuses to run when `CI` is set.
- **Offline** (`test/offline.ts`, installed by `test/msw.setup.ts` for every file of both projects): a DNS lookup of any name but `localhost` and a TCP connect outside 127.0.0.0/8 and ::1 are refused before anything is sent, and the test that tried fails (checked after each test and each file; a refused request alone can pass unnoticed, as the healthchecks ping swallows its error). So the integration database is on loopback (CI: `localhost:5432`). It also closes a connection that undici opens after an aborted request and sends nothing on, because msw 3 passes such an idle connection through to the real host one `setImmediate` later (mswjs/interceptors#881, open for msw 3.0.3). Before this, the Access middleware and healthchecks unit tests connected to `test-team.cloudflareaccess.com` and `hc-ping.com` on every run (the same code path runs in CI); in a cloud sandbox the TLS handshake met another name's certificate and undici's assert failed the run. `test/offline.test.ts` covers the refusals, loopback, the failing check and an aborted fetch with and without the close, and fails once msw stops passing idle connections through.
- **Web:** component tests render statically with `react-dom/server` in Vitest's node environment, with data as props: no DOM environment (owner, 2026-10-28). The first interaction tests (P2e) opt into **happy-dom** (21.0.4, a dev dependency) per file through `// @vitest-environment happy-dom`; the global environment stays node. They cover the lap table (colours per metric, deleted laps, missing values and their reasons, IN/OUT, compound dots, both orientations, driver order), the season page (`season-page.test.tsx`: the states, no result, and only `/seasons/:year`, `/live` and `/recordings` requested, P4c), the header switcher and the session head (`header.test.tsx`, P4c), the stints and the status panel. `compare.test.tsx` covers the lap chart option (dense `[lap, ms]` pairs on the value axis, `showAllSymbol: true` on every series, the own-best dot and the deleted lap as a `--bg` ring, the triangles for exactly the laps above the window, the Fit and 107 % windows and whole-second "1:33" labels, the bands from `from − 0.5` to `to + 0.5` with a one-lap VSC, the legend and end labels, the rich-text tooltip with its flags and `{|}` stripping, the grid's `outerBoundsMode: 'same'` and `outerBoundsContain: 'axisLabel'` in place of the deprecated `containLabel`), `lineStyles` (C2: equal colours, a close but unequal pair, a pair close only after the light clamp, a far pair, three close drivers), the delta table (null and deleted laps → `—`, the sign, the Best-lap row and its `—` without a best lap, the note for a reference without laps, the first selected as reference, one group per other driver, the delta-cell bars and none on `—`), the duel (the bar's side, colour and `--w`, `—`, one block per driver), the comparison (the Fit window and the pressed Reference) and the picker (chips in pick order, "reference" on the first chip on Compare only, the Remove labels, the `aria-pressed` grid inside `details`). Its "390 px" tests (P4f) draw the lap chart server-side in the node environment: `init(null, null, { renderer: 'svg', ssr: true, width: 390, height })` after `use([SVGRenderer])`, on a 57-lap race with 4 deleted laps, and count the symbols in the display list (every deleted lap hollow, both own bests, the triangles, two end labels that do not overlap, the bands kept when a driver is hidden); the same race on a category axis with `showAllSymbol: 'auto'` loses symbols, which shows the test can fail. `gap.test.ts` covers `gapFromCrossings` (a missing lap breaks the line, pit laps without a `lap_ms`, the fallback to `date_start + lap_ms`, a lapped driver) and the gap-chart option (a series per other driver, the styles, bands, y labels and tooltip). `session-url.test.tsx` (happy-dom, React `createRoot` + `act`) covers `?d=` in a real session view: read at mount, kept by the canonical redirect, written back on a pick, nothing written for the default or a bad value, and carried by the Compare and Mini-sectors links only; `minisectors.test.tsx` covers `segClass` for every mapped and several unknown codes, the grey cells, the one text per sector cell, padding, `—` speeds and the race banner. `apps/web/test/build.test.ts` runs a production `vite build` and scans `dist` for inline scripts, styles and handlers, `data:`/`blob:` URLs and third-party references, and checks from Rolldown's output that ECharts (`echarts`, `zrender`) sits only in the one lazy chunk of `lib/echarts.ts`, never in the entry's static import graph, and (P4f) from the source files that `lib/echarts.ts` is imported by `views/Chart.tsx` alone, which only the chart views `views/Compare.tsx` and (P5a) `views/Weather.tsx` import; `contrast.test.ts` checks the theme tokens for 4.5:1 in both themes, with the colour maths of `lib/colour.ts` (which the chart's line types use) pinned to the review's figures. No browser matrix in CI. P2e adds `player.test.tsx` (happy-dom, React `createRoot` + `act`, a fake `EventSource` on `globalThis`, fake timers, no fetch): the delay slider (0–120 in 1 s steps, ±1/±5/±30 clamped at the bounds), the stored delay (default 0 when storage throws), a delay change that closes the old stream and opens exactly one with the new delay, a seek, a nudge, pause and play each reopening at the right `at`, an error that closes the stream and reopens at the current T after the backoff (and at `live` when live), `end` that closes it for good, and a snapshot then deltas that update the tower while a `laps` event updates the lap table; its pure parts are `streamUrl` and the markers (the 2 s `Started` blip ignored, a start followed only by a TrackStatus change kept, TrackStatus Red, `Finished`, a RaceControl `RED` alone giving none, and none drawn before the recording started). `tower.test.tsx` covers `towerRows` (order, gap, interval, last/best, colours, sectors and mini-sectors, tyre, pits, retired), the waiting notice and feed text rendered as text. `views.test.tsx` gains the recorder warnings, the recordings list and the Next notice; `routes.test.ts` parses `/live` and `/replay/:recording`, and (P4f) `parseD` (order kept, unknown and repeated numbers dropped, null for a missing, malformed or over-long value or no known number) and `formatD`. P5a (owner, 2026-11-07) adds `weather.test.ts` (`summarise` with nulls and all null, `rainPeriods` split by a dry sample, a missing reading and a gap over 5 min, `compass` at 0°, 22.5°, 338° and 359°, `leaderLap` before the start, mid-race, after the flag and outside races, the head line and the period words), `weather-chart.test.ts` (the two line types, the bands, the tooltip with the lap in races only and `{|}` stripped, and a 390 px SVG render: Amsterdam `HH:mm` tick labels on round minutes, both end labels, the bands in `--rain`) and `weather-view.test.tsx` (the tiles, `—` with its reason, the rain periods with laps, the chart's name, the messages without rows and the session head's line); on the server the weather fixtures in `openf1-schemas.test.ts`, the ingest cases in `ingest.int.test.ts`, the grants in `db.int.test.ts`, the route in `api.int.test.ts` and the re-queue in `weather-migration.int.test.ts`. #97 adds to `player.test.tsx` a stubbed `navigator.wakeLock` (requested once while playing and visible, again on `visibilitychange`, one lock kept, released on pause and on unmount; a still frame and a hidden page request nothing; a refusal or no API changes nothing), to `season-page.test.tsx` the replay-first links on a card and in This weekend, the state word on every card, and a fake clock (`Date` and `setInterval`) across Qualifying's start, which moves "next" to "live now" and Up next to the Race without a remount, with the season refetched only while the weekend is in progress, and to `season.test.ts` the grace day (kept for 24 h, a meeting in progress beating it, the next meeting after it) and `started`. The same files also test the `/recordings` poll (a recording that ends while the page stays open turns "live now" into a replay link after 5 min), the poll's start and stop around the weekend (the season refetched from the first session on, no longer after the grace day), a wake lock granted after a pause (released at once), and the reload hint releasing the lock until the next snapshot.. P4f's `views.test.tsx` also covers `stintLayout` (a track to the driver's last lap, a stint without laps listed and not drawn, the ticks, used sets, the stops) and the stint rows (a `button` with `popovertarget`, its popover's lines, no `title`).
- **P5c: the rain map** (owner, 2026-11-07; none of them calls Open-Meteo, OpenStreetMap or GitHub):
  - **Unit:** `packages/contracts/test/geo.test.ts` (a projected distance against haversine within 1 %, the grid's corner and centre points, the round trip); `apps/server/test/circuits.test.ts` (every `circuit_short_name` of the `meetings-*` fixtures 2023–2026 is in `CIRCUITS`, its id is in the GeoJSON and its centre is the recomputed bounding-box centre); `rain-state.test.ts` (the live window, the frame windows, `apiFor`, the next time, the back-off 5 … 360 min and the 9th failure, the error codes); `map-extract.test.ts` and `test/record-rain-fixture.test.ts` (the scripts refuse under `CI`, and what they write); `test/rain-facts.test.ts` (the names in `scripts/rain-facts.sql` equal `Object.keys(CIRCUITS)`, so the list cannot drift).
  - **msw** (`apps/server/test/rain-client.test.ts`, fake timers, finite streams only): the fixed host, the query and the headers; the limiter at 6 a minute and 1 a second; 429 → `rate_limited` and the pause; 400 and 500 → `http_NNN`; a network error; a redirect refused (0 follows, `network`); a drip stream ended at 60 s (`timeout`); a body over 4 MiB (`schema`); a short answer (`schema`); no URL, query or body in any error; the three recorded fixtures parse; `rainQuery` and `toFrames`; the budget on a fake clock (4,000 an hour, 8,000 a day, the backfill stopping at 6,400, a refusal records nothing, the restart seed).
  - **Integration** (real PostgreSQL 18): `rain-migration.int.test.ts` (the CHECKs refuse a bad state, a bad code, size 8 and 48 elements, a frame without a grid; `f1_api` reads and cannot write; deleting a session cascades through the grid to its frames); `rain-job.int.test.ts` (msw for both hosts, an injected clock, the `f1_ingest` login: the live refresh every 15 min and the replaced frames, the wait until `date_end + 24 h` and the one Historical fetch to `final`, the back-off and the 9th failure, a 429, the backfill newest first and one per step, an unmapped circuit without a grid, the budget refusal logged once, the restart seed, and the rain step running during a live window while the OpenF1 tick waits with no OpenF1 request); `api.int.test.ts` (the `Rain` contract, the null grid, the cache rule, 400 and 404); `db.int.test.ts` (`types.ts` matches the migrations).
  - **Web** (`apps/web/test/`): `rain.test.ts` (the step boundaries 0.1, 2.5 and 7.6 mm/h, the summary, `firstFrameAt`); `rain-map.test.tsx` (the layers, rings, cells by class, legend, slider name and `aria-valuetext`, the summary, the three credits with `rel="noreferrer"` and when each shows, every state, no `style=`); `rain-map-player.test.tsx` (happy-dom: the slider moves the summary, no frame after T, the slider follows T and a seek back drops a pick); `player.test.tsx` (the 1 s tick alone still re-derives nothing); `map-data.test.ts` and `map-osm.test.ts` (one outline file per `CIRCUITS` id, each at most 40 KiB with an `M`, `L`, `Z` grammar, and the OSM files' notice and paths); `contrast.test.ts` (the rain tokens, A§13); `build.test.ts` (every map module in a lazy chunk of its own, and `third-party-licences.txt` in `dist`).

## 16. Risks

| Risk | Mitigation |
|---|---|
| F1 changes access again (as in Oct 2026) | OpenF1 is the v1 source. Raw responses and recordings are kept. Spikes run before each build phase |
| OpenF1 gaps, slowness, re-ingests | Refetch at +3 h/+24 h, `refetch` command, honest gaps in the UI |
| F1 TV login flow changes / bot protection | Manual token-paste fallback. Expiry warnings |
| Token expires mid-session | Pre-check before connecting. Logs in again on 401 and reconnects |
| GitHub/cloud IPs blocked by F1 | All F1 traffic from the home VM. CI uses fixtures only |
| F1 TV ToS (personal use) | Single-identity Access, no public data, no commits of F1 TV-derived data |
| Resource contention with Waterheight | Strict container limits, VM resize, measurement after backfill and the first live weekend |
| Only 6 race weekends left in 2026 | Record all of them early (P2-rec), then build P2 over the winter on those recordings |
| Lap reconstruction from the feed is hard | Port FastF1's logic deliberately and compare against OpenF1 laps for the same session as a test oracle |
